# Implementation report

How the demo was built and what was proven live: its current state, the acceptance checklist, the measured times, the risk register, and a short log entry for each phase. Statuses follow [ADR 0012](adr/0012-validation-model.md), so a successful deployment alone is never **Pass**. Every live result comes from one Azure subscription in West US 3 and one Fabric workspace, between 2026-10-19 and 2026-10-27. Times are single samples, and yours will differ; the linked docs have the details.

## Final report

**State as of 2026-10-27.** T1, T2, T3, and T4 were deployed and validated live. All four are in Database Hub's Estate through Azure Resource Graph; T1, T2, and T3 send performance telemetry, and T4 its Azure Monitor metrics. The Fabric items were deployed and checked live: the notebooks, the Real-Time Dashboard, the history model and report, and the Insights app. Destroy was verified in a disposable environment, and the demo environment was destroyed and redeployed from the T2 golden snapshot in Phases 20 and 22, with no manual step.

Phase 25d proved Phase 25b's eight newer scenarios and Phase 25c's on-demand activity control live on 2026-10-27. Not proven live yet: a fresh build with the parallel tracks and no snapshot, an end-to-end run with an identity that holds only Contributor, and Phase 26's first run end to end, with the creation of a missing Fabric workspace and lakehouse. Database Hub's pages and the Database Agent have no API, so checking them is the presenter's job (**Manual action required**).

| Target | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Foundation | **Pass** | The network, its NSGs, and the budget since 2026-10-19; `COST.BUDGET` and `COST.AUTOSHUTDOWN` **Pass**, and the nightly auto-shutdown deallocated both VMs on schedule |
| T1 `sqlvm01` | **Pass** | SQL Server 2022 Standard, pay-as-you-go, **Ready** on 2026-10-20. Telemetry arrived about 2 minutes after its SQL VM monitoring flag was set. Unified inventory lists only the database engine, after the image's Analysis Services and Integration Services were removed (Phase 7) |
| T2 `arcsql01` | **Pass** | Arc **Connected** on 2026-10-20, with IMDS and the wire server unreachable from the guest. SQL Server 2022 Standard, pay-as-you-go, through the setup wizard on 2026-10-21, with telemetry about 7 minutes after monitoring was turned on. Restored from the golden snapshot as Standard pay-as-you-go with monitoring in Phases 20 and 22 |
| T3 `azuresql01-<id>/ContosoCare` | **Pass** | General Purpose serverless and Microsoft Entra-only, with a private endpoint, because a tenant policy turns off public network access on SQL servers. The first telemetry came 2.8 minutes after creation (2026-10-20). The free offer wasn't available, because the subscription's free databases were in another region, so T3 runs as regular serverless with a preflight **Warning** |
| T4 `pgflex01-<id>` | **Pass** | Created on 2026-10-26 on General Purpose `Standard_D2ds_v5`, after the region refused `Standard_B1ms` and `Standard_B2s` (R441). `HUB.ESTATE.T4` through Resource Graph and `METRICS.T4` through Azure Monitor **Pass** ([Phase 24](#phase-24-t4-an-azure-database-for-postgresql-flexible-server)) |
| Fabric | **Pass** | Both notebooks imported and run; the Real-Time Dashboard's 14 tiles all returned data; the history model's 103 measures all returned data after Phase 20's scenarios; the report's four pages rendered; and every query of the Insights app returned rows |
| Database Hub pages and the Database Agent | **Manual action required** | Presenter-driven, with no API: a rehearsal checks them |

### Acceptance checklist

The original brief's acceptance criteria, verbatim, plus the 30-minute target set during the build. Where part of a criterion is proven and part is still owed, it has two rows.

| # | Criterion | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A new user can fork/clone and understand prerequisites without reading code. | **Pass** | The [README](../README.md); [prerequisites](prerequisites.md), with who provides each item and how it's checked; [permissions](permissions.md), the [FAQ](faq.md), and [troubleshooting](troubleshooting.md). `init.ps1` writes the configuration from your sign-in (Phase 26b). Static validation resolves every relative link |
| 2 | Preflight catches missing Azure/Fabric/permission/provider/region/quota/nested-size/preview prerequisites. | **Pass** | `preflight.ps1`: the subscription guard, P1–P6, providers, region, quota and nested-capable sizes, security types, zones, the policy inventory and what-if, the free offer, T4's size, and both cost ceilings, with unit tests for the missing cases. It ran live in Phases 14, 16, 20, 22, and 24. Only a Fabric administrator can read the Hub tenant setting (P4), so that check is **Manual action required** by design |
| 3 | One primary action deploys all supported resources, and re-runs are safe with no duplicate Arc resources. | **Pass** | `deploy.ps1`: Phase 20's redeploy (38.8 minutes) and Phase 22's (63.7 minutes, no check **Fail**), each followed by a second run that changed nothing (21.8 and 19.2 minutes), with `T2.ARC.NODUPLICATES` **Pass** and exactly one Arc machine. With T4, a second run took 20.1 minutes (Phase 24) ([Phase 20](#phase-20-fast-redeploy-parallel-tracks-and-a-t2-golden-snapshot), [Phase 22](#phase-22-faster-redeploy-the-follow-up-overlaps-and-one-timed-destroy-and-redeploy)) |
| 4a | T1, T2, and T3 are healthy; they appear in Hub after the documented interval, and supported ones show performance telemetry. | **Pass** | `HUB.ESTATE.T1`–`T3` through Resource Graph, which is how Hub finds them, and `TELEMETRY.T1`–`T3`, with the first samples 2 to 7 minutes after monitoring started, within the documented interval of up to 15 minutes. T4 is in Estate through Resource Graph, with its metrics in Azure Monitor (Phase 24) |
| 4b | The same criterion, seen in the Database Hub UI | **Manual action required** | Hub has no API, so checking Estate and Performance is presenter-driven (`HUB.ESTATE.VISUAL`) |
| 5a | Every scenario starts and stops; locking is visible and safely recoverable. | **Pass** (the first eight scenarios, and T4's) | Phase 20 (2026-10-22): all eight scenarios on their default targets, then 11 ten-minute incidents across T1, T2, and T3, each within its bounds. Blocking chains showed in T1's DMVs (a sleeping head blocker with an open transaction, and sessions waiting on `LCK_M_U`) and in the runner's status for T2 and T3. `RESET.T1`, `.T2`, and `.T3` **Pass**, with no demo session or open demo transaction left. T4's scenarios ran live in Phase 24 |
| 5b | The same criterion, for the eight scenarios Phase 25b added | **Pass** | Phase 25d (2026-10-27): each ran for 8 minutes on T1, T2, and T3 at once, every start and reset **Pass**, and the telemetry showed each one's designed waits. A profile set twice in a row waited for its first run to end instead of being refused. `RESET.T1`–`T4` **Pass** at the end, with no demo session or open demo transaction left ([Phase 25d](#phase-25d-phases-25b-and-25c-live)) |
| 6 | Agent skills work on supported signals, or the repo states why not. | **Manual action required** | The repo states each documented limit: `microsoft-sql-fdh` is read-only and calls routes that aren't a public contract, the `microsoft-sql` diagnostics skills target Azure SQL Database and can decline on T1 and T2, and the Database Agent's coverage isn't documented ([agent guide](../fabric/agent/README.md#limitations), [ADR 0013](adr/0013-agent-experience.md)). The [live coverage record](../fabric/agent/README.md#live-coverage-record) waits for a rehearsal |
| 7 | Reset works without redeploy. | **Pass** | `RESET.T1`, `.T2`, and `.T3` **Pass** in Phase 20, more than once, without a redeploy |
| 8a | Compute stops after the demo. | **Pass** (the automatic stop) | The nightly auto-shutdown deallocated both VMs on schedule; the activity profiles ended two minutes before it, and T3 auto-paused 15 minutes after its last session (2026-10-20) |
| 8b | The same criterion, with `stop-demo.ps1` | **Pass** | Phase 20: four runs of 338 to 371 seconds, each exit 0, with the profiles off, the guest shut down cleanly in 30 seconds, and both VMs deallocated. Phase 24: T4 stopped too, in 6.7 and 6.2 minutes |
| 9 | Everything, including Arc resources, destroys cleanly. | **Pass**; one item isn't the demo's | Phase 16: the test environment's destroy removed the group, the Arc machine and its SQL instance, the deployment records, and the managed identities, and a subscription snapshot compared with the one before deploy found nothing left; destroy now also deletes the group's budget, which outlives the group ([Phase 16](#phase-16-cleanup-testing)). Phases 20 and 22 destroyed the demo environment with the Arc resources first and the golden group kept. A role assignment that a tenant's governance automation gave one of the demo's identities outside the demo isn't the demo's: the residual check reports it as a **Warning** since the Phase 28 follow-up, and removing it needs Owner (R339). That follow-up also scopes the check to the environment's own group and `deploymentId`, after a destroy next to another environment with the same environment name listed that environment as residue |
| 10 | No secrets in Git or logs. | **Warning** | **Pass:** gitleaks on every pull request, with every default rule plus a broader rule for JSON Web Tokens; a real-value scan of every change; `SECURITY.SECRETSCAN.RUNCOMMANDS` and `.DEPLOYMENTS` on the running environment (2026-10-23); and notebooks committed without outputs. Setup's interactive sign-in can leave sign-in data on the VM; the scripts remove it right after the wizard and verify none remains (R74). **Warning:** `SECURITY.SECRETSCAN.OUTPUT` reports a **Fail** for patterns inside the third-party packages that the Fabric App stages under `out/`, while the demo's own output is clean (a follow-up), and Actions logs are scanned only once someone downloads them (R212) |
| 11 | Every manual step exists only because no supported automation was found, and is documented. | **Warning** | Every manual step reports **Manual action required** with exact steps, and the [automation support matrix](automation-support-matrix.md#what-stays-manual-and-why) justifies each with a Microsoft Learn citation. Most exist because no supported automation exists: the setup wizard (since 2026-11-05 only in the opt-in `PaygWizard` mode; the default `JumpstartImage` mode has no manual step for T2), the Hub tenant setting, the Hub UI, and the Database Agent. The rest are deliberate: human approval, permissions beyond Contributor, your own Azure CLI sign-in, and the GitHub environment settings, which `setup-github-oidc.ps1 -ConfigureGitHub` sets up on request (R283) |
| 12 | Deploy end to end in 30 minutes or less, repeatably, and measure it (set during the build) | **Fail** | 38.8 minutes in Phase 20, then 63.7 minutes with Phase 22's overlaps, which had T1 and T3 ready at 26.8 minutes. T2's SQL Server extension download (R72) keeps T2 on the critical path (R341) |

### Cost

Dated examples from `estimate-cost.ps1` at list prices from the Azure Retail Prices API, for West US 3 and the `lowest-cost` profile. They're examples, not quotes ([cost management](cost-management.md)).

- **A 5-hour session:** $7.41 (2026-10-21), against the $15 session ceiling; $10.30 with T4 on `Standard_D2ds_v5` (2026-10-26).
- **A light-use month:** $65.12 (2026-10-21), against the $250 hard ceiling; $229.75 with T4, including the worst case of a stopped T4 that starts on its own after seven days (2026-10-26).
- **T4's General Purpose fallback (Phase 28 follow-up):** with those 2026-10-26 prices, its cost check's worst-case month ($229.75) and a session ($10.30) are within the default ceilings, so it would create T4. The check reads the prices again at run time.
- **The T2 golden snapshot:** at most about $6.40 a month in the estimate (2026-10-22). It bills until `destroy.ps1 -IncludeGolden` deletes it.
- **Measured:** Phase 16's two-day test cycle came to about $4.50: its measured hours at list price, not a billed amount.
- **The golden recapture and its proof (2026-10-28):** about $3 at list price for the hours they ran: T1 and the T2 host, SQL Server's pay-as-you-go hours, and the test copy. The new snapshot, now the only one, holds about 48 GiB: about $2.40 a month at $0.05 per GB-month.
- **Outside the ceiling:** the Fabric capacity, while it runs (an F16 is about $2.88 an hour at list price, 2026-10-21), and viewer licenses on a capacity smaller than F64 (P11).

### Security summary

- **Footprint:** Contributor only, by default. The default path creates no role assignments and no Key Vault, gives the VMs no public IPs and no inbound management ports, and creates Bastion only on demand; `SECURITY.RBAC.ROLEASSIGNMENTS`, `SECURITY.SECRETS.KEYVAULT`, `SECURITY.NETWORK.NOPUBLICIP`, and `SECURITY.NETWORK.INBOUND` **Pass** live. The optional on-demand activity control needs an Owner once, for its role and grants ([ADR 0020](adr/0020-on-demand-activity-control.md)).
- **Credentials:** Arc onboarding uses the T2 host's identity when it's attached, and otherwise the caller's short-lived token as a protected Run Command parameter, never stored; a sweep after every onboarding verifies that none of it stays on the VMs (Phase 26d). The guest can't reach IMDS or the wire server (live, 2026-10-20). VM passwords are generated per deployment and never stored, and the Run Command that sets one clears the Windows PowerShell log when it recorded the value (R528). T3 and T4 are Microsoft Entra-only. Setup's interactive sign-in can leave sign-in data on the VM; the scripts remove it right after the wizard and verify none remains; the build fails if any remains (R74).
- **Scanning:** gitleaks on every pull request; the demo's own scanner over script output (UTF-16 logs included), deployment history, and Run Command instance views; and actions pinned by full commit SHA.
- **Accepted trade-offs:** T1's identity administers T3 and T4 through the P6 group (R26, R402), NSGs can't restrict egress by FQDN (R7), the nested guest's licensing isn't documented (R21), and the Contributor-only path isn't proven end to end (R210). The [security model](security-model.md) has the threat model.

- **Database Hub and the Database Agent:** the Hub pages and the agent on each target, in a rehearsal, with the live coverage record.

- **A fresh build with the parallel tracks and no golden snapshot,** timed: since Phase 28, one `deploy.ps1` with the guided stop, the status page, the keep-awake, the deferred policy inventory, T4's Burstable fallback, and the Run Command watchdog, with a person at the stop (R561, R566). Since 2026-11-05 the default is `JumpstartImage`, with no person: its first clean deployment ran live on 2026-11-06 and ended **DONE** in 91 minutes ([the live test of 2026-11-06](#the-live-test-of-2026-11-06-a-clean-first-deployment)), so what stays here is the opt-in `PaygWizard` path.
- **An end-to-end run with an identity that holds only Contributor** (R210).
- **The first run from a new tenant:** `init.ps1`, then a deploy whose Fabric track creates the workspace and lakehouse by name (Phases 26a and 26b).
- **The Insights app's Database Agent prompts** in a browser (`FABRIC.APP.BROWSER`).
- **The Insights app after Phase 27's updates:** a redeploy with `configure-fabric-app.ps1`, which builds it with Vite 8.3.5.
- **The output scan** without the Fabric App's third-party packages.
- **The Phase 28 follow-up, live:** the on-demand control's Run Command cleanup once its runbooks are published again, and `start-demo.ps1`'s (R589). `destroy.ps1 -ResidueOnly` passed live on 2026-11-02 (R586–R588, R591).
- **T4's General Purpose fallback** (Phase 28 follow-up): merged without a live proof, the owner's choice of 2026-11-02 after the independent review and a free preflight check (`PREFLIGHT.T4.FALLBACK` **Pass** in West US 3, with prices read live that day). Not run live: the create path (B1ms, then B2s refused, then `Standard_D2ds_v5` created), the loud lines, keeping the size on a redeploy, and the restart dates from `stop-demo.ps1` and `status.ps1`. Watch the first real fallback run (R600, R601). Its follow-up for a server that a failed attempt created is unit-tested only (R602).
- **`business-day` by default** (Phase 28 follow-up, 2026-11-03; review fixes and re-review fixes, 2026-11-04): unit-tested only. Not run live: a `deploy.ps1` and a `start-demo.ps1` that switch from the interim `baseline` once their own Run Commands are done, a Ctrl+C before the switch and one in the middle of it, the interim step on VMs that already run and on VMs a run starts (its retries while SQL Server starts, and the run's wait for it: `START.INTERIM_*`, `DEPLOY.INTERIM_*`), `stop-demo.ps1` during an incident (with a guest stop that `archost01` holds busy), `validate.ps1` during one (a time-out a **Warning**, a missing runner a **Fail**, and a host lane whose status lists several runs a **Warning**, once `deploy.ps1` has sent the new bundle), the problem panels filling, and **Run all** of the two notebooks with `CONFIRM = True` (R603 to R616).
- **Every start brings every target back** (Phase 28 follow-up, 2026-11-03): unit-tested only. Live: a cold Fabric start of every target after the rollout (`deploy.ps1`, then `setup-activity-control.ps1 -SkipFabric`), a capture's return of its source (`GOLDEN.CAPTURE.SOURCE`), a long run's monitoring re-checks, and `T2.GUEST.STATE` on an air-gapped and a released source (R626, R628); since the review of 2026-11-04, a Fabric start of T2 during a capture (refused, the guest left air-gapped), `GOLDEN.CAPTURE.CONTROL` during a run, and `start-demo.ps1` with T1 or T2 down (one 4-minute wait) (R629 to R632); since the re-review, `start-demo.ps1` during a capture (`T2.GOLDEN.SOURCE` **Warning**, the guest left air-gapped) and after a restore that failed before its Release (the marker ended, the guest released) (R633); since the rebase onto PR 16, the first `start-demo.ps1` after a capture (`T2.GOLDEN.SOURCE` before `START.INTERIM_T2`, which is **Not selected** while a capture runs) and a cold start with T1 or T2 down (about 15 minutes) (R634).

Still to see: a run whose sign-in opens the browser at the start, and the safety stop's first evening (R690).

Live: a `deploy.ps1` from a terminal whose `BROWSER` holds the `az login` value (the one line, then the default browser and its account picker), and one whose sign-in window is closed while the CLI is already signed in (the row **Done**, `DEPLOY.FABRIC.APPSIGNIN` **Pass**) (R691, R692). Measured live on 2026-11-06: the download's time, about 11 minutes for 34.1 GiB, about 51 MiB/s, bound by the host's disk (R693, closed); and the first boot's cleanup, `C:\Jumpstart` deleted and `JumpstartImageCheck` passed, through **DONE** (R685, closed). Still to see live: an interrupted download that continues from its partial file instead of starting over (R694).
- **The LSA secret Azure's provisioning leaves** (2026-11-06): unit-tested only. Live: a `deploy.ps1` whose `T2.ARC.ONBOARDING` and `DEPLOY.T1.READY` are **Pass** with the note, and a read-only presence check on `sqlvm01` and `archost01` that finds no LSA `DefaultPassword` secret (R706 to R708).
- **The history report's snapshot at the end of `deploy.ps1`** (2026-11-06): unit-tested only. Live: a `deploy.ps1` that ends **DONE** with `DEPLOY.FABRIC.HISTORY` **Pass**, its time, and the history report showing the run's telemetry with **Blocking Incidents** still blank; then `./scripts/stop-demo.ps1 -SaveHistory` after an incident, and the measure filled; and one with the capacity paused (a **Warning**, the run **DONE**, the capacity left paused) (R709, R711).

## Risk register

Every risk number that the docs, scripts, and tests cite, with its status: **Closed**, **Mitigated** (the risk remains, and the demo handles it), **Accepted** (by design, and documented), or **Open**. The first 30 numbers come from the original plan, and the phases numbered the rest.

| ID | Risk | Status |
|---|---|---|
| R2 | Database Hub, the Database Agent, and the telemetry endpoint are previews and can change | **Mitigated:** documented interfaces only; a changed shape is reported, never a silent **Pass** |
| R3 | Azure Arc on a nested Hyper-V guest isn't explicitly documented | **Closed:** it worked live (2026-10-20) |
| R7 | Network security groups can't restrict HTTPS egress by FQDN | **Accepted** ([ADR 0007](adr/0007-networking-modes-and-egress.md)) |
| R9 | A tenant's Azure Policy can deny part of the deployment | **Mitigated:** preflight's policy inventory and what-if name the assignment before anything is written |
| R21 | Licensing of a Windows guest in nested Hyper-V on Azure isn't documented | **Accepted** ([ADR 0006](adr/0006-sql-editions-and-licensing.md)) |
| R22 | The Arc telemetry role isn't listed in the built-in role catalog pages | **Mitigated:** resolved by name at run time, never pinned |
| R23 | The Database Agent can change, and nothing can test it from code | **Mitigated:** a prompt pack with fallbacks, and a rehearsal before each demo |
| R25 | The default `PaygWizard` mode keeps one manual setup-wizard step per guest build | **Accepted:** the golden snapshot keeps the finished guest, and the `ProductKey` and `Developer` modes avoid the step. Since Phase 28 the step is a guided stop of about 10 minutes in `deploy.ps1`'s run, with everything around the person's choice automated |
| R26 | T1's managed identity is a T3 administrator through the P6 group | **Accepted:** a single-purpose server with synthetic data; a hardened option is in [ADR 0008](adr/0008-identity-rbac-abac.md) |
| R27 | Some tenants don't allow the P6 group | **Mitigated:** the deploying user becomes T3's administrator and runs the T-SQL interactively |
| R28 | GitHub Actions needs a one-time administrator action (P7) | **Accepted:** `setup-github-oidc.ps1` prints the one command |
| R29 | The telemetry endpoint might not accept a notebook's own token | **Closed:** it did (2026-10-21); the printed-KQL fallback stays |
| R30 | Cloning needed a GitHub sign-in while the repository was private | **Closed:** the repository is public |
| R42 | The `demo-day` profile went over the monthly ceiling with P10 data disks | **Closed:** Standard SSD or Premium SSD v2 data disks by default ([ADR 0016](adr/0016-cost-profiles.md)); preflight warns above a ceiling |
| R45c | The policy inventory takes minutes in a large tenant | **Mitigated:** reused for up to 168 hours while the assignments are unchanged; `-SkipPolicyInventory` skips it |
| R49 | Policy-installed extensions can hold a VM's Run Commands for minutes | **Mitigated:** retried for about 12.5 minutes, with the cause named. Since Phase 28 (after a colleague's run sat on seven chunk Run Commands stuck in `Deleting`): chunk deletes aren't waited for, the VM's extensions settle before its artifacts and its loader (at most 20 minutes, named on the status page), a watchdog saves the instance view and restarts the VM once, and the run ends **NOT DONE** naming the extension when it's still stuck; unit-tested, not yet run live |
| R51 | Without data disks, the T2 host needs about 80 GB free on its 128 GiB OS disk | **Mitigated:** the free-space check fails early and points to `dataDiskSku` `E10` |
| R53 | Every guest build downloads about 6.2 GB of pinned media, and a changed file fails its SHA-256 check | **Mitigated:** resumable retries; update the pin after checking the new file's source |
| R71 | The WebView2 installer's link always serves the latest release, so only its signature can be checked | **Mitigated:** HTTPS and a valid Microsoft Authenticode signature, or the step fails |
| R72 | Extension downloads inside the T2 guest fail intermittently, so the SQL Server extension can take 30 minutes or more to arrive | **Open:** no documented fix; the agent retries, and it's T2's critical path on a redeploy (about 2 to 40 minutes measured) |
| R73 | A managed Run Command recreated soon after its deletion can return the previous run's result | **Mitigated:** detected by its start time, retried once, and otherwise failed closed |
| R74 | Setup's interactive sign-in can leave sign-in data on the VM; the scripts remove it right after the wizard and verify none remains; the build fails if any remains | **Mitigated** |
| R75 | Removing Analysis Services and Integration Services from T1 depends on the image's setup media | **Mitigated:** a failure is a health warning, and the services stay stopped and disabled |
| R80 | A managed identity token issued before T1 joined the P6 group can stay cached for up to about 24 hours | **Mitigated:** T1 joins before it asks for its first token; the step retries for 10 minutes, then warns |
| R83 | Recreating T3 right after deleting it can find the name not yet released | **Mitigated:** run `start-demo.ps1` again a few minutes later |
| R87 | A managed identity's new group membership can take hours to apply | **Mitigated:** the warning names the measured wait, and the fallback is documented |
| R121 | Fabric's notebook part path differed between Learn examples | **Closed:** Fabric accepted `notebook-content.ipynb` (2026-10-21) |
| R122 | The notebook parameters-cell tag isn't stated on Learn | **Closed:** a job's parameters overrode the tagged cell (2026-10-21) |
| R142 | The Hub tenant setting's internal name isn't documented, and its title can change | **Mitigated:** matched by title; reading it needs a Fabric administrator |
| R183 | While a T3 activity profile runs, T3's serverless database can't auto-pause | **Mitigated:** profiles end before the auto-shutdown, `stop-demo.ps1` turns them off, and a VM start doesn't restart them unless asked |
| R188 | The evaluation-only T2 mode has no workload runner | **Accepted:** an opt-in mode, reported as **Not selected** |
| R189 | Defender scanning newly extracted files made a folder rename fail | **Closed:** the files are extracted in place, with retries |
| R196 | Rebuilding the T2 guest without a full redeployment isn't supported | **Accepted:** `destroy.ps1`, then `deploy.ps1` |
| R201 | With the Key Vault opt-in, a new role assignment takes minutes to reach Key Vault | **Mitigated:** the first write retries for up to 10 minutes |
| R210 | The Contributor-only path isn't proven end to end: the live runs used an operator who could also assign roles | **Open:** the default path created no role assignment and no Key Vault, verified live |
| R211 | Run Command instance views can't be scanned while the VMs are deallocated | **Mitigated:** a **Warning** that says so; scanned after each start |
| R212 | GitHub Actions logs and notebook exports must be downloaded before they're scanned | **Mitigated:** the procedure in [ADR 0009](adr/0009-secrets-handling.md) |
| R213 | T3 keeps sending telemetry while it's paused, so a fresh sample doesn't prove it's awake | **Accepted:** the readiness gate also checks T3's extended property |
| R214 | The Windows PowerShell 5.1 analyzer profile is Windows Server 2019's, while the VMs run 2022 | **Accepted:** the VM-side tests run in Windows PowerShell 5.1 |
| R215 | Validation's Preflight area takes about 10 minutes in a large tenant, mostly for the policy inventory | **Mitigated:** the inventory cache, `-SkipPolicyInventory`, or `-Area` without Preflight |
| R216 | A few helpers delete temporary files with `Remove-Item`, which an inherited `-WhatIf` skips | **Accepted:** validation calls none of them under `-WhatIf` |
| R217 | `validate.ps1` exits 1, not 2, for **Manual action required** with `-TreatManualAsError` | **Accepted:** the contract with the validate workflow ([ADR 0012](adr/0012-validation-model.md)) |
| R218 | The secret scanner can flag look-alike text, and miss a secret that matches no rule | **Mitigated:** filters, registered values, and gitleaks on the repository |
| R219 | Right after a restart, the bootstrap loader can still report Pending | **Mitigated:** a **Warning**, never a **Fail** |
| R220 | The Marketplace image installs Analysis Services and Integration Services on T1 | **Closed:** T1's bootstrap removes them (Phase 7) |
| R221 | The guest's port ACLs haven't been tested on their own, with the guest firewall off | **Open:** both were verified together live (2026-10-20) |
| R222 | Validation's T3 checks through T1 resume a paused T3, which then bills until it auto-pauses | **Accepted:** only while T1 runs; `-Target` leaves T3 out |
| R223 | Validation creates temporary Run Commands, and a run that's killed leaves one behind | **Mitigated:** the next run deletes a leftover first |
| R224 | `COST.AUTOSHUTDOWN` reads the schedules by name and type instead of by ID | **Closed:** the cost checks passed in Phase 20's and Phase 22's validation runs |
| R225 | gitleaks can't read a token in a UTF-16 file | **Mitigated:** logs stay in the Git-ignored `out/`, and the demo's scanner decodes UTF-16 |
| R226 | The subscription guard depended on the Azure CLI's default subscription | **Closed:** it checks the configured subscription's own sign-in (2026-10-21) |
| R227 | The gitleaks configuration exempts the public commit pins in `config/versions.json` from one default rule | **Accepted:** one rule, one file, and those keys only; tests check the scope |
| R273 | Whether a budget at a resource group's scope outlives the group wasn't documented | **Closed:** it does, so `destroy.ps1` deletes it (2026-10-22) |
| R283 | GitHub environments and their secrets were set by hand, although GitHub documents an API for them | **Mitigated:** `setup-github-oidc.ps1 -ConfigureGitHub` sets them up on request, with your own GitHub CLI sign-in (Phase 26c); without it, the script prints the `gh` commands |
| R330 | Restoring a pay-as-you-go install from a disk snapshot isn't documented | **Accepted:** validated twice live (Standard, pay-as-you-go, and monitoring), and disclosed in [limitations](limitations.md) |
| R331 | The golden snapshot holds the hosts' secrets, such as password hashes and the guest's Arc agent key | **Mitigated:** no network access and no export; captured only after a clean sign-in sweep (Phase 26d), and the passwords are rotated at restore |
| R332 | Any Contributor on the subscription can create a disk from the snapshot | **Accepted:** restrict the golden group with a role assignment if the subscription is shared |
| R333 | A copy of a live source could check in as the captured Arc identity | **Mitigated:** the Release fails closed when it can't read the agent, and resets the copy unless its agent names another identity (proven live, 2026-10-23) |
| R334 | The guest's evaluation period runs on inside the snapshot | **Mitigated:** `status.ps1` and preflight warn 30 and 7 days before it ends; a new capture needs a fresh build |
| R335 | The SQL Server extension's download (R72) is on T2's path at every restore | **Open** (R72) |
| R336 | Policy-installed extensions can block Run Commands for a while | **Mitigated:** the conflict retry; deallocating and starting the host cleared a long block. Since Phase 28, the watchdog does the equivalent itself: one `az vm restart` of a VM whose extension work stays stuck for about 15 to 20 minutes (T1 before its bootstrap; the T2 host never during the guided stop, the golden Release, or its guest build), with its instance view saved for the diagnostics bundle (R49) |
| R337 | The parallel tracks' processes share one Azure CLI token cache | **Mitigated:** staggered starts and the guard's token retry; no conflict seen |
| R338 | Azure Resource Graph lists deleted Arc resources for a while after their group is gone | **Open:** the explicit Arc deletes run first, and the residual check reports the lag |
| R339 | A tenant's governance automation can give a demo identity a role assignment outside the demo, which outlives destroy | **Accepted:** not the demo's; the residual check reports it as a **Warning** that names its scope (a **Fail** before the Phase 28 follow-up), never deletes it, and removing it needs Owner or User Access Administrator |
| R340 | The budget outlives its group (R273) | **Closed** |
| R341 | The 30-minute deploy target isn't met | **Open:** 38.8 minutes, then 63.7 with the overlaps, which had T1 and T3 ready at 26.8; T2's extension download (R72) is the critical path |
| R342 | Unified inventory drops uninstalled features only after the SQL IaaS Agent restarts | **Closed:** T1's step restarts it, and validation fails if they're still listed 10 minutes later |
| R343 | `t2Source` `auto` builds T2 when no usable snapshot exists | **Accepted:** `PREFLIGHT.T2.SOURCE` says which source and why |
| R344 | A reused policy inventory can miss a changed definition behind an unchanged assignment | **Accepted:** the what-if still runs, and the cache lasts 168 hours at most |
| R345 | The parallel deploy differs from the sequential one in side effects | **Mitigated:** the two found live are fixed, and `-Sequential` stays |
| R346 | The runner's status can exceed Run Command's 4,096 bytes of output, so the bounds check can't read it | **Open:** with Phase 25b's longer outcomes, `WORKLOAD.<target>.BOUNDS` was a **Warning** on every target in Phase 25d's live run; the runner still clamps every scenario to its limits, and a compact status for the check is a follow-up |
| R347 | The golden snapshot's quarantine test needs quota for one more host and takes over an hour | **Accepted:** it checks the quota first |
| R348 | `configure-fabric-app.ps1` might take parameters the Fabric track doesn't pass | **Accepted:** the track passes only the parameters the script declares |
| R349 | The notebooks carried real Fabric workspace and lakehouse IDs | **Closed:** the notebooks carry no Fabric IDs, and the import injects the resolved ones (Phase 26a) |
| R400 | Updating an existing flexible server in a redeploy might be refused, or restart it | **Closed:** it succeeded, with the server ready throughout (2026-10-26) |
| R401 | T1's managed identity might not sign in to T4 as the P6 group's role | **Closed:** it did (2026-10-26) |
| R402 | T1's identity is a T4 administrator through the P6 group, as it is T3's | **Accepted** (R26) |
| R419 | Creating T4 makes a first deploy's template take longer: 8 minutes 45 seconds instead of about 2 | **Accepted:** only when T4 is created |
| R440 | A subscription's automation can switch a deallocated VM's disks to another type, which made the template fail | **Mitigated:** `deploy.ps1` keeps the current types with a **Warning**, and `status.ps1` reports the drift |
| R441 | Azure can refuse T4's size with `SkuNotAvailable` although the region lists it | **Mitigated:** a **Fail** that names the next size, chosen with `t4SkuName` and `t4AvailabilityZone`. Since Phase 28, without `t4SkuName`, the run falls back from B1ms to B2s, and since its follow-up (2026-11-02) to General Purpose `Standard_D2ds_v5` after a cost check against both ceilings, then to no T4 (a **Warning**, and **DONE** says so); a size you set is unchanged |
| R442 | T1's monitoring plugin may not come back after a start | **Mitigated:** the start waits up to 5 minutes for the plugin's status before it restarts the agent (Phase 25b); proven live in Phase 25d, where the plugin reported 36 seconds into one start and came back 209 seconds after a restart in another |
| R480 | Without a body, the runbooks' Windows PowerShell 5.1 sent a POST in a form encoding that the PostgreSQL resource provider refuses, so the control couldn't start or stop T4 | **Closed:** a POST without a body sends an empty JSON object; T4's start and stop through the runbooks proven live (Phase 25d) |
| R481 | A second run of `setup-activity-control.ps1` unlinked the daily T4 safety stop: Azure Automation names the schedule's time zone in another form, and refused the old link ID | **Closed:** setup compares the zones' rules and gives a new link a new ID; relinked and checked live (Phase 25d) |
| R482 | T4's profile lane can't end runs an earlier host left Running, so after a host restart T4's baseline waits for that run's deadline | **Open:** T4's refused starts are now tried again every 30 seconds, as on T1 to T3; recovering T4's runs is a follow-up |
| R483 | On a 2-vCPU target under `capacity-max`, sessions can time out waiting to open their connections, which failed the run on T2 | **Mitigated:** an open that timed out behind other opens is tried again within the connect window; not yet run live |
| R484 | `capacity-max` on T1 saturates `sqlvm01`, which also runs T3's and T4's sessions: T1's profile read took over 150 seconds, and T4's baseline couldn't connect | **Accepted:** the bounded reads and the retries keep each lane serving; T3's `capacity-max` runs in a round of its own |
| R485 | A deploy can leave T1's monitoring plugin not running, and the T2 track could pass while its host had failed | **Mitigated:** the deploy restarts the agent (`DEPLOY.T1.MONITORING`) and waits for T2's host (`DEPLOY.T2.READY`); unit-tested, not yet run live |
| R486 | An on-demand run's auto-stop counts from the request, so targets that start slowly get less of their activity | **Accepted:** the auto-stop bounds the cost; a T4 that can't start no longer delays the others |
| R487 | The control notebook's workspace needs a managed virtual network, so each request waits for a Spark start | **Accepted:** 107 to 166 seconds live; the demo's own workspace keeps its fast starts ([ADR 0020](adr/0020-on-demand-activity-control.md)) |
| R488 | `status.ps1` shows a VM's health **Warning** without its reason | **Open:** the reason is in the VM's health file (on `sqlvm01`, the one-time recovery of SQL Server administrator access); showing it is a follow-up |
| R489 | `-GrantRole -WhatIf` doesn't list A3, and setup's summary says ready while one of its steps warns | **Open:** the real run grants and checks A3 (**Pass** live); a follow-up |
| R523 | A file scan can't prove that deleted sign-in data is gone from a disk's free space, so the golden snapshot can hold fragments of its guest build's sign-in, which expired within about an hour | **Mitigated:** the only golden snapshot, captured on 2026-10-28, was taken after `capture-t2-golden.ps1 -WipeFreeSpace` zero-filled and trimmed the guest's and the host's free space and compacted the guest's disk; the older one was deleted (Phase 26d) |
| R528 | Run Command hands protected parameters to a script as arguments, so the Windows PowerShell log records the one-time host password and, in `ProductKey` mode, the SQL Server product key | **Mitigated:** the Run Command that receives the value drops its own closing event and clears that log when it holds the value (a **Warning**: endpoint protection may notice); the sweep finds product keys in older entries. Left: a value from a Run Command stopped before its search (until the next rotation or `-Clean`), a copy that endpoint protection sent off the VM, and the Security log when process-creation auditing records command lines (**Manual action required**) (Phase 26f) |
| R530 | Direct Lake doesn't see tables that a notebook has just created in a new lakehouse for a while (about 20 minutes on 2026-10-27), so a first deploy's model refresh failed with 0xC14700DF | **Mitigated:** `configure-powerbi.ps1` refreshes again every 2 minutes for up to 30 minutes while that error lasts, and a **Fail** at the end says to wait or to run the notebook (Phase 26g; the retry itself isn't proven live yet) |
| R540 | The Rayfin CLI 1.36.2, a dev dependency of the Insights app, declares an `@azure/msal-node` range with a high advisory (GHSA-xcx6-jx8p-x3h5) that's fixed only two major versions on, so `npm audit` reports it | **Open:** the advisory concerns apps that pass the authorization response to `acquireTokenByCode`. The CLI never does: its browser sign-in goes through MSAL's `acquireTokenInteractive`, which in the installed versions passes only the code (the advisory's workaround), so the vulnerable pattern isn't reached through the CLI's sign-in as installed. Not overridden, because nothing offline can prove the CLI's sign-in with MSAL Node 7; it waits for a Rayfin release that accepts MSAL Node 7.1 or later, or an override that a live sign-in proves (Phase 27) |
| R560 | While the guest's one automatic sign-in for the guided stop is armed, an administrator of the T2 host, or someone with the guest's disk, could read its temporary password from the LSA secret, and a security scan of the guest would flag automatic sign-in | **Accepted** (owner-approved, 2026-10-29): kept only as the LSA secret, cleared at the guest's sign-in and by the host's cleanup in a `finally`, rotated right after; a leftover fails the sign-in scan and blocks a golden capture ([ADR 0009](adr/0009-secrets-handling.md#phase-28-amendment-the-guests-one-automatic-sign-in-and-the-portal-password-reset-2026-10-29)) (Phase 28) |
| R561 | The guided stop's hops are unproven live: the guest's automatic sign-in over PowerShell Direct and the LSA API, the logon tasks, Virtual Machine Connection at the operator's sign-in, and the pre-filled setup | **Open:** unit-tested with mocks; after 3 failed arms the host gives the manual steps, and the by-hand path still works (Phase 28) |
| R562 | The setup check reads the Azure extension from setup's generated configuration file in its log folder, an inference: pay-as-you-go itself can't be read in the guest before the extension reports it | **Mitigated:** `T2.ARC.SQLINSTANCE` checks the license type in Azure after onboarding, and a wrong install isn't onboarded (Phase 28) |
| R563 | The settle wait reads extension states from `az vm get-instance-view`; an unexpected shape could make it wait in vain or not at all | **Mitigated:** bounded (20 minutes), then the watchdog; unproven live (Phase 28) |
| R564 | The watchdog's one restart could interrupt work on a VM | **Accepted:** once per run, T1 only before its bootstrap, never the T2 host while the guided stop's time limit is open, while the golden Release runs on it, or while its guest build may be running, judged from the deployment state (review fix and its follow-up, R569); a bootstrap state machine resumes after a restart by design (ADR 0003) (Phase 28) |
| R565 | With `networkMode` `private`, the deferred policy inventory reports a Modify or audit policy's effect only at the end of the run | **Accepted:** the template's what-if still catches a denial before anything is created, and the inventory resolves nothing the template needs in that mode (Phase 28). `public` isn't deferred (review fix): a Modify policy that turns SQL public access off isn't a denial the what-if sees, so `PREFLIGHT.POLICY.SQLPUBLIC` still stops that run before anything is created |
| R566 | The first-run estimates (about 2 to 3 hours, a person about 60 to 90 minutes in) aren't measured; a slower build moves the guided stop later | **Open:** the run waits up to `-T2WaitMinutes` (180) and at least 45 minutes once it asks, and a time-out ends **NOT DONE** with the command that continues; the first fresh build with the parallel tracks measures it (Phase 28) |
| R567 | `deploy.ps1` keeps only a Windows PC awake, and a GitHub codespace still stops after its idle timeout or 12 hours | **Mitigated:** progress lines at least every 5 minutes reset a codespace's idle timeout; the start banner says how to raise it; a rerun continues where it stopped (Phase 28) |
| R568 | Without a clipboard, the person sets the host password in the portal (**Reset password**, through the VMAccess extension), and the demo can rotate it only while the host runs | **Mitigated:** the handover is recorded, the guided stop's cleanup and `stop-demo.ps1` rotate it (a **Warning** with the portal's way when that can't run yet), and the next start rotates one left over (Phase 28) |
| R569 | The watchdog judges whether the T2 host may be building from the deployment state (`DbHubT2BuildUtc` and the T2 track's step), not from the host, whose agent is what's stuck; a record that couldn't be written, or a generation started before the record existed and then hidden by a rerun of earlier steps, could let its one restart interrupt a build | **Accepted:** the bootstrap state machine resumes after a restart by design (ADR 0003), a record that can't be written is a logged **Warning**, and while a generation is on record the host isn't restarted, so the run ends **NOT DONE** with `DEPLOY.EXTENSIONS_ARCHOST01` instead (Phase 28 review follow-up) |
| R570 | Azure Cloud Shell is recognized by `AZUREPS_HOST_ENVIRONMENT`, which Learn doesn't document | **Mitigated:** only a second line: Cloud Shell is Linux without an X display, which the documented clipboard rule (`Set-Clipboard` needs `xclip` on Linux) already refuses, and the fallback is the portal's **Reset password** (Phase 28 review fix) |
| R571 | A Ctrl+C at the guided stop leaves Bastion up and the guided stop's host password unrotated until the next `deploy.ps1` cleans them up when it gets to T2 (whether or not setup finished meanwhile) or `stop-demo.ps1` runs, and the guest's one-time sign-in armed until the host's request expires | **Accepted:** the person chose to stop, and the banner and the status page say what's left and the command; the request expires on its own (its time limit plus 5 minutes), and the host's next pass then turns the sign-in off and removes its data, with the fail-closed scan (brief item 12); `status.ps1` reports a Bastion that's still up, and the bastion-cleanup workflow, where it's set up, removes it after its expiry; the handover's record makes `stop-demo.ps1` rotate the password; and no run is **DONE** while the state records either (Phase 28 live finding and its review) |
| R572 | A track process that doesn't stop within about 3 seconds of a stop is ended; PowerShell then lists its job as running for about a minute, and a `pwsh -File deploy.ps1` process waits that minute before it exits | **Accepted:** only a track blocked in a call that can't be interrupted; the run's ending (the banner, the status page, the PC's sleep) comes first, and its slow Azure cleanups are left to the next run either way (Phase 28 live finding) |
| R573 | A `deploy.ps1` run whose T2 step runs while a `start-demo.ps1 -Interactive` session is open removes that session's Bastion and rotates the host password it handed over; one whose T2 step doesn't run ends **NOT DONE** until `stop-demo.ps1` runs | **Accepted:** **DONE** never leaves access to the T2 host behind, and the state can't tell a session's handover from a guided stop's; the banner names `./scripts/stop-demo.ps1 -RemoveBastionOnly`, and `start-demo.ps1 -Interactive` opens a new session ([ADR 0009](adr/0009-secrets-handling.md#no-clipboard-the-portals-reset-password); Phase 28 review) |
| R574 | `deploy.ps1` starts VMs that were stopped before preflight, so a run whose preflight then fails leaves them running, and billing, until `stop-demo.ps1` or the nightly auto-shutdown; and a resumed run waits for them, and for a stopped T4 (15 minutes at most), before its tracks start | **Accepted:** only on an existing environment and when the run goes past preflight; `DEPLOY.VMSTART` says what it started, and a **Warning** after a failed preflight says they keep running; the VM boot overlaps preflight, and Run Commands, the template, and T1's T4 step need them running anyway (Phase 28 live finding, 2026-10-30) |
| R575 | Setup's **Use Azure Login** in the T2 guest is an Internet Explorer-based control, so the guided stop turns IE Enhanced Security Configuration off for administrators in the guest, and makes Microsoft's sign-in hosts Trusted sites for its Administrator, from the preparation until its cleanup (usually 15 to 60 minutes). Why setup doesn't use the installed WebView2 runtime is an inference from MSAL.NET's documentation (its legacy web view for Microsoft Entra ID authorities), not from SQL Server's; and restoring the Trusted sites needs the Administrator's registry hive (loaded, or loaded with `reg load`) | **Accepted:** only while `deploy.ps1` waits for a person; the cleanup after success, failure, or a time-out turns IE ESC back on and restores the entries from a snapshot, fail closed (the setup step isn't done before, and `GuestSignInCleanup` and the golden capture still require IE ESC on); only the guest's administrators browse there, and it has no inbound path. The dialog's **Add...** is the person's fallback, and the instructions say so ([ADR 0009](adr/0009-secrets-handling.md#setups-azure-sign-in-ie-enhanced-security-configuration-live-finding-2026-10-30); Phase 28 live finding, 2026-10-30) |
| R576 | Every `deploy.ps1` run that ends with the validation and has no T1 Monitoring step checks T1's monitoring plugin, and restarts the SQL IaaS Agent service when it isn't running; and a target whose telemetry didn't come gets its monitoring checked and its telemetry waited for once more (up to `-TelemetryWaitMinutes`, 15) before the run is judged, so a run that ends **NOT DONE** on telemetry takes that much longer. T2 has no restart: its monitoring is a resource setting | **Accepted:** one extension read when the plugin runs; the restart is the one `start-demo.ps1` does, live in 144 to 166 seconds; the check runs in its own process beside the tracks, so the guided stop's prompt never waits for it, and it's ended with them on a stop; an access error, an unknown resource ID, or telemetry under another ID isn't retried, and DONE's checks do it only when everything else passed (Phase 28 live finding, 2026-10-30) |

And a `None` or `Refused` record that an earlier run wrote for a server that exists stays until the next `deploy.ps1` that deploys the template (`None`) or until `t4SkuName` is set (`Refused`) | **Mitigated:** a read that fails is rare, and taking it for an existing server would create a General Purpose server without its cost check when none exists. `status.ps1` (`STATUS.T4`) and `validate.ps1` (`T4.SERVER`) report a server that exists although the record says T4 is off as a **Warning** that it bills, with what keeps or removes it; `stop-demo.ps1` stops it whatever the record says; and the next `deploy.ps1` keeps it at its own size and clears a `None` record (Phase 28 follow-up; review of 64c28b2) |

Watch the first deploy and start: `DEPLOY.INCIDENTS_*` or `START.INCIDENTS_*` **Pass**, after a stop `DEPLOY.INTERIM_*` or `START.INTERIM_*` **Pass** with its seconds and attempts, `status.ps1`'s `STATUS.PROFILE_*` saying `business-day`, and the problem panels within the hour (Phase 28 follow-up, 2026-11-03) |
| R604 | A configuration that `init.ps1` wrote before 2026-11-03, and a GitHub secret built from one, names `"activityProfile": "baseline"` (`init.ps1` copies the example file), so it keeps `baseline`, with no notice | **Accepted:** an explicit value always wins, by design; the README, deployment, and the walkthrough say to remove the key or set `business-day`, and `status.ps1`'s `STATUS.PROFILE_*` says how to choose `business-day` for a configured `baseline` (Phase 28 follow-up) |
| R605 | Ctrl+C or a failure: the switch is sent without waiting, so a track process's interim `baseline` that's still in flight can land after it, and a target that was set to `baseline` and then skipped stays on `baseline` until the auto-shutdown; a switch that can't be sent at all leaves `baseline` on every target the run set | **Mitigated:** the console says so, with `./scripts/demo-control.ps1 -Target all -Scenario business-day -Duration 240`; a VM is forgotten only once Azure accepts its switch request, and the interim step notes each VM before its request goes out, so a Ctrl+C in the middle of either leaves the rest to that send, which says what it sent (review of PR 16, 2026-11-04); `status.ps1` names a `baseline` that a run hasn't switched yet, with the command; the next `start-demo.ps1` or `deploy.ps1` switches it; every profile ends at the auto-shutdown (Phase 28 follow-up) |
| R606 | `stop-demo.ps1` deallocates a VM whose profile wasn't off within 3 minutes, so its stored profile stays until the auto-shutdown it was set for: a VM started some other way before then (the portal, a restart) resumes it, unless the off, which does nothing after 10 minutes, still runs first; and a guest whose clean shutdown runs out of time, also while `archost01` answers 409 Conflict until the bound, is shut down by the host's automatic stop action | **Accepted:** the stop never waits on an incident (the coordinator's requirement); the **Warning** says the VM was deallocated anyway and how to turn the activity off after such a start (`demo-control.ps1 -Target all -Scenario off`); a guest stop that runs out of time on 409s is that **Warning** too, not a **Fail** (review of PR 16, 2026-11-04); `start-demo.ps1` and `deploy.ps1` set the interim `baseline` on a VM they start as soon as it runs; the VMs set none at boot by default (Phase 28 follow-up) |
| R607 | Whether Azure deallocates a VM while the profile's managed Run Command or the bounded guest stop's action Run Command still runs there, or refuses the deallocation until it ends, wasn't observed live; if it refuses it, a stop takes longer than its bound | **Open:** `Invoke-DemoVmPowerAction` retries a 409 Conflict for about 12.5 minutes, as before, then reports `STOP.VM_<vm>` **Fail** with the error, and the nightly auto-shutdown deallocates the VM regardless. Watch the first `stop-demo.ps1` during an incident: `STOP.PROFILE_*`, `STOP.GUEST`, and `STOP.VM_*` with their seconds (Phase 28 follow-up) |
| R608 | The estimate prices T3 at its maximum vCores for every running hour, which covers `business-day`'s T3 incidents, but counts no disk transactions or backup storage for any profile: Standard SSD operations under `io-pressure`, and T3's log backups beyond the free backup storage of its 32 GB maximum size when `log-rate-pressure`'s up to 2,048 MB of log an hour is kept for 7 days under heavy daily use | **Accepted:** small against the ceilings' margins: at most $0.51 a session and $1.01 a light-use month together with T3 between its minimum and maximum vCores, in a dated estimate (2026-11-03, West US 3, list prices from the Azure Retail Prices API; cost management); backups beyond the allowance list at $0.10 per GB-month (LRS) that day; the budget alerts on what's actually billed (Phase 28 follow-up) |
| R609 | `validate.ps1` still turns some **Fail**s into **Warning**s during an incident's minutes plus 3, which cover 33% of each hour on T1, 70% on T2, 43% on T3, and 58% on T4 (with T1's, for the reads `sqlvm01` makes): a runner status read or `T3.PROPERTY` whose failure looks like load (a time-out, a 409, a lock, or a resource limit), by the configured profile and the clock, and a host lane, by the target's own status. A fault that fails the same way (a Run Command stuck for another reason, a database that keeps timing out) is a **Warning** until the area runs again outside the window | **Accepted (narrowed after the review of PR 16, 2026-11-04):** a fault no incident causes (settings.json missing, no runner, a refused login, a host whose process ended) and any failure the check doesn't recognize stay a **Fail**, at any time; a host lane counts only while the VM runs an unexpired `business-day`, which the VM's relay reads from the runner's whole status (re-review of PR 16; R615); the **Warning** keeps the original message, names the incident, and gives the time to run the area again, after which a **Fail** is a fault; a property that's read and wrong stays a **Fail**; **DONE**'s checks don't read through a target's load (Phase 28 follow-up) |
| R610 | `CONFIRM = True` by default (the owner, 2026-11-03): **Run all** of `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity` sends `business-day` on every target for 240 minutes, which replaces the profile `start-demo.ps1` set (until the auto-shutdown) with one that ends 240 minutes later, again on every **Run all** more than 2 minutes apart (each a `demo-control.yml` job, with P9); without P9, each **Run all** records a request in `scenario_runs` as **Manual action required**, whether or not the presenter runs the printed command; and a pipeline or job run of either notebook that doesn't pass `CONFIRM` acts too | **Accepted:** the owner's decision (no dry-run defaults); `STOP_ACTIVITY` and `PURGE_DEMO_TABLES` stay `False`, so **Run all** never stops activity or drops a table; `CONFIRM = False` gives a read-only run, which the scripts' own notebook runs pass; the request is idempotent on the VMs; the notebooks, the README, `fabric/README.md`, and ADR 0014 say so (Phase 28 follow-up) |
| R611 | A configuration without `activityProfile` gets its notice on every run (once per run, as `PREFLIGHT.ACTIVITYPROFILE`, `DEPLOY.ACTIVITYPROFILE`, or `START.ACTIVITYPROFILE`), not once ever | **Accepted:** no script writes the configuration, and a run keeps no memory of earlier ones; the notice is one console line and one **Pass**, and it stops once `activityProfile` is set (Phase 28 follow-up) |
| R612 | `validate.ps1` tells a failure load causes by its text (`Test-DemoLoadFailureText`): a load failure whose wording it doesn't know (a driver's new message) stays a **Fail** during an incident, and a text that names both a fault and a sign of load counts as the fault | **Accepted:** a **Fail** is the safe side; the list covers the incidents' own failure modes (a Run Command, runner, or CLI time-out, 409 Conflict, SQL Server and Azure SQL time-outs, lock time-outs, deadlocks, 10928, 10936, 40501, and PostgreSQL 53300, 57014, and 55P03), a test pins each, and a live finding can widen it (review of PR 16, 2026-11-04) |
| R613 | A VM a run starts gets the interim `baseline` as soon as Azure reports it running, but its Run Command runs once the VM agent is ready, and T1's or T2's profile is set only once its SQL Server, or T2's guest, answers: until then the profile host can resume the profile stored there (about 2 minutes after the boot), so an incident due in those minutes can start, and a target that doesn't answer within the 10 minutes the VM tries keeps the stored profile under the run's own Run Commands there | **Mitigated (re-review of PR 16, 2026-11-04):** T3's and T4's profiles, files on T1, are set at once; the VM tries T1's or T2's every 15 seconds, and the run's first Run Command there waits for it, so an incident that started ends early (the runner applies `baseline` within its poll, `pollSeconds` 10, and stops the runs it doesn't want) before the run's own Run Commands there begin; one not set in time is a `START.INTERIM_*` or `DEPLOY.INTERIM_*` **Warning** with `validate.ps1 -Area Workload -Target <target>` and `demo-control.ps1 -Target <target> -Scenario business-day -Duration 240`; with `activityProfileOnBoot` the VM sets `baseline` itself at boot; the run's switch brings the profile back at its end (review of PR 16, 2026-11-04) |
| R614 | A stop (Ctrl+C) in the middle of a switch: a VM whose request Azure accepted just before the stop can get the same switch again from the fallback, and the fallback's line names only what it sent then | **Accepted:** sending `business-day` again is harmless (the request is idempotent on the VM, and the one Run Command name is replaced); the VMs whose requests were in run their switch; `status.ps1` shows each target's profile (review of PR 16, 2026-11-04) |
| R615 | A VM whose bundle predates the relay's profile (re-review of PR 16) still sends only the last 1,800 characters of the runner's status, so while its status lists two or more runs, its host lane stays a **Fail** during an incident, as before this change | **Accepted:** a **Fail** is the safe side; the next `deploy.ps1` sends the new bundle; without the relay's profile, `validate.ps1` still reads the profile from the kept output, which a status with one run keeps whole; a test pins both (re-review of PR 16, 2026-11-04) |
| R616 | A start or deploy that boots a VM now waits for the interim there before its own Run Commands: about 12.5 minutes when T1's SQL Server or the T2 guest never answers, and for a deploy, that wait comes before T4's (re-review of PR 16, 2026-11-04) | **Accepted:** the wait ends as soon as each target is set, expected within a few minutes of the boot (not measured live); a target that isn't set up isn't tried again; a target not set in time is a **Warning** with the commands, and the run goes on; T4's start, requested with the VMs, goes on meanwhile; a VM that was already running isn't waited for (re-review of PR 16, 2026-11-04) |
| R620 | A capture interrupted after Prepare (Ctrl+C, a closed window, a crash) can still leave its source air-gapped: PowerShell runs the `finally` that returns the source, but a stopping pipeline can't start the Run Commands it needs | **Mitigated:** the next capture releases it before anything else and ends its marker; `start-demo.ps1` and `deploy.ps1` do once its snapshot exists (before that, no snapshot names the host); a start of T2 from the Fabric control does once the marker no longer counts (R629); `status.ps1` and `validate.ps1` report it (`T2.GUEST.STATE` **Fail**, `T2.ARC.CONNECTED` **Fail** after 10 minutes) with the command (Phase 28 follow-up, 2026-11-03) |
| R621 | `GOLDEN.TEST.SOURCE` proves less when the source host runs during a boot test: the source heartbeats as `arcsql01` itself, so its status can't show an impostor | **Accepted:** the copy's isolation (deny-all outbound before its first boot, the endpoint probes from the guest) and its reset before any network are the proof; the check reports a **Warning** that says to stop the demo first for the full proof, and still fails on an Administrative operation or a changed machine ID ([ADR 0018](adr/0018-fast-redeploy-parallel-tracks-golden-snapshot.md#amendments-2026-11-03-the-capture-returns-its-source-to-service)) |
| R622 | A capture's return of its source can wait up to 30 minutes for `arcsql01` to read Connected after the air gap, because a left-over Connected status is told from a new one only by a later status change or by 30 minutes passing ([agent status](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-arc/servers/overview#agent-status)) | **Accepted:** bounded; a source that went Disconnected during the capture (any capture with `-WipeFreeSpace`) proves itself as soon as it reconnects |
| R623 | A Fabric run's `DbHub-Control` job now lasts until its dispatches and T1's plugin check are done (at most about 35 minutes, usually less than 15), and each re-check starts a `DbHub-Monitor` job of a few minutes | **Accepted:** bounded; Azure Automation bills job run time, priced at run time like every other cost ([ADR 0016](adr/0016-cost-profiles.md)); nothing waits on the notebook's side |
| R624 | Until both the bundle (`deploy.ps1`) and the runbooks (`setup-activity-control.ps1 -SkipFabric`) are updated, a mix behaves as before: an old host ignores `readySeconds`, so a cold start can still fail a dispatch, and a new host behind old runbooks may outlast their shorter Run Command timeouts | **Mitigated:** `CONTROL.SETUP` is **Manual action required** while `DbHub-Monitor` is missing; the rollout is in ADR 0020, the CHANGELOG, `fabric/control/README.md`, and the pull request |
| R625 | The readiness wait retries only the documented transient errors; a cold start that fails with another error is taken as an answer, and the command fails as it did before | **Accepted:** the lists are Learn's (SqlClient's built-in list, the network-related errors, Azure SQL Database's transient faults, PostgreSQL's connection exceptions); the Run Command's result names the error, and the next run tries again |
| R626 | `T2.ARC.CONNECTED`'s 10 minutes count from the host's start as the instance view's provisioning-status time gives it; Learn documents that time only as "the time of the status" | **Open:** confirm live that `status.ps1` right after a start reports a **Warning**, not a **Fail**, for a machine that was Disconnected for days; without a host time the rule falls back to the last status change alone |
| R627 | The control's T2 relay releases a source but can't tag the snapshot (`dbhubSourceReleasedUtc`; it has no rights on `rg-<prefix>-golden`), so the next `start-demo.ps1` or `deploy.ps1` runs the Release again | **Accepted:** on a source the Release only reconnects what's connected and rewrites `releasedUtc`, then tags the snapshot |
| R628 | All of the 2026-11-03 change is proven by unit tests only | **Open:** the coordinator's live checklist in the pull request: a cold start of every target from the notebook after the rollout, a capture's `GOLDEN.CAPTURE.SOURCE`, a long run's re-checks, and `T2.GUEST.STATE` and `T1.SQLVM.MONITORING` on the broken states |
| R629 | A capture interrupted after Prepare (Ctrl+C) leaves its marker, so a Fabric start of T2 refuses the source for up to 6 hours | **Accepted:** the bound is longer than a capture takes from Prepare to its return of the source; the refusal and `T2.GUEST.STATE` say when it ends, and the next `capture-t2-golden.ps1` releases the source and ends the marker at once (2026-11-04) |
| R630 | The capture refuses only while a run is active when it starts: a Fabric run started during the capture still starts `archost01` (also between the deallocation and the snapshot, which then copies a running host's disk) and its auto-stop can deallocate it during the return of the source; the relay keeps the guest air-gapped either way | **Accepted:** the capture and the Fabric control are the same owner's; `GOLDEN.CAPTURE.SOURCE` reports a return cut short, and `test-t2-golden.ps1` proves a snapshot before it's used (2026-11-04) |
| R631 | Until `deploy.ps1` updates `archost01`'s bundle, its relay doesn't read the capture marker that the module's Prepare (sent as content) already writes | **Mitigated:** the rollout runs `deploy.ps1` first; the CHANGELOG says to run it before the next capture (2026-11-04) |
| R632 | `start-demo.ps1` gives T1 and T2 one 4-minute readiness budget, so a target that takes longer than that after the start gets a **Warning**, not its profile | **Accepted:** the same time the three attempts allowed before 2026-11-03; the Warning says to set it again with `demo-control.yml` or `start-demo.ps1 -ActivityProfile`, and a Fabric start waits 10 or 20 minutes (2026-11-04) |
| R633 | A capture marker that can't be read, or a host whose Azure VM ID can't be read while a marker counts, holds every release but the capture's own back: the Fabric relay, `start-demo.ps1`, and `deploy.ps1` leave the guest air-gapped | **Accepted:** fail closed by design, because only Prepare writes the marker; the next `capture-t2-golden.ps1` ends it at once, `start-demo.ps1` does once that capture's snapshot exists, and `T2.GUEST.STATE` and `T2.GOLDEN.SOURCE` say so (re-review, 2026-11-04) |
| R634 | Since PR 16's interim `baseline`, a start that boots the VMs waits about 15 minutes for a T1 or T2 that never answers (the interim's 10 minutes and 150 seconds, then one attempt each for the profile and the switch), about 19 when both never answer; and on a host whose relay predates this branch, PowerShell Direct's "not in running state" now ends T2's interim at once, **Not selected**, where a guest Hyper-V hadn't started yet would have been tried again | **Accepted:** bounded and reported (`START.INTERIM_*` and `START.PROFILE_*` Warnings with the commands); the waits no longer multiply (`readySeconds` 0 in the profile Run Commands, one attempt without a wait after the interim's, none for a guest a golden capture holds); the golden source's release comes first, so a source the capture left off isn't one of those guests; `deploy.ps1`'s rollout sends the relay that starts an off guest itself (rebase onto PR 16, 2026-11-04) |
| R640 | Database Hub's panel thresholds that the showcase aims at (High CPU over 80%, CPU spikes against a 48-hour baseline, databases limited by service tier) and its **Recent signal changes** aren't on Learn; they were seen in the UI on 2026-11-03, and the preview can change them | **Mitigated:** the README's table labels each one *observed* with its date and cites Learn for everything else; the owner's live check confirms or corrects them before a customer sees them (the showcase) |
| R641 | T1 and T2 may never appear in **High CPU** or **CPU spikes**: on 2026-10-23 the telemetry's `AvgCPUPercent` was 0 for both while SQL Server's CPU was at 97-99%, and the panel's source isn't documented. It's intermittent (*observed 2026-11-04*): during T2's `cpu-pressure` at intensity 8, `arcsql01`'s `AvgCPUPercent` was at most 81 from 14:36 to 14:46 UTC and at most 0 from 14:46 to 14:56, while `SQLProcessCPUPercent` stayed at 84-88; T3's `AvgCPUPercent` reached 99.9 and T1's `Processes blocked` 6 in the same run | **Open:** the README's High CPU row says "may not appear; observed intermittent" for T1 and T2 until the Hub itself is checked, and limitations says so; T3 (and T4 on its own dashboard) carry the High CPU story, and T1's and T2's CPU shows in the telemetry, the Real-Time Dashboard, and the notebook. The owner's live check during T2's CPU slot settles it (the showcase) |
| R642 | The showcase is unit-tested only: its slots, anchors, cycles, and tiers, the request stamps, and which visual each slot fills and how soon | **Open:** the owner's live check, with the README's table as the expectation; until then, limitations lists it as not proven live (the showcase) |
| R643 | Whether automatic tuning forces `plan-regression`'s plan within its 10-minute slot isn't documented: Learn gives the 10-second CPU gain and the 30-minute to 72-hour verification, not the detection time | **Open:** the README says "likely, not proven live"; the DMV shows the regression whether or not the plan is forced, and the forced plan stays after the run for the presenter. The owner's live check times it (the showcase) |
| R644 | T4's new `max_connections` takes effect only after a restart: an environment deployed before it keeps its old limit until a run that deploys the template and passes T1's T4 step (`deploy.ps1 -FromStage Infrastructure`), and the restart ends T4's open sessions | **Mitigated:** `T4.MAXCONNECTIONS` is a **Warning** while the value waits, deployment's "Update an existing environment" gives the command, the restart runs before T1 sets T4 up and the runner's connections retry, a T4 that isn't Ready isn't restarted (a **Warning**), and a deployment that changes nothing never restarts T4. Not proven live (the showcase) |
| R645 | `connection-surge`'s refused attempts, and **Failed connections** filling on every T4 size, are unit-tested only | **Open:** at most 5 refused attempts in any minute and 30 a run, the free slots held 5 seconds at most, only "too many connections" (53300) counted, and a sign-in failure stops the surge. The owner's live check on the next deploy closes it; limitations says so (the showcase) |
| R646 | An environment whose runner predates the showcase refuses `showcase` ("Unknown activity profile"), and the control notebook can't see a refusal | **Open:** deployment's "Update an existing environment" delivers the runner (`-FromStage Infrastructure`, or `-FromStage Artifacts` for the runner alone); the same holds for `--vcores` (2026-11-04). | R647 | T1's CPU slot shares `sqlvm01` with the runner that drives T3 and T4, and Phase 25d saw a saturated `sqlvm01` starve a lane | **Mitigated:** T1's CPU slot is 7 minutes late in the cycle with 2 sessions, `capacity-max` isn't in the showcase, and the profile host runs at above-normal priority; its lanes keep their bounded reads and retries. Not proven live (the showcase) |
| R648 | A Burstable T4 can run out of CPU credits: "When credits are exhausted, the VM is restricted to baseline CPU" | **Mitigated:** on Burstable, the showcase's T4 CPU slot is 6 minutes in odd cycles only; a T4 without a recorded tier runs the Burstable slots. Not proven live: West US 3 refused the Burstable sizes for the test subscription (the showcase) |
| R649 | A second Run all while a run is active is refused, so changing what runs takes **Stop now** first (the newest-wins rule is deferred to a later pull request) | **Accepted:** the refusal says "Stop now first, or wait for the run to end", the README, the notebook's guide, limitations, and ADR 0020 say so, and the runner's request stamps stay ready, unused (the showcase, PR-A) |
| R650 | T4's 4-vCore default bills more: $0.356 an hour against $0.017 for `Standard_B1ms` (a dated example, 2026-11-03), and the 7-day restart's worst case is up to $256.32 a month without the safety stop | **Accepted (user-directed, 2026-11-04):** `monthlyCeilingUsd` defaults to $375; the restart counts as about a day where the safety stop is fully in place; the docs and the monthly check give three ways to stay cheap (`t4SkuName` `Standard_B1ms`, `t4Enabled` false, a lower ceiling) (the showcase, PR-A) |

| R652 | A refusal of the scale or of `Standard_D4ds_v5` is recognized only as `SkuNotAvailable` from the deployment's operations; the zone retry, `Standard_D4ads_v5`, and the scale are unit-tested only | **Open:** the owner's live check; another error fails the template, as before (the showcase, PR-A) |
| R653 | When autonomous tuning's first recommendation shows, and whether the runner's `missing-index` lookups rank in Query Store, aren't documented or proven live | **Open:** the README says "likely in a 90-minute run on the default size (not proven live)", at least about 60 minutes after the activity starts; the walkthrough gives the portal path and the CLI (the showcase, PR-A) |
| R654 | An existing T4 database lacks `missing-index` in `demo.fn_scenario_catalog` until T1's T4 step sets T4 up again, and an older runner refuses `--vcores` | **Mitigated:** `deploy.ps1 -FromStage Infrastructure` (or a plain run after **DONE**) delivers the runner and sets T4 up again ([update an existing environment](deployment.md#update-an-existing-environment)) (the showcase, PR-A) |

| R656 | T1 missing right after a cold start from Fabric (live, 2026-11-04): the instance view's status from before the stop said Running and OK, so no restart went out, and T1 sent no telemetry for 12 minutes | **Mitigated:** the runbooks, `start-demo.ps1`, and `deploy.ps1` count only a status whose `MainServiceLastReportedTime` is later than the VM's start, wait up to 8 minutes, and restart the agent once when none comes. **Open:** how often the agent reports that time isn't documented; an agent that doesn't report it gets one restart after 8 minutes on a cold start (the showcase, PR-A) |
| R657 | The plugin's wait is 3 minutes longer (8 instead of 5), so a cold-start run's job can look after the plugin for up to about 28 minutes, and `start-demo.ps1` can wait 3 minutes more | **Accepted:** within the job's 35-minute bound and the free 500 job minutes; a fresh status ends the wait at once (the showcase, PR-A) |
| R658 | The 30-minute `DbHub-Monitor` re-checks are scheduled only while at least 5 minutes of the run remain, so a run under about 35 minutes gets none | **Accepted:** the run's own job looks after the plugin first; the 90-minute showcase gets two; the README and the notebook's guide say so (the showcase, PR-A) |

| R660 | An `off` for a VM that doesn't run isn't sent (the review of PR 20), so that target's stored profile stays as it was | **Accepted:** nothing runs on a stopped VM; a stored profile ends at its own end (a run's at the run's end plus 2 minutes, `business-day`'s after its window), `stop-demo.ps1` sets every profile off, and the profile host's leftover-run cleanup ends what a stopped run left at the next boot (the showcase, PR-A) |
| R661 | A control run says Dispatched once Azure accepts each VM's Run Command, so a profile set that then fails (the 547 of 2026-11-04 on T1 and T2) shows only in that Run Command's result, and the target keeps its earlier profile | **Open:** [#28](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/28); troubleshooting names the symptom and its fix, and the showcase hotfix removes the 547 itself |
| R662 | The nightly safety stop is unit-tested only: its schedules firing in the account's time zone, the race with the DevTest Labs auto-shutdown, a starting T4, the T3 check's verdicts, the Contributor path, `grant-safety-stop.ps1`, and `destroy.ps1`'s cleanup haven't run live | **Open:** the live proof in [ADR 0024](adr/0024-nightly-safety-stop.md#validation), after the merge (the nightly safety stop) |
| R663 | A deployer who can't create or assign roles gets the safety stop without its role: its jobs end `NotGranted`, and a restarted T4 bills until someone stops it; after each destroy and redeploy the assignment is needed again | **Mitigated:** `SAFETYSTOP.ROLE` is a **Warning** with the one command for an Owner, **DONE** and the status page say what isn't covered and what still applies, `STATUS.SAFETYSTOP` and `COST.SAFETYSTOP` repeat it, and the cost check counts the rest of the month until it's fixed (the nightly safety stop) |
| R664 | `deploy.ps1` creates a role assignment by default, against ADR 0008's default | **Accepted (user-directed, 2026-11-05):** one stop-only custom role (7 actions, no start, no Run Command, no data access) for the safety stop's own identity at the demo resource group only; `SECURITY.RBAC.ROLEASSIGNMENTS` accepts exactly that, and any other scope is a **Warning** (the nightly safety stop) |
| R665 | The role definition "DbHub Demo Safety Stop" stays in the subscription after `destroy.ps1` | **Accepted:** it has no assignment left and no cost, the next deployment reuses it, and `DESTROY.SAFETYSTOP.ROLE` gives the `az role definition delete` command (the nightly safety stop) |
| R666 | Whether `az rest` hands back Job - Get Output and Runbook - Get Content as the raw text isn't proven live | **Open, fail safe:** an unreadable output makes `STATUS.SAFETYSTOP` a **Warning** and the cost check count the rest of the month; unreadable runbook content only republishes it on every deploy. | R667 | The safety stop's job minutes: about 2 to 4 a night, up to about 12 when it waits for a starting T4; Azure Automation includes 500 a month, not on rate plans with a flat discount or a fixed monthly credit, and the control's jobs use the same allowance | **Accepted:** the estimate counts 120 minutes a month at the meter's price from the Retail Prices API at run time, $0 within the included minutes (the nightly safety stop) |
| R668 | A first deployment's preflight runs before the safety stop exists, so it counts T4's restart for the rest of the month: at list prices on 2026-11-05, `enhanced-performance` comes to $394.53 and fails its first preflight against $375, as it did before this change | **Accepted:** fail safe, no prediction; the message names the fix (a cheaper profile, `t4SkuName`, or a higher `monthlyCeilingUsd` for the first run), and later preflights count about a day per restart (the nightly safety stop) |
