| R670 | On macOS and Linux, and in a codespace, the sign-in runs in the run's own terminal, so the CLI's output interleaves with the run's lines, and in a codespace the browser sign-in may not complete | **Accepted:** Windows gets a window of its own; the status page is unaffected; an unfinished sign-in is ended at its deadline (a **Warning**), and the app can be added later from a machine with a browser (the Insights app in every deployment) |
| R671 | The sign-in runs the pinned CLI through `npx --yes`, outside the app's stage, so a first run downloads the package from the npm registry at the start of the run | **Accepted:** the same package and version the stage installs (`config/versions.json`, `fabricApp.cliVersion`); a start that fails is a **Warning** that only the app's step feels (the Insights app in every deployment) |
| R672 | The app's errors are recorded in `out/deploy.jsonl` as **Manual action required** with `reportedStatus` `Fail`, not as **Fail**, so a tool that reads only the status sees no failure | **Accepted:** that's what keeps **DONE**, `DEPLOY.SUMMARY`, and the exit codes as the owner asked; the evidence keeps the reported status, and the status page, `status.ps1 -Watch`, and the banners show **Failed** ([ADR 0012](adr/0012-validation-model.md#2026-11-05-the-insights-app-in-every-deployment)) (the Insights app in every deployment) |
| R673 | For a caller who isn't a Fabric administrator, `PREFLIGHT.FABRIC.APPSETTINGS` is a **Warning** on every run ("Couldn't read ..."), one more line among preflight's warnings | **Accepted:** it never blocks, it names both settings and the portal steps, and the app's own step proves them by creating the app and running its queries (the Insights app in every deployment) |
| R674 | The concurrent sign-in, the containment of the app's step, and the status page's **Failed** row and **Needs attention** box are unit-tested only | **Open:** the next live `deploy.ps1` ([still to verify](#still-to-verify)) (the Insights app in every deployment) |

| R681 | Arc Jumpstart can replace or remove the image at the same address, or change its published password, at any time; the program "is currently in maintenance mode" ([release notes](https://jumpstart.azure.com/release_notes), checked 2026-11-05) | **Mitigated:** a newer image is used only once it passes the image checks, with a **Warning** that names it (`STATUS.T2.IMAGE`); a changed password stops the first boot after 15 minutes with the fix (point `credentialSource` at the matching Jumpstart commit); a removed image ends **NOT DONE**, and `PaygWizard`, `ProductKey`, and `Developer` remain; nothing falls back by itself |
| R682 | A deploy in `JumpstartImage` mode needs Jumpstart's public storage and GitHub (`raw.githubusercontent.com`) at that moment, and a tenant firewall or proxy can block either | **Accepted:** an outage or a block ends **NOT DONE**, and `./scripts/deploy.ps1` again resumes the download; the prerequisites and troubleshooting name both hosts |

| R684 | The host's 128 GiB OS disk (`lowest-cost`) can't hold the image's 100 GiB maximum once the guest has grown | **Accepted** (user decision, 2026-11-05: no shrink): the space check counts the download, a 20 GiB growth allowance, and the margin; `STATUS.T2.DISKSPACE` warns under 20 GiB free, with the fix (`dataDiskSku` `E10`, or free space) |

| R686 | An existing environment whose configuration doesn't name `t2SqlInstallMode` now gets `JumpstartImage`: its `PaygWizard` golden snapshot isn't used, and its existing host refuses the new mode (`GuestSource`) | **Mitigated:** the refusal names the fix (add `"t2SqlInstallMode": "PaygWizard"`, or destroy and deploy again), and the CHANGELOG says so; a configuration that `init.ps1` wrote names its mode |

| R688 | The evaluation guard (`slmgr.vbs /rearm`, a restart, `/ato`) for the build modes' guests isn't proven live, and it runs only while the host runs | **Open:** the first build-mode guest that reaches 30 days left; with no rearm left it's a warning in the host's log and `health.json` (not a status check of its own), and `STATUS.VM_ARCHOST01` shows the days left |
| R689 | Preflight doesn't see a mode that differs from an existing host's guest; the host refuses it later in the run (`GuestSource`) | **Accepted:** preflight can't read a deallocated host cheaply; the refusal comes before any change to the guest, with the fix |
| R690 | The three 2026-11-05 changes were combined and tested together offline only: the Insights app's sign-in at the start, T2 from the Jumpstart image, and the nightly safety stop's setup after the template have never run in one live deployment | **Open,** narrowed: the three ran in one live deployment on 2026-11-06, a clean first deployment that ended **DONE** in 91 minutes; the Rayfin CLI was already signed in, so no browser opened. Left: a browser sign-in at the start and the safety stop's first evening ([still to verify](#still-to-verify)); each change keeps its own risks (R662 to R668, R669 to R674, R680 to R689) (the three changes combined; the live test of 2026-11-06) |
| R691 | Both 2026-11-06 sign-in fixes rely on what the Rayfin CLI 1.36.2 does, which its reference doesn't document: that it opens its sign-in page as `"<BROWSER>" "<url>"`, and what `rayfin login status --json` prints. A later CLI could change either | **Accepted:** `config/versions.json` pins the version, and both fail safe. If the CLI changed how it uses `BROWSER`, at worst a usable value is left out and the default browser opens. A status the module doesn't recognize is `Unknown`, a **Warning**, never a false **Pass** (the sign-in fixes) |
| R692 | The status read after a sign-in window that didn't finish holds `deploy.ps1`'s wait for the tracks for up to 90 seconds, and when it finds no sign-in, 10 seconds and up to 90 more (since the third finding; it was a minute), once a run. Before a stage exists it runs the pinned CLI through `npx --prefer-offline`, which downloads the package when npm's cache lacks it | **Accepted:** only when the window was closed, failed, or was stopped; never after Ctrl+C; the tracks run in their own processes meanwhile; the sign-in itself has just put the package in npm's cache. Measured on 2026-11-06: about 9 seconds warm, more than 30 cold through npx, and 64.8 seconds cold through the stage's CLI, live (the sign-in fixes; the third finding) |
| R693 | The Jumpstart image's download speed (2026-11-06) is measured offline only, in Windows PowerShell 5.1 on the authoring PC through a stream that returns 16 KiB per read; the live host downloaded and verified the image with it on 2026-11-06, but its rate, bound by the network and its disk, isn't recorded yet | **Closed** (measured live, 2026-11-06): the 34.1 GiB image downloaded and verified in about 11 minutes, about 51 MiB/s, bound by the host's disk (`Standard_E2s_v5` with an E10 Standard SSD) (the Jumpstart image, live; the live test of 2026-11-06) |
| R694 | Resuming the Jumpstart image's download never worked before 2026-11-06: Blob storage answered the open-ended range with the whole image, and the host started over silently. The closed range that replaces it is proven against the image from Windows PowerShell 5.1 (206) and by unit tests, but no live resume is observed yet | **Open:** still not observed after the live test of 2026-11-06. The next interrupted download on a live host: its log says "resuming at ..." and no "ignored the range request" **Warning** follows ([still to verify](#still-to-verify)) (the Jumpstart image, live) |
| R695 | The read of the CLI's sign-in starts before the subscription guard, judged from `-FromStage` and `-StopAfter` alone, so a run that turns out not to need it (a resume past the Fabric track, or a guard that fails) runs a read-only status query for nothing; through `npx`, on a machine whose npm cache lacks the pinned CLI, that includes its download | **Accepted:** the read is read-only, and the run ends it as soon as it knows; the download is the one the sign-in and the app's stage need anyway (the start's read, 2026-11-06) |
| R696 | A read that doesn't answer within 90 seconds of its start, such as a slow first download through `npx` or a token refresh that hangs, still starts a sign-in, which may not be needed. Cold reads measured 44 seconds through the stage's CLI (live) and 42 and 61 seconds through `npx` on a busy machine (2026-11-06) | **Mitigated:** the **Warning** line says why and names the command to read it yourself; signing in again is harmless, and only the app's step ever waits for it (the start's read, 2026-11-06) |
| R697 | The early read, its overlap with the subscription guard, and the "Checking" message are unit-tested only, with the process layer's start and wait run as real processes | **Open:** the next live `deploy.ps1` with the CLI signed in, cold and warm: no browser, and the log's Info line with the read's seconds ([still to verify](#still-to-verify)) (the start's read, 2026-11-06) |
| R698 | The third finding's cause is inferred, from the pinned packages' code and the run's timing, not observed: the run's read and the app step's read overlapped at the deadline, and a token renewal's in-place rewrite of the token cache can make a read find no account. A Rayfin process outside the run, in another terminal or an editor, can still overlap a read | **Mitigated:** the run's own reads no longer overlap; a read that finds no account is read again 10 seconds later; and the app's records settle the row. An overlap from outside the run can still cost a needless sign-in at the start (R696), or a **Warning** that the app's records then settle (one answer, 2026-11-06) |
| R699 | Past the sign-in's deadline, the app's step waits while the run reads the sign-in, at most 4 minutes, which can end the Fabric track, and so the run, that much later | **Accepted:** only when nobody finished the sign-in and the app's step reaches the deadline before the run has settled it, once a run; the run's read takes about 9 seconds warm, and 200 at most with its second read; the app's row says it's waiting (one answer, 2026-11-06) |
| R700 | A sign-in **Warning** becomes **Pass** on the app step's records alone: `FABRIC.APP.SIGNIN` **Pass** (the same `rayfin login status --json`) or `FABRIC.APP.DEPLOY` **Pass** (`rayfin up` with that sign-in) | **Accepted:** both prove a sign-in to the demo's tenant existed when the app's step ran, which is what the row reports; the earlier message stays in the record's evidence (`earlierStatus`, `earlierMessage`), and the log says it was settled (one answer, 2026-11-06) |
| R701 | The grace, the second read, and the settle from the app's records are unit-tested only | **Open:** the next live `deploy.ps1` whose sign-in nobody answers while the CLI is signed in ([still to verify](#still-to-verify)) (one answer, 2026-11-06) |
| R706 | The lone LSA secret's origin is inferred from two VMs on one day (Windows Server 2022 Datacenter, build 20348, West US 3): Azure's Windows provisioning or the image. Another image, region, or provisioning agent may leave it or not | **Accepted:** nothing depends on where it comes from. Wherever the secret is alone, the sweep deletes it with a note; wherever automatic sign-in is armed, it's a **Warning** or a **Fail** as before (the LSA secret Azure leaves, 2026-11-06) |
| R707 | T1 has a new step that can fail its bootstrap closed: when the LSA secret can't be deleted, or automatic sign-in can't be read, 3 times, `sqlvm01` is **Failed** and `DEPLOY.T1.READY` a **Fail** | **Accepted:** it runs as SYSTEM, with the LSA calls the host's sweep already makes on every deploy, and it's the last step, so ContosoCare, its seed, and the runner are in place by then; `deploy.ps1 -FromStage Artifacts` tries it again in a new generation (the LSA secret Azure leaves, 2026-11-06) |
| R708 | The lone secret's classification, T1's step, and the notes in `T2.ARC.ONBOARDING` and `DEPLOY.T1.READY` are unit-tested only | **Open:** the next live `deploy.ps1`, then a read-only presence check on `sqlvm01` and `archost01` ([still to verify](#still-to-verify)) (the LSA secret Azure leaves, 2026-11-06) |
| R709 | The history report's snapshot at the end of `deploy.ps1` is unit-tested only: no live run has shown its time (about 2 to 5 minutes is an estimate from runs by hand), the notebook's analyze-only run as the deploying user, the refresh right after it, or the status page's row | **Open:** the next live `deploy.ps1` ([still to verify](#still-to-verify)); its record's evidence keeps the job's status, its seconds, and the refresh's status (the history report's snapshot) |
| R710 | `configure-fabric.ps1 -RunLiveActivity`, which the snapshot's messages name for running the notebook again, sent the live-activity notebook no `RESOURCE_GROUP` (and still sends a `TELEMETRY_LOOKBACK_MINUTES` that it doesn't declare, which does nothing), so its queries kept the notebook's default, `rg-dbhub-demo`: right for the default prefix and environment, the wrong resource group for any other. Live, 2026-11-06: a clean deploy in `rg-dbhublt-demo`, then `-RunLiveActivity` 6 minutes after **DONE**: the job completed in 66 seconds and the refresh passed, but `telemetry_snapshots` held 0 rows. The same run with `RESOURCE_GROUP` `rg-dbhublt-demo` at 14:04 ET saved 1,249 | **Closed:** its step 5 sends `RESOURCE_GROUP` (`rg-<prefix>-<environment>`) since 2026-11-06, as `stop-demo.ps1 -SaveHistory` and the snapshot do (`tests/unit/DbHubDemo.Fabric.Tests.ps1`); those are the only scripts that run the notebook. Found here; it predated the snapshot (the history report's snapshot) |
| R711 | A run's `FABRIC.SEMANTICMODEL.*` records and the report's check run before the snapshot, so their **Warnings** for blank measures stay in that run's results after the snapshot has filled the report | **Accepted:** the snapshot's **Pass** message says so, and `./scripts/configure-powerbi.ps1 -CheckModelData` checks the measures again; moving the Fabric track's checks after **DONE** would lengthen every run (the history report's snapshot) |

## Phase log

One short entry per phase, in order: what it built, what was proven live, and when. Phases 0–2 (the inspection, a documentation check, and the blocking questions) produced the plan. New phases add their entry at the end.

### Phase 3: Architecture and ADRs

2026-10-19. The architecture, the automation support matrix, the draft security model, the prerequisites and permissions, and ADRs 0001–0016, including the plan's second revision and the subscription guard rule. Documentation only; the 24 facts the ADRs depend on were checked against Microsoft Learn.

### Phase 4: Scaffolding

2026-10-19. The shared PowerShell module `DbHubDemo` (the redacting logger, the six-status result model, the configuration loader, and the subscription guard), the configuration schema, the static validation and CI, the .NET workload generator's skeleton, the devcontainer, and the repository's templates. No live work.

### Phase 5: Foundation infrastructure

2026-10-19 and 2026-10-20. Subscription-scope Bicep for the resource group, the network and its NSGs, and the budget; `preflight.ps1`, with a read-only Azure Policy inventory and a what-if; and `estimate-cost.ps1` on the Retail Prices API. Live: the foundation deployed in about a minute and converged on a redeploy. The policy inventory showed that the tenant's policies force storage accounts to turn off shared-key and public network access, so the planned artifact cache was dropped, and the bootstrap bundle travels in Run Command chunks instead. A policy that turns off public network access on SQL servers makes `networkMode` `auto` resolve to private. The $250 hard monthly ceiling was set here.

### Phase 6: T1 VM, T2 nested host and guest, SQL

2026-10-19 and 2026-10-20. T1 `sqlvm01` with SQL Server 2022 Standard pay-as-you-go; the T2 host `archost01` with its nested guest `arcsql01`, built from pinned evaluation media; the deterministic `ContosoCare` database; and bootstrap delivery in SHA-256-checked Run Command chunks, with no storage account. Live (2026-10-20): both VMs deployed in about 6.5 minutes, the chunks arrived, and T1 was **Ready** with `ContosoCare`. Two defects were found and fixed live: the bootstrap folder's permissions, and the guest image's boot files, which need `bcdboot /offline` on an updated host.

### Phase 7: T2 Arc onboarding, SQL Server enabled by Azure Arc, and T1 SQL VM monitoring

2026-10-20 and 2026-10-21. `configure-arc.ps1` onboards the guest with the caller's short-lived token as a protected parameter and converges the Azure extension for SQL Server with pay-as-you-go and monitoring; `configure-monitoring.ps1` turns on T1's SQL VM monitoring. Live: T1's telemetry about 2 minutes after its flag was set, and Arc **Connected** on 2026-10-20, with IMDS and the wire server unreachable from the guest. The setup-wizard step ran on 2026-10-21; its sign-in needed the WebView2 runtime in the guest, which a host step now installs. The SQL Server extension then took about 30 minutes to arrive (R72), and T2's telemetry came about 7 minutes after monitoring was on. Two more steps came out of that session: T1's bootstrap removes the image's Analysis Services and Integration Services, and a cleanup step removes the sign-in data that setup can leave on the VM (R74).

### Phase 8: Azure SQL Database (T3)

2026-10-20. T3: the General Purpose serverless `ContosoCare` database, Microsoft Entra-only with the P6 group as its administrator, private when policy requires it, on the free offer when it's available, and configured from T1 through its managed identity. Live (2026-10-20): deployed in about 3 minutes; T1 ran T3's 36 setup batches in 9 seconds over the private endpoint; and every T3 check passed, with the free-offer fallback as the expected **Warning**.

### Phase 9: Database Hub prerequisites and monitoring validation

2026-10-20. The Hub checks: Estate through Azure Resource Graph, telemetry for each target through the documented telemetry endpoint, T3's readiness gate, and presenter access. Live (2026-10-20): `HUB.ESTATE.T1` and `.T3` and `TELEMETRY.T1` and `.T3` **Pass**, with T3's first telemetry 2.8 minutes after its creation; T2 followed once it was onboarded.

### Phase 10: Agent skills (prompt pack and install guide)

2026-10-20. The Database Agent prompt pack, with an in-Hub and a skills prompt for each scenario and a fallback ladder, and the install guide for the `microsoft-sql-fdh` and `microsoft-sql` plugins, with a schema and tests for the prompts. Nothing can run the agent from code, so its live coverage record waits for a rehearsal.

### Phase 11: Scenarios and workload generator

2026-10-20. The `ContosoCare.Workload` runner; bounded scenario procedures with stop flags, and a reset that touches only `ContosoCare.Demo/` sessions; the healthy baseline and seven incidents; and the activity profiles. Tested against a local SQL Server, and run live in Phase 20.

### Phase 12: Fabric notebooks

2026-10-20 and 2026-10-21. `NB_DatabaseHub_Deployment`, the validator, and `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity`, the live analysis, with their import through the Fabric REST API. Live (2026-10-21): a paused capacity blocked the first import, which now reports **Manual action required**. Once the capacity ran, both notebooks were imported and read back, and the deployment notebook ran read-only in 57 seconds; the telemetry endpoint accepted the notebook's own token (R29).

### Phase 13: One-click deployment, GitHub Actions, and the workload runner on the VMs

2026-10-20 and 2026-10-21. Milestone A installed the workload runner on the VMs. Milestone B added `deploy.ps1` with resumable stages; the lifecycle scripts (start, stop, status, reset, destroy, and demo-control); on-demand Bastion; the Key Vault opt-in; and the GitHub workflows with OpenID Connect. The guard update made every script independent of the Azure CLI's default subscription. Live: the runner ran the baseline on T1 and T3 until two minutes before the nightly auto-shutdown, and T3 auto-paused 15 minutes later (2026-10-20); the guard passed with another tenant's subscription as the default (2026-10-21).

### Phase 14: Validation and security

2026-10-21. `validate.ps1`, the live check catalog in eleven areas; the secret scanner, with UTF-16 logs; the gitleaks configuration; the Windows PowerShell 5.1 analyzer rules for the VM-side scripts; the final threat model; and the role matrix. Live (2026-10-21, read-only, T1 and T3): 73 checks, none **Fail**: 44 **Pass**, 18 **Warning** (mostly for a deallocated VM), 2 **Manual action required**, and 9 **Not selected**.

### Phase 15: Demo walkthrough

2026-10-20 and 2026-10-21. The 15- and 30-minute presenter scripts, each step with its time, narration, expected signal, fallback, and reset, and a test that checks them. The rehearsal is still to come.

### Phase 16: Cleanup testing

2026-10-21 and 2026-10-22. `destroy.ps1`'s manifest, the deletion of the deployment record and of the group's budget, and the residual check, with opt-in teardown tests and a deployment-stack trial. Live, in a disposable test environment: a fresh sequential build (T1 ready at +58.5 minutes, T3 at +62.9, and T2 after two runs), which found and fixed a race in the T2 host's DNS forwarder; the guard's retry after a Continuous Access Evaluation challenge; a stop in 4.2 minutes; and a destroy after which the residual check, and a subscription snapshot compared with the one before deploy, found nothing of the environment left. The cycle came to about $4.50 at list price ([cleanup](cleanup.md#tested-results)).

### Phase 17: Final docs and report

2026-10-21. Troubleshooting, the official sources, the FAQ, limitations, cost management, the automation matrix's final pass, and this report's first summary.

### Phase 18: The Real-Time Dashboard as code

2026-10-21. `RTD_DatabaseHub_LiveView`, a KQL dashboard defined as code over the telemetry endpoint, which queries as each viewer; its import and read-back; a check of each tile's data; the Hub's monitor-template wizard as a walkthrough moment; the presentation docs; and viewer licensing (P11). Live (2026-10-21): imported in 35 seconds, read back identical in content, and 14 of 14 tiles returned data.

### Phase 19: The Power BI history report and its semantic model

2026-10-21 and 2026-10-22. The live-activity notebook's report tables; `SM_DatabaseHub_History`, a Direct Lake model with 103 measures, as TMDL; `RPT_DatabaseHub_History`, four pages as PBIR from a deterministic generator; and `configure-powerbi.ps1`, which deploys, refreshes, and checks every measure with DAX. Live (2026-10-22): deployed in 265 seconds, with 98 of the 103 measures returning data (the other five need a scenario run), and the four pages rendered in Power BI Desktop. The org app that was planned was dropped in favor of the Fabric App.

### Phase 19b: The Insights app (a Fabric App over the history model)

2026-10-22. `APP_DatabaseHub_Insights`, a read-only Fabric App (preview) built with the Rayfin SDK: four pages, custom visuals, DAX and CSV export on every visual, and ready-made prompts for the Database Agent, deployed by `configure-fabric-app.ps1`. Live (2026-10-22): `rayfin up` updated the item in place in 47 seconds, every query in its catalog returned rows, and the app loaded and connected when opened from its workspace item. Opened from its hosted URL, the preview's sign-in hand-off fails.

### Phase 20: Fast redeploy (parallel tracks and a T2 golden snapshot)

2026-10-21 and 2026-10-22. `deploy.ps1` runs T1 with T3, T2, and Fabric as parallel tracks, and restores T2 from a golden snapshot of the finished host, captured once after the setup wizard and proven in quarantine first ([ADR 0018](adr/0018-fast-redeploy-parallel-tracks-golden-snapshot.md)). Live (2026-10-22), in steps G1 to G11: the snapshot was captured (G1) and proven in quarantine in 75 minutes (G3); the demo environment was destroyed in 18.4 minutes (G5) and redeployed in 38.8 with no manual step (G6); validation ran in full (G7); all eight scenarios and 11 ten-minute incidents ran across T1, T2, and T3, with every reset **Pass** (G8); second deploys converged in 25.9 minutes with changed code and 21.8 with the same code (G9); and a stop and a timed start took about 6 minutes and 481 seconds (G10). The defects found live were fixed, among them the explicit Arc deletes, T1's inventory and monitoring plugin, and a Fabric track that stopped at once. The 30-minute target wasn't met ([deployment](deployment.md#parallel-tracks-phase-20)).

### Phase 21: The README front door

2026-10-22. The README: what's deployed; every step, automated or manual, with why, how long, and who; the prerequisites and how each is checked; redacted screenshots and command output; a quick start with measured times; and the presentation surfaces.

### Phase 22: Faster redeploy (the follow-up overlaps) and one timed destroy and redeploy

2026-10-23. The overlaps: the tracks' guards run while the template deploys, T2's restore starts as soon as its host exists and sends its artifacts during the Release, T1's bundle and runner go in one wave with its monitoring set up beside its bootstrap, and T3 is set up first. Live (2026-10-23): a destroy in 1,191 seconds, then a redeploy in 63.7 minutes with no check **Fail**. T1 and T3 were ready at +26.8 minutes, 9.5 minutes sooner, but T2 only at +61.7, because the SQL Server extension's download in its guest took about 29.5 minutes (R72). A second deploy converged in 19.2 minutes. The golden Release's identity reset was fixed and proven live ([deployment](deployment.md#faster-redeploy-phase-22)).

### Phase 24: T4, an Azure Database for PostgreSQL flexible server

Built 2026-10-23, live 2026-10-26. T4 `pgflex01-<id>`: PostgreSQL 18 with the `contosocare` database, Microsoft Entra only, virtual network integration, and its own bounded scenarios; a T4 track in the deploy; start and stop with the demo; and status, validation, and cost ([ADR 0019](adr/0019-t4-postgresql-flexible-server.md)). Live (2026-10-26): West US 3 refused `Standard_B1ms` and `Standard_B2s` (R441), so T4 runs on `Standard_D2ds_v5`. The deploy that created it took 40.3 minutes, a second deploy 20.1, and a stop, a start, and a stop 6.7, 7.9, and 6.2 minutes. T1's identity signed in to T4 as the P6 group's role, and T4's scenarios ran. A redeploy also met VM disks that the subscription's automation had switched while the VMs were deallocated; `deploy.ps1` now keeps them, with a **Warning** (R440).

### Phase 25b: Diverse SQL incident scenarios, and Phase 25a's findings

2026-10-23 and 2026-10-26. Eight more bounded scenarios, one per wait family (`blocking-chain`, `deadlock-storm`, `tempdb-contention`, `memory-grant-pressure`, `parallel-scans`, `log-flush-waits`, `slow-consumer`, and `capacity-max`), with their prompts and notebook rules. Also the fixes for what Phase 25a's long incident program found on 2026-10-23: a supervised profile host with a heartbeat, retries for refused starts, T1's and T2's CPU from `SQLProcessCPUPercent` (`AvgCPUPercent` is 0 for SQL Server in this preview), and the Insights app check's time range; and, from Phase 24, the start's wait for T1's monitoring plugin (R442). Tested statically and against a local SQL Server; not yet run live.

### Phase 25c: The on-demand activity control

2026-10-26. The optional control: a Fabric notebook sends one bounded request to an Azure Automation webhook, whose runbook checks it against the scenario catalog, creates the run's auto-stop first, and starts the targets; **Stop now**, the auto-stop, and a daily T4 safety stop end it. `setup-activity-control.ps1` sets it up with Contributor, and an Owner grants its one custom role once; its vault is public or private, depending on the tenant's policy ([ADR 0020](adr/0020-on-demand-activity-control.md)). Unit-tested; not yet proven live.

### Phase 26a: Fabric resources by name

2026-10-27. The repository stores no Fabric ID ([ADR 0021](adr/0021-fabric-resources-by-name.md)). The configuration names the workspace, the lakehouse, and the capacity; the scripts resolve their IDs at run time through documented Fabric REST calls, and create a missing workspace and lakehouse while `fabric.createIfMissing` allows it, never changing a capacity. Every consumer uses the resolver: the Fabric scripts and the deploy's Fabric track, preflight's P5, `status.ps1`, `validate.ps1`, the activity control's setup, and the notebook runs. The notebooks carry no IDs: the import binds the resolved ones, and a first deploy imports missing notebooks without asking. Live (2026-10-27): the lookup by name, read-only, then the create path end to end in a test workspace (Phase 26g).

### Phase 26b: First-run automation

2026-10-27. A first run in another tenant takes `az login`, `./scripts/init.ps1 -CreateAdminGroup`, `./scripts/preflight.ps1`, and `./scripts/deploy.ps1` ([ADR 0023](adr/0023-first-run-automation.md)). `init.ps1` writes the Git-ignored configuration from your sign-in and asks only when there's a choice, and `-CreateAdminGroup` creates the P6 group with you as its owner where the tenant allows it. `configure-fabric-app.ps1 -SignIn` runs the Rayfin CLI's one-time sign-in; the opt-in `fabric.manageCapacity` resumes a paused capacity at start and pauses only what it resumed, for a capacity that's yours alone; `stop-demo.ps1 -SaveHistory` runs the live-activity notebook after the VMs stop, to save the session's history; and a dev container, also for GitHub Codespaces, carries every tool. Live (2026-10-27): `init.ps1` ran read-only against a live subscription; the first run end to end is still to be proven.

### Phase 26c: A public repository: fork-safe workflows, masked Actions logs, and the GitHub setup

2026-10-27. What a public repository needs ([ADR 0022](adr/0022-public-repository-and-github-actions-hardening.md)): workflows that are safe with forks (no `pull_request_target`, read-only permissions by default, and every workflow that reaches Azure run by hand or on a schedule, in a GitHub environment); the identifiers kept as environment secrets and masked in Actions logs, job summaries, and result files; `setup-github-oidc.ps1 -ConfigureGitHub`, which creates the environment with you as its required reviewer and sets its secrets, with an opt-in schedule environment; `setup-github-repo.ps1`, which applies a ruleset on the default branch, secret scanning with push protection, private vulnerability reporting, and Dependabot alerts; internal names in code and test fixtures replaced by the facts they stood for; and the community files and the README's "Getting help". Built and unit-tested; the GitHub setup scripts haven't run against a public repository yet.

### Phase 26d: No operator token left anywhere

2026-10-27. Nothing from the operator's sign-in stays on the VMs, in the T2 guest, in their logs, in Run Command resources, or in snapshots ([ADR 0009](adr/0009-secrets-handling.md#phase-26d-amendment-no-operator-token-left-anywhere-2026-10-27), [ADR 0005](adr/0005-arc-onboarding-authentication.md#amendment-the-host-identity-whenever-its-attached-phase-26d-2026-10-27)). A sweep finds token-shaped values on each VM and in the guest, reporting counts and locations only, and removes them after the setup wizard, after every Arc onboarding, before a golden capture, and in a copy of the snapshot; `check-sign-in-data.ps1` runs it on demand, and a result fails closed while anything is left. Arc onboarding uses the host's identity whenever it's attached, and otherwise hands the caller's token to the guest's agent in a protected file that's zeroed and deleted right after, with the command line only as a fallback that reports a **Warning**. `capture-t2-golden.ps1 -WipeFreeSpace` zeroes the guest's free space before a capture. The docs describe the sign-in data in neutral words, and a test keeps them that way. Live (2026-10-27): a read-only audit of the running VMs found sign-in data from an earlier onboarding in a host log, which the automatic flow now clears; the host-identity onboarding and the free-space wipe are proven with the next golden capture.

### Phase 26e: Public docs

2026-10-27. This report became a concise public summary of the build, with the risk register and this log. The docs, the README, and the changelog were generalized for the public repository: phase names instead of pull request numbers, the tenant's policies and governance automation without their internal names, no operator-specific details, and links that point to the public repository. No file names the archive repository any more: the JSON schemas' `$id` URLs and the Real-Time Dashboard's name-based element IDs follow the public repository's URL, and a conventions test keeps the old name out. The neutral-wording test now covers this report too.

### Phase 26f: Protected values in the host's PowerShell log

2026-10-27. Run Command hands protected parameters to a script as arguments, so the Windows PowerShell log recorded the one-time host password and, in `ProductKey` mode, the SQL Server product key (R528). The Run Commands that receive them now turn off their session's further engine events and, after use, search that log for the exact value in memory and clear it only when it's there: a **Warning**, because endpoint protection may notice, or **Manual action required** with the command when it can't be cleared. The Security log is never cleared. The sign-in data sweep also finds product-key-shaped values, ignoring placeholders and masked keys, so `-Clean` removes older entries ([ADR 0009](adr/0009-secrets-handling.md#phase-26d-amendment-no-operator-token-left-anywhere-2026-10-27)). Proven on Windows PowerShell 5.1 with a test value: turning the events off drops the session's closing event, and the exact search finds the other seven. The password's Run Command runs live with the next golden capture.

### Phase 26g: The create path, live

2026-10-27. A new user's Fabric side, proven from nothing, by name, with no ID in the configuration, in a uniquely named test workspace on the demo's capacity. `init.ps1 -NonInteractive` wrote the configuration: names only, `fabric.importNotebooks` true, and `fabric.manageCapacity` false. Preflight's P5 was a **Warning**, because the workspace didn't exist yet. Then the deploy's Fabric track ran. `configure-fabric.ps1` created the workspace on the capacity and the lakehouse with schemas, imported both notebooks without asking and the Real-Time Dashboard, and ran the live-activity notebook once, in 3.8 minutes. `configure-powerbi.ps1` created the model and the report, and `configure-fabric-app.ps1` deployed the Insights app in 4.8 minutes. One defect: the model's first refresh failed with 0xC14700DF, because Direct Lake didn't see two of the five new tables for about 20 minutes, and the same refresh passed later, unchanged. The refresh is now sent again every 2 minutes for up to 30 minutes while that error lasts (R530). The data check and the app's checks passed, apart from the **Warnings** for an empty history and the known **Manual action required** steps: the browser check, and a tenant setting only an administrator can read. The test workspace was deleted afterward with Delete Workspace.

### Phase 25d: Phases 25b and 25c, live

2026-10-27. The live proof of Phase 25b's eight scenarios and Phase 25c's on-demand activity control, with fixes for what it found. A converging deploy first failed the runner's setup on T1 and T2, because it looked for one procedure name per scenario, and three of the new scenarios name others; it now checks each scenario's own. Each new scenario then ran for 8 minutes on T1, T2, and T3 at once, and the telemetry showed the waits it's designed for: lock waits with 124 and 140 deadlocks in T1's and T2's windows, blocking chains of up to 9 sessions with lock escalation, tempdb page latches from about 120,000 temporary tables on T1 and on T2, memory-grant queues, parallelism, log flushes, network waits, and CPU at 97 to 100 %. On T3, at 1 vCore, the parallel reports ran serially, as designed. The profile host waited for a run still ending instead of refusing a new one, and ended the runs an earlier host had left Running. The live-activity notebook found CPU windows on T1 and T2 for the first time, among 46 incident windows, and every query of the Insights app returned rows. The control's setup chose the private path, the owner's grant created its role and three assignments, and notebook requests started the targets and their scenarios; a second request was refused while one was active, and **Stop now**, the run's auto-stop, and `start-demo.ps1`'s takeover each worked. Fixed along the way: the runbooks' T4 start and stop (R480), the safety stop's link on a second setup (R481), retries for T4's refused starts (R482) and for connections under `capacity-max` (R483), the deploy's T1 plugin restart and wait for T2's host (R485), a notebook drivable by the job API, and lookups that the Windows command line couldn't pass.

### Phase 27: Dependency and security updates

2026-10-28. The repository's open Dependabot alerts and pull requests, one pull request per concern. The Insights app had nine alerts. Vite 8.3.5 (from 8.0.16) and Vitest 4.1.12 (from 4.1.11) close the seven in Vite and `@vitest/mocker`. An npm override moves zod from 4.3.6 to 4.6.7, because even the newest Rayfin packages declare `~4.3.6`, and a test runs Rayfin's own zod code with it. `@azure/msal-node`'s alert stays open (R540). `npm audit` now reports only that advisory, and nothing for the production dependencies. The browser bundle has no zod, but the new Vite builds it, so the next deploy ships a rebuilt bundle. The CodeQL action moved to v4.39.1, recorded in `config/versions.json`, and the workload generator to Azure.Identity 1.21.0, System.CommandLine 2.0.14, and JsonSchema.Net 9.5.0, with lock files that a locked restore accepts: Dependabot's own pull requests had failed on exactly those two points. Static only: the app's redeploy waits for the Fabric capacity.

### Phase 26d: The golden recapture, live

2026-10-28. The T2 golden snapshot, captured again with Phase 26d's sweep, free-space wipe, and host identity, and Phase 26f's log clearing, from the public repository's code. First a read-only audit (9.4 minutes): T1 passed, 1,572 files, its four setup log archives opened and clean; the T2 host's 834 files were clean, but its Windows PowerShell log held 9 tokens from the caller-token onboarding of 2026-10-23; in the guest, one of two setup log archives held sign-in data (one token); no Security log held any. `check-sign-in-data.ps1 -Clean` (5.8 minutes, a **Warning** as designed) cleared that log and deleted the archive, and its scan found nothing left on the host (838 files) or in the guest (585). The capture (37.8 minutes) passed every gate. The host password's Run Command found its value seven times in the Windows PowerShell log and cleared it (Phase 26f's **Warning**, live). The guest's free space was zero-filled, 38.2 GiB in 174 seconds without growing its dynamic disk, and trimmed. With the guest off, its disk was compacted from 28.5 to 26.8 GB, and the host's free space was zero-filled (76.0 GiB) and trimmed. The incremental snapshot, about 48 GiB of data with no network access, took 17 seconds. Its proof (53.2 minutes) passed the boot, the air gap, isolation (none of 8 endpoints answered), the identity reset, SQL Server Standard, the guest password, the sign-in check, and a fresh onboarding with the host identity in 20 minutes (`tokenSource` HostIdentity, `connectAttempts` 1, `authorizationWaitSeconds` 0, the token handed over in a file) with PAYG and monitoring, and the teardown left nothing. One false alarm failed it: `GOLDEN.TEST.SOURCE` counted two Resource Health events about the source's Arc machine as operations, although the machine read Disconnected with the same last status change, machine ID, and agent version before and after. The check now counts only Administrative entries as operations, lists the other events in its evidence, and fails on any status change of a source that was already Disconnected. The owner accepted the proof without a rerun, because every other check passed and the source's properties didn't change, and deleted the older snapshot the same day, once the new one was boot- and onboard-verified: the golden group now holds only the new one, and the live host's OS disk, created from the older one, doesn't depend on it. The demo's configuration now sets `assignRoles: true`, so redeploys onboard with the host identity: the next deploy creates `id-<prefix>-<env>-arc-onboard` with Azure Connected Machine Onboarding on the demo's group. The proof's one connect attempt and zero seconds of authorization wait mean it adds no time to a redeploy; the bounded retry allows up to 7.5 minutes only while a new role assignment propagates. The day cost about $3 at list price.

### Phase 28: One command, one guided stop

2026-10-29. Two colleagues' first runs took more than 2 hours and never finished; a third run sat on seven chunk Run Commands stuck in `Deleting`, and listed the optional Insights app's prerequisites as if they blocked. The owner's decisions: "I want the build to run and not give a template that is reliant on me. This whole thing should be one click independent for anyone", T2 stays `PaygWizard` as a guided wizard with one prompted stop, `ProductKey` and `Developer` stay opt-in, and nothing may depend on the owner's resources ([ADR 0018](adr/0018-fast-redeploy-parallel-tracks-golden-snapshot.md#amendments-2026-10-29-phase-28-one-command-one-guided-stop), [ADR 0006](adr/0006-sql-editions-and-licensing.md#amendment-one-command-one-guided-stop-phase-28-2026-10-29)).

**Work summary.**

- **One command after `az login`:** `deploy.ps1` writes the configuration on a first run (`Initialize-DemoFirstRun`, as `init.ps1 -CreateAdminGroup`), keeps the PC awake for the whole run, waits for T2 by default in every install mode (`-T2WaitMinutes` 180; `-NoWaitForT2` opts out; CI and GitHub Actions keep today's behavior), continues an unfinished deployment when run again with no parameters, and ends with one banner, **DONE** or **NOT DONE**, with an exit code that's 0 only when **DONE**.
- **The guided stop (`PaygWizard`):** the host's request with a time limit, on-demand Bastion, Arc onboarding first, then the host prepares SQL Server setup pre-filled from a configuration file, a logon task in the guest, Virtual Machine Connection at the operator's sign-in to the host, and one automatic sign-in of the guest (the password only as the LSA `DefaultPassword` secret, cleaned up in a `finally`, rotated, and fail closed in the sign-in scan, owner-approved; [ADR 0009](adr/0009-secrets-handling.md#phase-28-amendment-the-guests-one-automatic-sign-in-and-the-portal-password-reset-2026-10-29)). One prompt: a banner, a beep, the console window flashing, the portal page in the browser, the time limit, reminders every 5 minutes, and `HUMAN ACTION REQUIRED: DEPLOY.T2.GUIDEDSTOP` for an assistant to relay. The host password comes by clipboard or the Key Vault opt-in, or, with neither, from the person through the portal's **Reset password**, recorded and rotated afterward. The host checks the result (Standard, the default instance, the Azure extension) and shows the documented fix for a wrong install; then the run finishes T2 by itself.
- **The live status page:** one progress model written atomically by `deploy.ps1` and every track, a local page regenerated at least every 15 seconds (meta refresh, no JavaScript, HTML-encoded and redacted), one banner in four states, every step per track with its time and message, stale and Azure-side waits marked; `status.ps1 -Watch` shows the same in any console.
- **What DONE requires:** Database Hub's own views of every target (`HUB.ESTATE.T1`–`T4`, `TELEMETRY.T1`–`T3`, `METRICS.T4`), T2 at Standard with PAYG and monitoring in `PaygWizard` and `ProductKey` modes, T1's inventory, and the required Fabric items (`FABRIC.ITEMS.REQUIRED`); T4 off and `Developer` mode are said plainly, and the optional Insights app never blocks **DONE**. ADR 0018's clock includes T4.
- **Off the critical path, and no dead ends:** with `networkMode` `private`, the policy inventory runs beside the deployment; T4 falls back from B1ms to B2s, then to no T4, when its size isn't set ([ADR 0019](adr/0019-t4-postgresql-flexible-server.md#2026-10-29-the-burstable-fallback-phase-28)); chunk deletes aren't waited for, extensions settle first, a watchdog restarts a stuck VM once, and a stall goes sequential ([ADR 0003](adr/0003-t2-host-size-security-type-bootstrap.md#phase-28-amendment-chunk-hygiene-that-doesnt-stall-2026-10-29)).
- **Help when it doesn't finish:** `collect-diagnostics.ps1` writes a redacted, secret-scanned zip; the **Deployment problem** form asks for it. The README's "Deploy it, step by step" and "Running it with an AI assistant", and the docs and ADRs listed in the changelog.

**Changed files.** New: `scripts/collect-diagnostics.ps1`; the module's `Private/DeployExperience.ps1`, `DiagnosticBundleHelpers.ps1`, `GuidedStopHelpers.ps1`, `ProgressHelpers.ps1`, and `VmExtensionHelpers.ps1`, and `Public/Complete-DemoDeploymentRun.ps1`, `Export-DemoDiagnosticBundle.ps1`, `Get-DemoRunEnvironment.ps1`, `Initialize-DemoFirstRun.ps1`, `Show-DemoDeploymentProgress.ps1`, `Start-DemoDeploymentRun.ps1`, and `Write-DemoDeploymentOutcome.ps1`; `vm/hyperv-guest/GuestSetup.ps1` and `vm/bootstrap/Set-DemoGuidedSetupRequest.ps1`; and the tests `DbHubDemo.OneCommand.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Progress.Tests.ps1`, and `Vm.GuidedSetup.Tests.ps1`. Changed: `scripts/deploy.ps1`, `init.ps1`, `start-demo.ps1`, and `status.ps1`; the module's manifest, loader, `ParallelDeployment.ps1`, `TrackHelpers.ps1`, `RunCommandHelpers.ps1`, `DeployHelpers.ps1`, `T4Helpers.ps1`, `SignInDataHelpers.ps1`, and `ValidationAreas.ps1`, and `Invoke-DemoPreflight`, `Invoke-DemoTemplateDeployment`, `Invoke-DemoDeployment`, `Invoke-DemoArcConfiguration`, `Invoke-DemoVmRunCommand`, `Initialize-DemoConfig`, `New-DemoBastion`, `Stop-DemoEnvironment`, `Get-DemoEnvironmentStatus`, `Get-DemoDeploymentState`, `Set-DemoDeploymentState`, `Get-DemoVmHealth`, and, for the deployment record's name, `Get-DemoTeardownManifest`, `Remove-DemoEnvironment`, and `Test-DemoTeardownResidue`; `vm/bootstrap/Invoke-HostBootstrap.ps1`, `HostSteps.ps1`, `HostPlatform.ps1`, and `Invoke-DemoSignInResidue.ps1`, `vm/hyperv-guest/GuestSql.ps1` and `GuestVm.ps1`, and `vm/health/Get-DemoHealth.ps1`; `config/environment.schema.json` (`t4SkuName` without a default); the **Deployment problem** issue form; the tests for the tracks, the bundle, the lifecycle, preflight, T4, the host plan, the sign-in sweep, the lifecycle scripts, and the conventions; and the README, CHANGELOG, this report, deployment, troubleshooting, the security model, limitations, the FAQ, architecture, prerequisites, cost management, cleanup, references, the automation support matrix, the VM READMEs, and ADRs 0002, 0003, 0006, 0009, 0011, 0012, 0018, 0019, and 0023 with the ADR index.

**Validation.** Static, 2026-10-29: `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check PSScriptAnalyzer, Pester, Markdown`, with strict mode as in CI: PSScriptAnalyzer **Pass** (no findings in 322 files), Pester **Pass** (2,674 tests passed, none failed, and 7 skipped by their own conditions, such as gitleaks not being installed on the machine), and markdownlint **Pass** (no issues in 128 files). The new behavior has its own tests: the first run from `deploy.ps1`, the wait by default, auto-resume, the keep-awake, the banners and exit codes, the guided stop's prompt, credentials, reminders, time-out, and continuation, the guest's automatic sign-in and its cleanups, the setup configuration file and the setup check, the progress model, its page, its banner choice, its HTML encoding, and its stale detection, the policy inventory's deferral, T4's fallback, what DONE requires, the Run Command watchdog, the diagnostics bundle, and the deployment record's prefix. Bicep, PSRule, the .NET tests, and the Fabric App weren't changed and weren't run locally; CI runs them. The public push scan of `origin/main..HEAD` found 0 hits. Live: **pending go-ahead**: the proof from scratch, with a person at the guided stop.

**Decided differently from the brief, and why.** A session that can't take keyboard input gets the notice and the run goes on, because an assistant can relay the guided stop to a person; the brief's "before spending anything" is met by printing it before preflight. The guest's cleanup signs the console out first, before it deletes the Winlogon values, because Windows writes `DefaultUserName` at a sign-in. `status.ps1` stays read-only: it reports the guest's automatic sign-in leftovers as a **Warning**, and the host's next pass removes them. The host password's rotation failure is a **Warning** everywhere, with the portal's way, and the record stays, so a later stop or start tries again. `t4SkuName` lost its schema default, so an unset size can fall back while a size set on purpose never changes. `-Sequential` has no guided stop. GitHub Actions keeps its exit codes and inputs, and doesn't auto-resume. The person-needed time stays "about 60 to 90 minutes after the start", the brief's own estimate, labeled as one.

**Risks.** R25, R49, R336, and R441 (updated), and R560–R585. Found while preparing the proof and fixed: the template's subscription-scope deployment record was named `dbhub-<environment>-main` whatever the prefix, so a throwaway environment with its own prefix and the same environment name would have replaced, read, and, at its destroy, deleted the owner's record; it's now `<prefix>-<environment>-main`, the same name with the default prefix. Nothing ran live: the live proof from scratch needs a person at the guided stop, a throwaway resource group with its own prefix, and a temporary Fabric workspace, and removes everything afterward.

**Review fixes (2026-10-29, the same pull request).** An independent review of the pull request found seven issues, and setting up the live proof found an eighth. Each fix came with a Pester test that reproduced it first:

1. A golden redeploy in `PaygWizard` mode skipped the configuration of SQL Server enabled by Azure Arc and ended **NOT DONE**: the wait for the host's setup check ignored `-Force` and stopped a copy settled at `ArcOnboardingBeforeWizard` after its Release. It now honors `-Force` and lets a guest that already runs Standard, with nothing armed, through.
2. With `-NoStatusPage`, or a status page that couldn't be set up, nobody was prompted at the guided stop: the T2 track's process left the prompt to `deploy.ps1`'s, which prompts only from the progress model. Without a model, the T2 track's process now prompts the person itself, and the handoff line points to the console.
3. `Test-DemoClipboard` was true on Linux, in Codespaces, in Cloud Shell, and over SSH, where PowerShell's `Set-Clipboard` keeps the text in its own process without failing, so the portal's **Reset password** was never offered. A clipboard now counts only on Windows when `Set-Clipboard` takes text, on macOS with `pbcopy`, and on Linux with an X display and `xclip`; never in Codespaces, an SSH session, or Cloud Shell. `start-demo.ps1 -Interactive -ResetAdminPassword` uses the same check.
4. `deploy.ps1 -Sequential` could never be **DONE**: it records no tracks and never checked the Fabric items. It now runs DONE's read-only checks after its validation and is judged by its recorded stage.
5. A recorded "no T4" (`DbHubT4Sku` `None`) outlived a `t4SkuName` set afterward, so a broken T4 never blocked **DONE**. Whether T4 is off is now decided from the effective configuration, where a size the configuration sets wins, and a deployment with T4 in it clears the record.
6. The watchdog could restart `archost01` during the golden Release, or while its guest build ran after `-FromStage Artifacts` or `Bootstraps`. The artifacts sent during the Release now go without the extension wait or the watchdog, and the host is never restarted while a golden guest action runs on it or its guest build may be running (judged from the deployment state since the follow-up below).
7. Deferring the policy inventory with `networkMode` `public` removed the early stop for a Modify policy that turns SQL public access off, which the what-if doesn't see. Only `private` defers it now.
8. A relative `-ConfigPath` or `-OutputPath` resolved against the process's working directory, which `Set-Location` and `Push-Location` don't change: `init.ps1 -ConfigPath ./config/proof.local.json` after a `Push-Location` wrote the file elsewhere. Every script now resolves its paths against PowerShell's location first (`Resolve-DemoUserPath`), and so does the module wherever it made such a path absolute.

Changed files: every script in `scripts/` (for 8; also `deploy.ps1` for 4 and 7, `start-demo.ps1` for 3, and `configure-arc.ps1` for 1); the module's manifest and loader, `DeployExperience.ps1`, `FirstRunHelpers.ps1`, `GoldenHelpers.ps1`, `GuidedStopHelpers.ps1`, `ParallelDeployment.ps1`, `ProgressHelpers.ps1`, `TrackHelpers.ps1`, and `VmExtensionHelpers.ps1`, the new `Public/Resolve-DemoUserPath.ps1`, and `Complete-DemoDeploymentRun`, `Export-DemoDiagnosticBundle`, `Export-DemoRealTimeDashboard`, `Get-DemoFabricDashboardDefinition`, `Get-DemoFabricReportDefinition`, `Get-DemoFabricSemanticModelDefinition`, `Initialize-DemoConfig`, `Initialize-DemoFirstRun`, `Invoke-DemoArcConfiguration`, `Invoke-DemoDeployment`, `Invoke-DemoPreflight`, `Invoke-DemoTemplateDeployment`, `New-DemoBootstrapBundle`, `Publish-DemoWorkloadRunner`, `Show-DemoDeploymentProgress`, `Sync-DemoFabricAppStage`, and `Test-DemoFabricApp`; the tests for Arc, the first run, the lifecycle, one command, preflight, the tracks, and the lifecycle scripts; and the README, CHANGELOG, this report, deployment, troubleshooting, architecture, the automation support matrix, and ADRs 0003, 0009, 0018, and 0019.

Validation of the fixes, 2026-10-29: `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check PSScriptAnalyzer, Pester, Markdown` with strict mode: PSScriptAnalyzer **Pass** (no findings in 323 files), Pester **Pass** (2,696 tests passed, none failed, 7 skipped by their own conditions), and markdownlint **Pass** (no issues in 128 files); the affected test files again with `GITHUB_ACTIONS` set, as CI masks its logs: **Pass** (494 tests). The public push scan of `origin/main..HEAD` found 0 hits.

Decided differently from the review's suggestions: on Linux a clipboard needs `DISPLAY` (XWayland sets it too), not `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` alone, because `xclip` is an X client; Azure Cloud Shell is recognized by `AZUREPS_HOST_ENVIRONMENT`, which Learn doesn't document, so it's only a second line behind the Linux rule (R570); and `-Sequential` is judged by the same DONE requirements, with its own DONE check, rather than by its old exit codes.

**Review follow-ups (2026-10-29, the same pull request).** The reviewer confirmed the eight fixes and found two follow-ups. Each fix again came with Pester tests that reproduced it first:

1. A fresh `deploy.ps1 -Sequential` still couldn't be **DONE**: its Fabric stage neither imported the Real-Time Dashboard nor ran the Power BI stage, so on a new workspace `FABRIC.ITEMS.REQUIRED` failed and every stage passed, but the run ended **NOT DONE** with exit code 1. The stage now does what the parallel Fabric track does: `configure-fabric.ps1` with the dashboard's import, then `configure-powerbi.ps1` for the history semantic model and report; a **Fail** in either fails the stage.
2. The watchdog couldn't restart a stuck `archost01` once its bootstrap had started, which includes every golden redeploy: it read the host's health through a Run Command, which times out exactly when the VM agent is stuck, so it only added about 10 minutes before `DEPLOY.EXTENSIONS_ARCHOST01` failed. It now judges from the deployment state alone. A new tag, `DbHubT2BuildUtc`, is set when the host's loader starts a bootstrap generation, in the parallel track and in the sequential Bootstraps stage, and cleared when a new golden copy is released and when T2 is done. The T2 track's step, and a sequential run's stage, cover a state written before the tag existed, and a state that can't be read refuses the restart. The guided stop's time limit and a golden Release in the same process still refuse it, as before.

Changed files: `Invoke-DemoDeployment` (for both), `TrackHelpers.ps1`, `VmExtensionHelpers.ps1`, `DeployHelpers.ps1`, `Get-DemoDeploymentState`, and `Set-DemoDeploymentState` (for 2); the tests for the lifecycle, one command, and the tracks; and CHANGELOG, this report (R564 and R569), deployment, troubleshooting, and ADR 0003.

Validation of the follow-ups, 2026-10-29: `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check PSScriptAnalyzer, Pester, Markdown` with strict mode: PSScriptAnalyzer **Pass** (no findings in 323 files), Pester **Pass** (2,702 tests passed, none failed, 7 skipped by their own conditions), and markdownlint **Pass** (no issues in 128 files); the affected test files with the conventions and neutral wording, with `GITHUB_ACTIONS` set: **Pass** (389 tests). The public push scan of `origin/main..HEAD` found 0 hits.

Decided differently from the review's suggestions: the restart's guard doesn't read the instance view either, because the VM agent's status there shows that the agent is stuck, not whether a guest build runs; the new tag exists because a rerun of earlier steps rewrites the T2 track's step; only the Release of a new copy clears it, because on a copy that an earlier run released, that run's loader may have started a generation since; and the optional Insights app stays out of the sequential Fabric stage, because it's optional (`./scripts/configure-fabric-app.ps1`).

**Live proof findings (2026-10-29, the same pull request).** The live proof from scratch, with its own prefix and a temporary Fabric workspace, ran to the guided stop and paused there. It proved the start banner and the line about the person's part, the keep-awake, the status page, preflight with `auto` resolved to `private`, the chunk deletes that don't wait (**Warning** only, T1 not stalled), T4's Burstable fallback (B1ms, then B2s refused, then the deployment without T4), T1 and T3 done with Database Hub's telemetry, T2's build in about 45 minutes, Arc onboarding before the wizard, the guided stop's preparation, the **WAITING FOR YOU** banner with the portal page and the clipboard, and the reminders every 5 minutes. It found three problems; each fix came with Pester tests that reproduced it first:

1. Ctrl+C at the guided stop didn't stop `deploy.ps1`: its process and the T2 track's had to be ended by process ID. PowerShell did stop the wait for the tracks, but the parallel deployment's `finally` block then called `Stop-Job` on the T2 track, which ran that track's own `finally` block: the guided stop's completion, minutes of Azure calls (Run Commands, the Bastion removal, the password rotation), with nothing on the screen; and a `finally` block doesn't take a second Ctrl+C. Now the run records the Ctrl+C (`Console.CancelKeyPress`, without changing what it does) and writes a cancel marker for the track processes, so the slow Azure cleanups in `finally` blocks (a Run Command's delete, the chunks' cleanup, the guided stop's completion) are skipped and left to the next run or `stop-demo.ps1`. Each job reports its process ID first and gets its own `Stop-Job`; one that still runs after about 3 seconds is ended with every process it started. The wait for the tracks checks for a stop every second, and a time-limited Azure CLI call waits in half-second steps and ends the CLI when it's stopped. The tracks' private files now live under the run's handoff directory, which goes. The console and the status page end **Stopped, not done** with the command that continues, the steps that were running go back to **Pending**, and the PC may sleep again, even when the stop comes while the run's experience starts.
2. The optional Insights app showed **Skipped** with a **Fail**, and a generic message about two tenant settings that were on. The cause in the log was the app step's own lookup of the workspace by name (`FABRIC.RESOLVE.WORKSPACE`; the step ended after 11 seconds), right after the Fabric step had created it. The Fabric track now gives the app's step the workspace it resolved or created (`configure-fabric-app.ps1 -WorkspaceId`, now an override of the configuration's), so it isn't looked up again. In `deploy.ps1` the step's records are never a **Fail**: a failure is **Manual action required**, marked optional, with the reported status kept in its evidence. The status page and **DONE** give the actual reason, that record's own fix, the tenant settings only when they're open, and `configure-fabric-app.ps1 -SignIn`.
3. `stop-demo.ps1` before T2's setup had finished gave `STOP.PROFILE_T2` a **Warning** with the guest's `Import-Module` error, because the guest gets its demo module only after SQL Server setup. It's **Not selected** now, "T2 isn't set up yet", in `stop-demo.ps1`, `start-demo.ps1`, and `status.ps1 -Detail`. The on-demand activity control, asked to run something there, keeps its **Warning** that T2's runner isn't ready yet, now for this error too. The guest's script checks for its module first and says so itself.

Changed files: `scripts/deploy.ps1`; the module's loader, the new `Private/RunCancelHelpers.ps1`, `ParallelDeployment.ps1`, `TrackHelpers.ps1`, `GuidedStopHelpers.ps1`, `RunCommandHelpers.ps1`, `PrivateFileHelpers.ps1`, `Invoke-DemoAzCli.ps1`, `ProgressHelpers.ps1`, `DeployExperience.ps1`, `Invoke-DemoVmRunCommand`, `Start-DemoDeploymentRun`, and `Complete-DemoDeploymentRun` (for 1); `scripts/configure-fabric-app.ps1` and `DeployHelpers.ps1` (for 2, with `TrackHelpers.ps1`, `DeployExperience.ps1`, and `ProgressHelpers.ps1`); `LifecycleHelpers.ps1`, `Get-DemoEnvironmentStatus`, `Invoke-DemoControl`, and `vm/bootstrap/Invoke-DemoWorkloadControl.ps1` (for 3); the tests for Arc, the bundle, the Fabric app, the Fabric track, the lifecycle, one command, the tracks, and the lifecycle scripts; and the README, CHANGELOG, this report (R571 and R572), deployment, troubleshooting, and ADR 0018.

Among them are a real Ctrl+C in a hidden console of its own (Windows only), a real pipeline stop of `deploy.ps1` in a runspace, and real jobs whose processes are ended. `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check PSScriptAnalyzer, Pester, Markdown` with strict mode: PSScriptAnalyzer **Pass** (no findings in 324 files), Pester **Pass** (2,725 tests passed, none failed, 7 skipped by their own conditions), and markdownlint **Pass** (no issues in 128 files); the affected test files with the conventions and neutral wording, with `GITHUB_ACTIONS` set: **Pass** (592 tests). The public push scan of `origin/main..HEAD` found 0 hits. Live, not yet: the proof continues from the guided stop with these fixes.

Decided differently from the findings' suggestions: a stop doesn't finish the guided stop's cleanup on its way out, because that cleanup is what made it slow; Bastion stays up and the host password unrotated until the next run or `stop-demo.ps1`, which the banner says (R571). In `deploy.ps1` a failed app step is **Manual action required** rather than **Not selected**, because a person can act on it; the app's own `configure-fabric-app.ps1` still reports a **Fail**, because there the app is what was asked for. The log didn't show why the second lookup failed, so the fix removes that lookup rather than retrying it.

**Review of the live-proof fixes (2026-10-29, the same pull request).** The reviewer confirmed the three fixes, and that the Ctrl+C path can't end the person's own shell, and found one problem (Medium): after a Ctrl+C at the guided stop, a later run could end **DONE** without removing Bastion or rotating the host password. The stopped guided stop skips its cleanup (`Complete-DemoGuidedStop`), which the live finding's fix leaves to the next run; but when setup finished in the guest meanwhile, the next run's guided stop wasn't needed and returned before any cleanup, T2 finished, and the run ended **DONE** with Bastion up (its cost, and a standing access path) and the handed-over password valid (with the Key Vault opt-in, its secret still enabled). `-NoWaitForT2` and `-Sequential` reruns, which don't run the guided stop, left them the same way. The fix, with Pester tests that reproduced it first:

- `Clear-DemoGuidedStopLeftover` runs `Complete-DemoGuidedStop` when the deployment state records a guided stop whose cleanup didn't finish (`DbHubGuidedStopUtc`) or a handed-over host password that isn't rotated (`DbHubHostPasswordUtc`), then reads the state again: `DEPLOY.T2.GUIDEDLEFTOVER` is **Pass** when nothing is left, and **Manual action required**, naming `./scripts/stop-demo.ps1 -RemoveBastionOnly`, when something is. The guided stop runs it when it isn't needed; the T2 step runs it at its end, whatever the step reached (`-NoWaitForT2`, a golden redeploy, a failure), unless the guided stop already did, so a cleanup that failed inside the guided stop is tried once more; and `-Sequential`'s T2 stage runs it after the Arc configuration. Never while `deploy.ps1` is being stopped, and not in the evaluation-only mode, which has no guided stop.
- **DONE** never comes while the state records either: `Get-DemoDeploymentOutcome` reads them (`Get-DemoGuidedStopLeftover`) and ends **NOT DONE**, with `./scripts/stop-demo.ps1 -RemoveBastionOnly` as the command (with the run's `-ConfigPath`), or names them in a note when another reason comes first. The banner of a run stopped at the guided stop says that the next run cleans up, whether or not setup finished meanwhile.
- `Complete-DemoGuidedStop` keeps the guided stop's record while its Bastion couldn't be removed, so the next run tries again and **DONE** waits; `stop-demo.ps1` clears it once Bastion is gone.
- The docs no longer promise exit code 2 after Ctrl+C: PowerShell usually ends the script itself, so `deploy.ps1`'s `exit` isn't reached and `pwsh` returns its own code, while its `finally` blocks still print **NOT DONE** and set the status page.
- The review's optional suggestion too: each job reports its process's start time with its ID, and a stop ends a process only when its start time matches within a second, so a reused process ID ends nothing else.

Changed files: the module's loader, `GuidedStopHelpers.ps1`, `TrackHelpers.ps1`, `DeployExperience.ps1`, `ParallelDeployment.ps1`, and `RunCancelHelpers.ps1`, and `Invoke-DemoDeployment`, `Stop-DemoEnvironment`, and `Get-DemoDeploymentState`; `scripts/deploy.ps1`'s help; the tests for one command, the tracks, and the lifecycle; and the README, CHANGELOG, this report (R571 and R573), deployment, troubleshooting, the security model, and ADRs 0009 and 0018.

Decided differently from the review's suggestions: **DONE** is guarded by the state, not only by the cleanup's result, so a run whose T2 track finished earlier, and doesn't run its T2 step again, still isn't **DONE** while access is open. A password that `start-demo.ps1 -Interactive` handed over counts too, because the state can't tell it from a guided stop's (R573). The guided stop's record now outlives a Bastion that couldn't be removed, because that record is what lets the state show such a Bastion without another Azure call at the end of the run.

**Live proof, second day (2026-10-30, the same pull request).** Resuming the proof found that running `./scripts/deploy.ps1` again didn't start VMs that were stopped, so "run ./scripts/deploy.ps1 again; it continues where it stopped" failed whenever the nightly auto-shutdown, `stop-demo.ps1`, or a Ctrl+C followed by a stop had deallocated them: nothing in the deploy path read the power state, and the first Run Command failed with "...can't run, because the VM isn't running (deallocated or stopped)". The fix, with Pester tests that reproduced it first:

- `Request-DemoStoppedStart`, right after the state is read and before preflight, on an existing environment and when the run goes past preflight: each VM's power state is read; a deallocated or stopped `sqlvm01` or `archost01` gets `az vm start --no-wait` (`Request-DemoVmStart`), one still deallocating or starting is waited for later, a running one, or one that doesn't exist yet, is only read; a deployed T4 that's stopped gets Servers - Start (`Invoke-DemoT4PowerAction -WaitMinutes 0`, which only reads a Ready T4). So the VMs boot while preflight runs.
- `Wait-DemoStoppedStart`, after preflight and before the tracks start or the template deploys (in `-Sequential`, before the first stage after preflight): each VM until it runs (`Invoke-DemoVmPowerAction`), a golden snapshot's source host's guest (`Invoke-DemoGoldenSourceRelease`; any other guest starts with its host, its automatic start action), T1's monitoring plugin when no T1 Monitoring step runs in that run (`Restore-DemoSqlVmMonitoringPlugin`, as `start-demo.ps1` does after a deallocation), and T4 until it's Ready (15 minutes at most; the template's own T4 start is then skipped). `DEPLOY.VMSTART` is **Pass**, or **Fail** with the next action when a VM still doesn't run, and the run stops there, before anything needs it.
- The status page's Preflight track has a new step, **Start the VMs that were stopped**: "Starting the VMs that were stopped: no action needed (sqlvm01 (deallocated), ...)", then what started; **Skipped** when nothing was stopped or nothing is deployed yet. A run whose preflight then fails says, in a **Warning**, that what it started keeps running until `stop-demo.ps1` or the auto-shutdown.
- Unchanged: activity profiles (the run's own steps set them), T3 (it resumes on its own at its first connection), a first deployment, a what-if, and `-StopAfter Preflight`, which read no power state.

Decided differently from the finding's suggestions: there's no `Start-DemoVmNoWait`; the existing helpers are `Request-DemoVmStart` (the start without waiting) and `Invoke-DemoVmPowerAction` (the power-state polling), which `start-demo.ps1` uses. There's no separate guest start either: the T2 guest starts with its host (Hyper-V's automatic start action, and the host's own bootstrap), except on a golden snapshot's source host, whose guest `Invoke-DemoGoldenSourceRelease` reconnects, as `start-demo.ps1` does. T1's monitoring plugin is checked too, though the finding didn't ask, because after a deallocation it can stay **NotRunning** for hours, and a resumed run whose T1 Monitoring step doesn't run would otherwise wait for T1's telemetry in vain. T4 is waited for in the same step, before the tracks, because T1's T4 step and the T4 track need it Ready, and the template rejects a stopped server.

CI at 22d17e5 failed one test on the Linux runner, twice (the run and one rerun): the test of Ctrl+C at the guided stop, which passed on Windows and at c7df7d7. Its run takes about 250 ms, so the mocked T2 thread job could be stopped before its script started, which ends a thread job **Failed** rather than **Stopped** (reproduced locally: 30 of 30 **Failed** when stopped at once, 30 of 30 **Stopped** once it runs). The mock now waits until the job is inside its `try` block, as the T2 track is at the guided stop; the code under test is unchanged (commit 35fa25e).

**Live proof, the guided stop (2026-10-30, the same pull request).** At the guided stop, on c7df7d7, setup's **Use Azure Login** opened a white window, then Internet Explorer's dialog: "Content within this application coming from the website listed below is being blocked by Internet Explorer Enhanced Security Configuration. `https://login.microsoftonline.com`". `archost01`'s health showed `GuestWebView2` **Succeeded**, and the guest had signed itself in and opened setup as designed. The owner then turned IE ESC off for Administrators in Server Manager while setup was open, and the dialog still came; **Add...** for the site got past it. So setup's sign-in is an Internet Explorer-based control, and a change made while setup runs doesn't reach it. Why not WebView2: MSAL.NET documents that it uses its legacy, IE-based web view for Microsoft Entra ID authorities whatever is installed, and that the legacy view is a .NET Framework app's default ([using WebView2 with MSAL.NET](https://learn.microsoft.com/entra/msal/dotnet/advanced/webview2)); SQL Server's documentation doesn't say which library setup uses, so that's an inference, recorded as R575, and IE ESC is the dependable path. On 2026-10-21, WebView2 was installed and IE ESC was turned off by hand on the same day, so the blank window then was put down to WebView2 alone. The fix, with Pester tests that reproduced it first:

- **Before anyone is asked, before the guest's automatic sign-in, and before setup opens** (`Initialize-DemoHostGuidedSignIn`, in the `SqlSetupWizard` step once `deploy.ps1`'s request is valid): the WebView2 runtime is checked in the guest (`Test-DemoGuestWebView2`) and installed when it's missing (`Install-DemoHostGuestWebView2`); IE ESC is turned off for administrators (`IsInstalled` 0, as [Azure File Sync's deployment guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/storage/file-sync/file-sync-deployment-guide#prepare-windows-server-to-use-with-azure-file-sync) documents for its interactive Azure sign-in, and Server Manager's **Local Server** setting does); and the Azure portal's documented [authentication hosts](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-portal/azure-portal-safelist-urls#azure-portal-urls-for-proxy-bypass), as exact hosts, become Trusted sites (zone 2) for the guest's Administrator under both `ZoneMap\Domains` and `ZoneMap\EscDomains` ([IE security zones registry entries](https://learn.microsoft.com/previous-versions/troubleshoot/browsers/security-privacy/ie-security-zones-registry-entries)), with a snapshot of what was there first. The host records it before it changes anything (`guided-setup-signin.json`: state, names, and zones, no secret), so a run that stops midway still restores.
- **Nobody is asked before it's ready:** until WebView2 is there and IE ESC is off, the step is **InProgress** with "Preparing setup's Azure sign-in in arcsql01 (Azure-side, no action needed): ...", and nothing is armed or opened. The host's health says `guidedSignIn` (`none`, `preparing`, `ready`, or `restoring`; `Get-DemoHealth.ps1 -View Summary` prints it), and `deploy.ps1` asks only at `ready`; the status page shows the host's message on the guided stop's step, marked Azure-side. A health without the field, from a host bundle that predates it, is ready at its step as before.
- **Undone in the cleanup** (`Restore-DemoHostGuidedSignIn`), in the automatic sign-in's: when setup is done, when the request expires or goes (the host's guard before every pass, and the step), but not between two of its tries. IE ESC goes back on for administrators and users, and the Trusted sites entries go back to the snapshot, so sites added with **Add...** meanwhile go too; without a snapshot, only the sign-in hosts go. The record goes only once the guest confirms it; otherwise the cleanup fails closed, the record stays, and the setup step isn't done (`Test-DemoHostGuidedSetupDone`).
- **The instructions:** the host's steps, the steps beside Virtual Machine Connection, and the evaluation-only VM's no longer say a blank window means a missing WebView2. If the dialog appears, select **Add...** for each site it names, then **Use Azure Login** again; if the window stays blank, close setup, turn IE ESC off for Administrators (**Server Manager** > **Local Server**), and open setup again. The guest's launcher logs IE ESC's state as setup starts.
- **Not used, and why:** the **Site to Zone Assignment List** policy, because it locks the user's own zone settings, so the dialog's **Add...** would stop working ([seamless SSO quickstart](https://learn.microsoft.com/entra/identity/hybrid/connect/how-to-connect-sso-quick-start)); and wildcard entries, whose matching the documentation doesn't spell out.

Changed files: `vm/hyperv-guest/GuestSetup.ps1` (`Get-DemoGuidedSignInHost`, `Invoke-DemoGuestSignInBrowserAction`, the record, `Initialize-DemoHostGuidedSignIn`, `Restore-DemoHostGuidedSignIn`, the step, the cleanup, the guard, the done check, the health detail, and the launcher), `GuestSql.ps1`, `vm/bootstrap/HostPlatform.ps1`, `HostSteps.ps1`, `Invoke-HostBootstrap.ps1`, and `Invoke-EvalArcVmBootstrap.ps1` (the instructions and comments), `vm/dsc/host.psd1` (a comment), `vm/health/Get-DemoHealth.ps1` (`guidedSignIn`), and the module's `GuidedStopHelpers.ps1`; the tests for the guided setup, the host plan, and one command; and the README, CHANGELOG, this report (R575), deployment, troubleshooting, the security model, limitations, references, architecture, the automation matrix (row 169), the VM READMEs, and ADRs 0003, 0005, 0006, and 0009.

The affected test files (every `Vm.*` suite and one command) with the conventions, neutral wording, and the walkthrough, with `GITHUB_ACTIONS` set: **Pass** (536 tests).
