Decided differently from the finding's suggestions: the Trusted sites entries are per-user registry entries, not the policy, as above, and they're restored from a snapshot rather than by deleting a fixed list, so the sites a person adds with **Add...** go too; WebView2's absence holds the prompt back as the owner asked, though Microsoft's documentation suggests setup's sign-in doesn't use it; the Trusted sites entries don't hold the prompt back when they can't be written (a **Warning** in the host's log), because IE ESC off is what the sign-in needs and **Add...** covers the rest; and the instructions for a blank window also say to close and reopen setup, because the owner found that a change made while setup is open doesn't reach it.

**Live proof, the resumed run (2026-10-30, the same pull request).** The run resumed after the Ctrl+C at the guided stop, on c7df7d7, finished T2 end to end: `DEPLOY.T2.GUIDEDLEFTOVER` **Pass** (it removed the leftover Bastion and rotated the handed-over password), `T2.ARC.SQLINSTANCE` Standard with pay-as-you-go, and `T2.ARC.MONITORING` and `TELEMETRY.T2` **Pass**. It ended **NOT DONE**, exit code 1, on `TELEMETRY.T1`: "No performance telemetry from T1 (sqlvm01) in the last 60 minutes. System.String[]". `sqlvm01` had been deallocated overnight, and `start-demo.ps1` had started it at about 07:45 and found its monitoring plugin running at 07:47 (`START.T1.MONITORING` **Pass**); by 09:12 the SQL IaaS extension reported `DatabaseMonitorArcPlugin` `{"State":"NotRunning"}`. The T1 track had finished in an earlier run, so its Monitoring step didn't run, and nothing looked at the plugin before the final check failed. `Restore-DemoSqlVmMonitoringPlugin`, run by hand, restarted the SQL IaaS Agent service, and the plugin was running again after 161 seconds. The fix, with Pester tests that reproduced it first:

- **T1's plugin on every run:** every run that ends with the validation and has no T1 Monitoring step checks the plugin once the VMs run, after the start of the VMs that were stopped, whether or not this run started `sqlvm01` (`Invoke-DemoT1MonitoringCheck`: `Restore-DemoSqlVmMonitoringPlugin`, `DEPLOY.T1.MONITORING`), and restarts the SQL IaaS Agent service when the plugin isn't running. In the parallel deployment it runs in its own process beside the tracks (`Start-DemoT1MonitoringJob`, with its own guard), so the guided stop's prompt never waits for it, and a stop ends it with the tracks; without track processes it runs in line, and in `-Sequential` before the first stage that needs the VMs. A failure is a **Warning**: the telemetry check decides. The VM start no longer restores the plugin itself (its `-RestoreT1Monitoring` is gone), because this check covers every run.
- **A second look before the run is judged:** a target whose telemetry didn't come (`Get-DemoTelemetryRetryTarget`: no rows, or a newest sample that's too old; not an access or query error, an unknown resource ID, or rows under another ID) gets its monitoring checked once (`Invoke-DemoTelemetryRecovery`): T1's plugin, restarted when it stopped; T2's Arc connection and monitoring, read-only, because monitoring is a setting of the Arc SQL Server instance that a restart doesn't turn off; and T3's warm-up, which wakes a paused database. A target whose monitoring is on then gets its telemetry waited for once more, up to `-TelemetryWaitMinutes`, and the new result replaces the first (`Invoke-DemoTelemetryCheck`). It runs in the tracks' telemetry steps, in `-Sequential`'s validation, and last in DONE's checks, there only when everything else DONE needs passed. The status page says "T1's monitoring stopped after a restart; restarting it, no action needed".
- **`System.String[]`:** `Get-DemoMemberValue` returns an array whole, so `@(Get-DemoMemberValue ...)` was an array that held the notes' array: the reason's format printed it as `System.String[]` (`Complete-DemoDeploymentRun`), and the banner printed every note on one line. The notes are read into a variable first now, and every message and fix in the ending goes through `ConvertTo-DemoPlainText`, which joins an array's items.

**Live proof, the final run (2026-10-30, the same pull request).** With T1's plugin restored, the final run on c7df7d7 passed every DONE requirement: `TELEMETRY.T1` to `T3`, `HUB.ESTATE.T1` to `T3`, `T2.ARC.SQLINSTANCE` (Standard, pay-as-you-go), `T2.ARC.MONITORING`, `T1.SQLVM.ASSOCIATEDSERVICES`, and `FABRIC.ITEMS.REQUIRED`, with `DEPLOY.TIMING` **Pass** ("Everything is deployed and populated in 20.5 minutes"). It still ended **NOT DONE**, exit code 2, with `./scripts/deploy.ps1` as the next step, which couldn't change anything: `DEPLOY.SUMMARY` held three **Manual action required** reminders, `PREFLIGHT.P4.HUBSETTING` and `FABRIC.P4.HUBSETTING` (the same tenant setting twice) and `FABRIC.APP.BROWSER` (the optional Insights app, deployed this time, waiting for its browser check), and the reason ended in `System.String[]` again. The owner: "The HTML report page says you stopped because the fabric admin setting to allow the whole org to the database hub is not enabled. It has always been enabled and just confirmed. Is this something that is a false positive ... We need to have better logging and correct this false positive if true." It was a false positive, with three causes: `Test-DemoFabricHubSetting` called the Fabric admin API only with `-FabricAdmin`, which `deploy.ps1` never passed, so it always said "Confirm that ... is enabled; reading it needs a Fabric administrator", whatever the setting was; `Get-DemoDeploymentOutcome` exempted only the preflight copy from DONE, so the Fabric track's copy blocked it; and the banner and the status page gave that reminder as the reason the run stopped, so it read as "the setting is not enabled". The fix, with Pester tests that reproduced it first:

- **The setting is read when it can be:** `Test-DemoFabricHubSetting` calls [Tenants - List Tenant Settings](https://learn.microsoft.com/rest/api/fabric/admin/tenants/list-tenant-settings) on every call, every page, in preflight (`-CheckId PREFLIGHT.P4.HUBSETTING`) and in the Database Hub checklist; `-FabricAdmin` is accepted and changes nothing. Read and on for the organization: **Pass**. On only for security groups: **Warning**, as before. Read and off: **Manual action required**, "is disabled (read through the Fabric admin API)", the one P4 result that keeps a run from DONE. Anything else (HTTP 401 or 403, no token, the setting not among those read, an answer without an `enabled` value, another error): **Manual action required**, "Couldn't check the Fabric tenant setting ... (needs a Fabric administrator; HTTP 403): please confirm it's enabled", never "disabled". The evidence tells them apart (`checkedThroughApi`, `statusCode`, `pages`, `settingsRead`, `complete`, `settingRead`, `matchedBy`, `settingName`, `enabled`, `readError`), and the log records each call and its outcome (the status code, the number of settings read, and whether the setting matched by `settingName` or by title), never a token.
- **What only a person can confirm never makes a run NOT DONE** (`Test-DemoConfirmOnlyResult`): the Hub tenant setting when it wasn't read, decided from its evidence (`Test-DemoTenantSettingRead`) whichever check ID reported it; `FABRIC.P5.CAPACITY` on an Active capacity whose SKU the check doesn't recognize; `HUB.PRESENTER.*`, the roles an administrator grants when `assignRoles` is false; and `FABRIC.APP.BROWSER`. When every DONE requirement passes, the run is **DONE**, exit code 0, and the console and the status page list those items under **Please confirm**, once each (`Get-DemoConfirmItem`: the setting is one line whichever check reported it, and none when a check read it on for the organization).
- **NOT DONE only when a rerun or a named step can help:** its reason names only what blocks DONE, and its next step is that item's own fix, a failed requirement's included; only telemetry, estate entries, or metrics that lag get "wait about 10 minutes, then run `deploy.ps1` again". `DEPLOY.SUMMARY` (`Get-DemoDeploySummary`, in both deployment paths) lists what needs you apart from what to confirm, and says "Nothing else needs you for DONE" when only the latter remain.
- **No `System.String[]`** in the reason or the summary: every message and fix goes through `ConvertTo-DemoPlainText`.
- **The optional item:** on a new workspace, the history model's blank measures (about 40 `FABRIC.SEMANTICMODEL.MEASURE.*` **Warnings**) are one **Warning** in `deploy.ps1`'s Power BI step, `FABRIC.SEMANTICMODEL.MEASURES`: "The history report is empty until NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity saves its first snapshots; that's expected right after a first deployment", with how to fill it (`Merge-DemoBlankMeasureResult`). `configure-powerbi.ps1` keeps every record.

**The owner's instruction for setup's sign-in (2026-10-30, the same pull request).** The owner asked, verbatim: "Add in the readme or instructions when registration the IaaS extension, if the azure login is still blank or throws an error that it is not a trusted site then add the microsoft login page to the trusted site list which should be following the directions on the screen". The same short instruction is now the fallback, beside the automatic fix (IE ESC off before setup starts, and the sign-in hosts added to Trusted sites), everywhere the person sees the guided stop's steps: "If the Azure Login window stays blank, or Internet Explorer says content from `https://login.microsoftonline.com` is blocked, follow the dialog's directions: select **Add...**, then **Add**, to put the Microsoft sign-in page in Trusted sites, and **Close**. Then select **Use Azure Login** again. Repeat for any other sign-in site it names", noting that it was confirmed live on 2026-10-30 and that turning the setting off while setup is open doesn't help. It's in the README's guided-stop steps and its AI assistant section, the console's **WAITING FOR YOU** banner and the status page's **Waiting for you** (`Get-DemoGuidedStopInstruction`), the host's own instructions in `health.json` (`Get-DemoPaygWizardInstruction`), the steps beside Virtual Machine Connection (`Get-DemoGuidedSetupStepText`), the evaluation-only VM's instructions, troubleshooting, and deployment. The instructions' earlier advice for a blank window, to close setup and turn IE ESC off in Server Manager, is gone from them; troubleshooting keeps it for a window that stays blank with no dialog to use. No instruction blames a missing WebView2.

Decided differently from the findings' suggestions: T1's plugin check runs beside the tracks rather than before them, so the guided stop's prompt never waits up to about 3 minutes for an agent restart; its message says "after the start" when this run started `sqlvm01`. The second look also covers a newest sample that's too old (a **Warning**), not only a **Fail**, and the tracks' telemetry steps too, once per check; T2 gets no repair, only the read that decides whether to wait, and T3's is its warm-up. `start-demo.ps1` doesn't check the plugin again after a delay: `deploy.ps1` now checks it on every run, and the docs say so, and that `validate.ps1` only reports `TELEMETRY.T1` and doesn't repair it. What only a person can confirm is an explicit list rather than every **Manual action required**, so a new kind of reminder blocks until it's added (R577); the Estate page's visual check isn't a result of its own, it's in **Where to look**; and a setting on only for security groups is listed under **Please confirm** too, asking that every presenter is a member. `DEPLOY.SUMMARY` stays **Manual action required** when only items to confirm remain, because it lists them and isn't one of DONE's inputs. The measures are collapsed only in `deploy.ps1`'s Power BI step. The guest's steps beside Virtual Machine Connection got the owner's instruction too, though the request didn't list them.

**Live proof, the run on the fixes (2026-10-30, the same pull request).** On 3618de3, `./scripts/deploy.ps1` ran against the proof environment, whose VMs the nightly auto-shutdown had deallocated, and ended **DONE**, exit code 0, in 20 minutes, with no person needed: 171 **Pass**, no **Fail**, 73 **Warning**, 16 **Not selected**, 1 **Not supported**, and 2 **Manual action required** (`FABRIC.APP.BROWSER`, the optional app's browser check, and `DEPLOY.SUMMARY` with its list to confirm). It proved:

- the start of the VMs that were stopped, without `start-demo.ps1`: "Started sqlvm01 and archost01, which had been stopped, in about 1 minute(s).";
- `PREFLIGHT.P4.HUBSETTING` and `FABRIC.P4.HUBSETTING` both **Pass** through the Fabric admin API (HTTP 200, 182 tenant settings read, matched by title);
- the **DONE** banner, with the T4-off note and its **Please confirm** list, and no `System.String[]` anywhere in the run's output;
- T1's monitoring plugin **Running**, with its uploads OK, after the start (`T1.SQLVM.MONITORING` **Pass**, nothing to repair), so the repair path didn't run; the restart itself was proven by hand earlier that day (161 seconds).

That run and the reviewer's delta review of c7df7d7..3618de3 (two Low findings, no blocker; 2,782 Pester tests passed) led to the last changes, each with Pester tests:

1. **Fewer lines before the banner:** `Write-DemoResultReport` logged one **Warning** line per remediation, so about 58 blank-measure **Warnings** with the same remediation came right before **DONE**. Now 3 or more **Warnings** that share a remediation are one line, `58 checks (FABRIC.SEMANTICMODEL.MEASURE.*, e.g. ...): <remediation>`, with the check IDs' common prefix and two examples; every **Fail** keeps its own line, and the table, `out/deploy.jsonl`, and the GitHub job summary keep every result.
2. **The heading after DONE:** "2 item(s) need you (Manual action required):" contradicted `DEPLOY.SUMMARY`'s "Nothing else needs you for DONE". `deploy.ps1` now passes `-OutcomeDone` when its run ended **DONE**, and the heading says "2 item(s) are Manual action required; none of them keeps this run from DONE:".
3. **The VM start in words:** the status page says "Starting sqlvm01 and archost01, which were stopped (deallocated); no action needed from you." instead of "Starting the VMs that were stopped: no action needed (sqlvm01 (deallocated), archost01 (deallocated))".
4. **Preflight in words:** "Preflight passed (network mode: private; T3 free offer: not used; T2 source: the existing host, built from the image)." instead of "T3 free offer False" and "T2 source existing-build" (`Get-DemoPreflightPassText`, on both deployment paths).
5. **This record:** the run above, in this paragraph.

- **A. The Hub tenant setting by its latest read** (Low, from the delta review): when an administrator turned the setting on during a run, preflight's read as disabled still made the run **NOT DONE**, though the Fabric track's later read said enabled, because each P4 result was judged on its own. Now the run's latest read decides (`Get-DemoHubSettingLatestRead`, with `-Context` in `Test-DemoConfirmOnlyResult`, the outcome, `DEPLOY.SUMMARY`, the confirm list, and the Validation step), and when that read says disabled, `Invoke-DemoDoneGate` reads it once more (`FABRIC.P4.HUBSETTING`, one call) before the run is judged.
- **B. The snapshot of the Trusted sites** (Low, from the delta review): a pass whose PowerShell Direct call failed after the guest had prepared left no snapshot on the host, so the next pass's snapshot already held the sign-in hosts' entries, and the cleanup kept them as if they had been there before. A fresh snapshot now leaves out the sign-in hosts' Trusted sites values (`https` 2), so the cleanup always removes them, the dialog's **Add...** entries for them included.
- **C. The sign-in preparation fails open:** a preparation that never finished (WebView2 not installed, IE ESC still on, PowerShell Direct failing) kept the setup step **InProgress** with nobody asked, up to the run's 180 minutes, though **Add...** in setup's dialog worked live. The host's record now holds when this request's preparation first began and its passes; not ready about 15 minutes or 3 passes after it began, the host logs a **Warning**, turns the record's state to `Fallback` (health `guidedSignIn` fallback), and opens setup anyway. `deploy.ps1` then asks for the person, its console, status page, and the host's own steps lead with **Add...**, and `DEPLOY.T2.GUIDEDSTOP` says the automatic preparation didn't finish. The record still drives the cleanup, so the done check and the golden gates are unchanged.
- **D. What bills when the run isn't DONE:** a **NOT DONE** or stopped run that started VMs that were stopped, or created them, adds one line: "Running and billing, because this run started or created them: sqlvm01 and archost01. ./scripts/stop-demo.ps1 stops them now; otherwise the nightly auto-shutdown stops them at 19:00 (Pacific Standard Time)." The names come from the progress model (`Add-DemoProgressStartedVm`: what the VM start asked to start and started, and on a first deployment what the template creates, recorded before it runs), the only record after a Ctrl+C, and from this run's `DEPLOY.VMSTART`, without a VM that didn't start.

Decided differently from the requests: the heading's switch is set from the outcome's `Kind`, as `Complete-DemoDeploymentRun` reads it, and Preflight's text names T2's source in words too, not only the free offer. For A, the second read is the Fabric track's check, whichever check read the setting as off, and one that can't read keeps the earlier "off". For B, only the snapshot's filter, not a separate read before Prepare: it covers a lost answer and the dialog's entries alike, and the hosts' keys stay as empty keys, which map nothing. For C, the record is now written when the preparation begins, so a pass that fails before the guest answers counts too, and `guidedSignIn` says preparing from the first pass; after the fallback, the same request doesn't prepare again, a new request starts over, and a check that fails after the preparation was ready for the request keeps it ready. For D, T4 is named with the VMs, while the auto-shutdown clause names only the VMs, since T4 stops with `stop-demo.ps1`; a run without a progress model names only what `DEPLOY.VMSTART` reports (R578).

**Live proof, the rerun on b18a028 (2026-10-30, the same pull request).** Started from deallocated VMs, the rerun ended **DONE**, exit code 0, with 171 **Pass** and no **Fail**, but in 45 minutes instead of 20, all of it T1's plugin. T1's Monitoring step started at 13:29:21 and showed nothing on the console or the status page; `T1.SQLVM.MONITORING` came as a **Warning** at 13:54:43, 25 minutes later: the 20-minute wait, counted as 20 reads a minute apart, plus each read's 15 to 20 seconds. Then `DEPLOY.T1.MONITORING` **Pass** at 13:57:15 ("...the SQL IaaS Agent service was restarted, and it's Running with uploads OK after 17 seconds."), and `TELEMETRY.T1` **Pass**, its newest sample 0.5 minutes old. The flag was set, and the plugin reported `{"State":"NotRunning"}` about 30 minutes after the VM started; on 3618de3 it had been running (the step took 24 seconds), and that morning, after the overnight deallocation, it was `NotRunning` for hours, so about 2 of 3 starts leave it `NotRunning`, and it doesn't come back by itself. The run proved items 1 to 4 above live: one "60 checks (FABRIC.SEMANTICMODEL.MEASURE.*, ...)" line, "2 item(s) are Manual action required; none of them keeps this run from DONE:", and both texts in words. Its console still printed "T1.SQLVM.MONITORING: Run configure-monitoring.ps1 -WaitMinutes 15, or check again later." after the restart, and `PREFLIGHT.FABRIC.VIEWERLICENSE`'s remediation twice. With the reviewer's delta review of b18a028 (one Low finding, 9/10, reproduced for T4; ready to merge once the live rerun passed), the changes, each with Pester tests:

- **A short grace for a `NotRunning` plugin, not the whole wait:** `Enable-DemoSqlVmMonitoring -NotRunningGraceMinutes` ends the wait once the plugin has reported a state other than Running for that long, and the step restarts the agent (`Restore-DemoSqlVmMonitoringPlugin -NotRunningGraceMinutes 0`, since the grace is spent). The grace is 3 minutes when the flag was set before the run, and 5 when the run merged it (T1's Bootstraps step, or the same call), because the setting takes a few minutes to apply ([performance monitoring for SQL Server on Azure VMs](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/virtual-machines/windows/enable-performance-monitoring-sql-vm): most data within three to five minutes); `Get-DemoSqlVmPluginGrace` holds both. A plugin with no status yet still gets the whole wait, then the restart, as before.
- **Counted in elapsed time:** `-MonitoringWaitMinutes` bounds the whole call, the prerequisites' wait, the merge, and the reads included, through `Get-DemoUtcNow`, and no sleep runs past the wait's or the grace's end; the result's evidence has `waitedSeconds`, `notRunningGraceMinutes`, and `stoppedForRestart`.
- **Said while it waits:** every read sets the status page's T1 Monitoring step, and the console gets a line when the wait starts, when the plugin's state changes, and every few minutes (`Show-DemoSqlVmPluginWait`): "T1's monitoring plugin reports NotRunning (3 min so far); the deployment restarts it if it doesn't start by itself within 1 minute; no action needed.", then "Restarting T1's monitoring plugin, which stopped after a restart; no action needed." and a line once the agent restarted.
- **No 20-minute wait anywhere else:** `start-demo.ps1` after a start, the check beside the tracks, and the second look at T1's telemetry before **NOT DONE** never waited 20 minutes: they restart a `NotRunning` plugin at once, and still do (`Restore-DemoSqlVmMonitoringPlugin -NotRunningGraceMinutes` is 0 by default; see the follow-up below). `start-demo.ps1`'s wait after it recreates T3 did wait (all of `-MonitoringWaitMinutes` on a `NotRunning` plugin, and no restart); it now takes the deployment's path with `-RestartAtOnce`, so the restart comes at the first `NotRunning` read, though a flag it merges still gets 5 minutes. `configure-monitoring.ps1` keeps its own `-WaitMinutes`, 0 by default, and doesn't restart the agent.
- **No stale remediation after a restart that worked:** the deployment's T1 Monitoring step, `-Sequential`'s Monitoring stage, and `start-demo.ps1`'s T3 path share `Invoke-DemoSqlVmMonitoringWait`. After a restart that passed, it reads `T1.SQLVM.MONITORING` again, read-only (`Test-DemoSqlVmMonitoring`), and reports it as it is then, with the restart in its evidence (`restartCheckId`, `restarted`, `pluginStateBefore`) and its message; a read that fails, or a plugin not Running and OK again yet, keeps the wait's **Warning**, marked superseded and without a remediation. A restart that didn't help leaves the **Warning** and its remediation. It's the step's own code, so it holds on every run whose T1 track runs it, the first or a later one.
- **One line per check and remediation:** `Write-DemoResultReport` logs a check ID with the same remediation once, however many sources reported it (a **Fail**'s pair wins over the same **Warning**'s), and a **Manual action required** check with the same steps once; the table, `out/deploy.jsonl`, and the job summary are unchanged.
- **The billing line names only what exists** (from the delta review): a first deployment recorded `sqlvm01`, `archost01`, and T4 before its template ran, so a failed template, or the Burstable fallback ending without T4, named what didn't exist ("T4 ... isn't deployed" and "Running and billing ...: sqlvm01, archost01, and T4" in one banner). It now records them once the template succeeded, with T4 only when it was deployed, and nothing after a failed template. The VM start records only the VMs this run issued a start for: its own request, or the start `Invoke-DemoVmPowerAction` issued once a stopping VM had settled (evidence `issued`), never one that something else was already starting; `DEPLOY.VMSTART`'s evidence says so (`started`), and `Get-DemoStartedVm` reads it. This corrects D above.
- **Two accepted risks of C,** recorded as R579 (no snapshot after a fallback from a missing WebView2 or a failing PowerShell Direct, so another site added with **Add...** stays in Trusted sites) and R580 (a new request ID resets a `Fallback` record, so the host can say "Azure-side, no action needed" for up to about 15 minutes after the person was asked); the grace itself is R581.

Decided differently from the requests: the grace counts from the first read that showed `NotRunning`, so a plugin that had no status before gets the full grace once it reports one; "about 3 minutes" is three reads a minute apart, the last sleep shortened to the grace's end. In 3a26bdb, `start-demo.ps1` and the check beside the tracks got the grace too, as first asked; the owner then kept their immediate restart (the follow-up below). After a restart, `T1.SQLVM.MONITORING` is read with the read-only check rather than `Enable-DemoSqlVmMonitoring` again, and replaced, rather than only marked superseded, whenever that read works; `Get-DemoUtcNow`, the clock the guided stop already reads, is the seam that lets the tests count a read's time.

**Follow-up: the immediate restart kept (2026-10-30, the same pull request).** On 3a26bdb, the owner kept the immediate restart for `start-demo.ps1` and the check beside the tracks: it's the proven behavior, presenters run `start-demo.ps1` before each session, so 3 more minutes count there, and the request had only been that nothing wait 20 minutes. `Restore-DemoSqlVmMonitoringPlugin -NotRunningGraceMinutes` is 0 by default again, which covers `start-demo.ps1`'s check after a start, the check beside the tracks, and the second look before **NOT DONE**; the T1 Monitoring step's and `-Sequential`'s grace, through `Enable-DemoSqlVmMonitoring -NotRunningGraceMinutes` and then `Restore-DemoSqlVmMonitoringPlugin -NotRunningGraceMinutes 0`, is unchanged. `start-demo.ps1`'s path after it recreates T3 went through the Monitoring step's helper, not Restore's default, so it now passes `-RestartAtOnce`; for that, `Enable-DemoSqlVmMonitoring -NotRunningGraceMinutes` takes 0 for the first `NotRunning` read, and its default is -1, the whole wait, as before for `configure-monitoring.ps1`. Changed files: `SqlVmMonitoringHelpers.ps1`, `Enable-DemoSqlVmMonitoring`, `Start-DemoEnvironment`, and `DeployExperience.ps1`; the lifecycle and Arc tests; the README, CHANGELOG, this report (R581), deployment, limitations, and ADR 0018.

**The reviewer's delta review of 3a26bdb, and the runs on it (2026-10-30, the same pull request).** The review found one Low issue (9/10, reproduced; its verdict was ready to merge once the live rerun passed; 2,809 Pester tests passed). `Get-DemoSqlVmMonitoringAfterRestart` treated as current only a **Pass**, or a **Warning** that isn't a wait. So a read after the restart that came back **Fail** (uploads that fail with HTTP 403, for example) became the wait's **Warning**: marked superseded, without a remediation, and saying "...restarted, and the plugin reported Running with uploads OK". The T1 track then went on to a vaguer **NOT DONE** on `TELEMETRY.T1`. Restore's early **Pass** checked only that the state was Running, so a plugin that started by itself just after the grace, with its uploads already failing, took that path. The fix, with Pester tests for both of the reviewer's variants and for a running plugin whose uploads fail:

- **A Fail read is returned as it is:** after the restart, or after a restore that found the plugin running, a **Fail** read keeps its own message and fix, and its evidence notes the restart, or that there was none (`restartCheckId`, `restarted`, `pluginStateBefore`). The step's **Fail** decision then stops the T1 track on its own cause. A read that throws is the same **Fail**, "Azure couldn't be read", that `Test-DemoSqlVmMonitoring` reports (R584).
- **"Superseded" only for a read that's still a wait,** after a restore that passed. Its message gives what that read shows (state and upload), never "uploads OK". A **Pass**, or a **Warning** that isn't a wait, is reported as it is, with "the SQL IaaS Agent service was restarted" or "then it started without a restart".
- **Restore passes a running plugin only with `MetricsUploadStatus` OK.** Running with uploads that fail (an HTTP error, the documented troubleshooting symptom) is a **Warning** that names the VM's identity and outbound HTTPS to the telemetry endpoint. Anything else not OK is a **Warning** to check again. Neither restarts the agent, which doesn't fix uploads.

The run on 3a26bdb ended **NOT DONE**, exit 1, for one reason only: `configure-powerbi.ps1` hit a transient TLS error during the history model or report import (at about 19:24 UTC), "The SSL connection could not be established, see inner exception." Everything else passed:

- **T1's plugin grace:** the plugin reported `NotRunning`, the restart came at 3.5 minutes, and the step passed in 6 minutes 5 seconds instead of 28.
- **The billing line was right:** "sqlvm01 and archost01 ... the nightly auto-shutdown stops them at 19:00 (Eastern Standard Time)".

A plain `./scripts/deploy.ps1` after it resumed and ended **DONE**, so the error was transient, but the resume was right for the wrong reason:

- **13:14:** a full run ended **DONE**.
- **15:14:** a fresh run (the recorded stage was `Validation`, so it didn't resume) ran every track. Its Fabric step failed, so its record wasn't written and still said `Fabric` from 13:14. The run ended with the stage `Monitoring`.
- **15:40:** the resume trusted that record, showed Fabric's three steps as "Finished in an earlier run.", never ran the failed step again, and ended **DONE**. That was right only because the report already existed with an unchanged definition.

The fixes, each with Pester tests:

- **A resume trusts only this deployment's records:** a track's record (`DbHubTrackT1`, `DbHubTrackT2`, `DbHubTrackT4`, `DbHubTrackFabric`) is written only when a step succeeds, and nothing cleared it.
  - Now, once preflight passes and before the template reads the group's tags or any track starts, a run that starts over clears every track's record (`Get-DemoTrackRecordReset`). Starting over means the last deployment ended **DONE**, or `-FromStage Preflight`. The tracks it skips are cleared too (`-SkipFabric`, `-StopAfter`).
  - A run over a range lowers the records of the tracks it runs again to the step before; a resume keeps them.
  - The run's state in memory follows, so the validation's `allTracksDone` sees the reset. The outcome reads the state at the end.
  - `-Sequential`, which records no tracks, resets them once its preflight passes.
  - The status page shows the step each resumed track picks up at as "Didn't finish in the previous run; running it again.", on the first row the run uses.
  - Tests: a **DONE** run, then a fresh run whose Fabric step fails, then a plain rerun that runs Fabric again; the same for T1's first step and a later one; `-Resume` writes no record, and `-FromStage` lowers only what it runs again.
- **Transient network errors are retried:** one policy, `Invoke-DemoTransientRetry`, like `Invoke-DemoHubTelemetryQuery`'s.
  - It covers `Invoke-DemoFabricRequest` and `Invoke-DemoPowerBIRequest`, and through them the item imports, their long-running operations, `Invoke-DemoHubFabricGet`, the tenant-settings read, and the refresh and notebook-run polling; and `Invoke-DemoRestRequest`.
  - What's retried: a network error (`HttpRequestException`, such as a failed TLS handshake, a reset connection, a DNS failure, or a socket error; and a timeout, `TaskCanceledException` or `TimeoutException`), and HTTP 408, 429, 502, 503, and 504.
  - Up to 4 attempts, 5, 15, and 30 seconds apart, or after Retry-After (up to 60 seconds), with one line per retry: "A network error reached api.powerbi.com; retrying in 5 s (attempt 2 of 4); no action needed."
  - The token handling and the allow-lists are unchanged.
  - `Invoke-DemoHubRestCall` keeps its own policy, which already retried network errors and HTTP 408, 429, 502–504, and 520.
- **POST safety:** a request that starts or adds something goes again only on HTTP 429, or when it never reached the service: `HttpRequestError` NameResolutionError, ConnectionError, SecureConnectionError, or ProxyTunnelError, or a TLS or connect-time socket error under it ([HttpRequestError](https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/api/system.net.http.httprequesterror)).
  - That applies to the notebook run's submit, the refresh, Microsoft Graph's group create and owner and member adds, and the activity control's folder, workspace, and managed private endpoint creates.
  - The other creates are sent again, and one that finds its name taken resolves it. The items (`ItemDisplayNameAlreadyInUse`, then the update path) and the lakehouse already did; the workspace now looks its name up again on HTTP 409 or `AlreadyExists`.
- **The error text:** after its attempts, a network error throws an `HttpRequestException` that names the host, the attempts, and the innermost cause, tagged for `Test-DemoNetworkFailure`. For example: "A network error reached api.powerbi.com on each of 4 attempts: The SSL connection could not be established: Authentication failed because the remote party sent a TLS alert ...". The steps' catches show the innermost cause, redacted, instead of "see inner exception" (`Get-DemoExceptionText`).
- **A console line as soon as a step fails,** once per step, with the status page's label (`Get-DemoStepFailureLine`): `The history report step failed: <why>. The other tracks continue; the run ends NOT DONE, and ./scripts/deploy.ps1 resumes it.`
- **NOT DONE after a network error:** sometimes the result that keeps the run from **DONE** says a network error lasted through the retries (`Test-DemoNetworkResult`: its evidence has `networkError`, or its message has the network failure's text). The next step is then "Run ./scripts/deploy.ps1 again: a network error lasted through the retries, and it resumes where it stopped.", not "Run ./scripts/configure-powerbi.ps1 yourself to see its output". Other failures keep their own fix.

Decided differently from the requests:

- **The order of work,** as confirmed: the re-read fix, then the track records, then the retries, then the console line and the advice.
- **Track records:** the reset, not a run ID. Records are cleared or lowered before anything writes, and the formats stay as they are. A run that starts over also clears the tracks it skips, so the next resume trusts only what was recorded since. The status page's note says the step "didn't finish" rather than "failed", because the run before can also have been stopped, or not have reached it, and its model is rewritten at the start.
- **Restore's running plugin with failing uploads** is a **Warning**, like Restore's other results; the read after it is the **Fail** that stops the step, and `start-demo.ps1` keeps a **Warning**.
- **The activity control's creates** go again only when unsent, rather than resolving by name, since they don't look their names up again.
- **The console line** is a **Warning**, and uses the status page's label, so "The history report step failed" names the row the person sees.

**Live finding: a redeploy in a later month, and the independent review of c7c86ec (2026-11-02, the same pull request).** On November 2, a plain `./scripts/deploy.ps1` on an existing environment, whose budget had been created in October, ended **NOT DONE** at the template step, about 4 minutes in. Its reason said only "DeploymentFailed ... At least one resource deployment operation failed. Please list deployment operations for details." The deployment's operations had the cause: the nested deployment `budget` failed on `Microsoft.Consumption/budgets` `budget-<prefix>-<environment>` with HTTP 400, "Start date of budgets cannot be updated. Please delete and create a new budget." `Get-DemoBudgetStartDate` read the budget (`az rest`, [Budgets - Get](https://learn.microsoft.com/rest/api/consumption/budgets/get)) as JSON, and PowerShell 7's `ConvertFrom-Json` turns ISO 8601 text into a `[datetime]`, whose `[string]` is "10/01/2026 00:00:00". The read succeeded, but its `yyyy-MM-dd` match never held, so it returned nothing, and the template sent its default, the first day of the current month. In the budget's own month that's the same date, so the bug had been hidden since the first public release; it breaks every redeploy in a later month, GitHub Actions' deploy workflow included. The review of c7c86ec (ready to merge once a final live run passes) added three Low items. The fixes, each with Pester tests that reproduced it first:

- **The budget's start date, read as text:** `az rest --method get --url <Budgets - Get> --query properties.timePeriod.startDate --output tsv` returns the text the API wrote. It's read as UTC and written as `yyyy-MM-dd` with the invariant culture, whatever the time zone, the culture, or PowerShell's date conversion. Not found (HTTP 404) means a first deployment: nothing, quietly. Any other failure, or a date that isn't the first of a month, logs one redacted **Warning** ("The start date of the budget budget-dbhub-demo couldn't be read (...)"), and the run goes on as before (R585). The budget is read even when the resource group doesn't exist, because a budget at its scope outlives the group (live, 2026-10-22).
- **The same date everywhere:** the deployment and `-Sequential` (both through `Invoke-DemoTemplateDeployment`), GitHub Actions' deploy workflow (it runs `deploy.ps1`), and preflight's what-if, which had left it to the template's default.
- **A failed template names its cause:** when `az deployment sub create` fails with `DeploymentFailed`, the deployment's failed operations are read through `Invoke-DemoAz`: [`az deployment operation sub list`](https://learn.microsoft.com/cli/azure/deployment/operation/sub#az-deployment-operation-sub-list), then [`az deployment operation group list`](https://learn.microsoft.com/cli/azure/deployment/operation/group#az-deployment-operation-group-list) for each failed nested deployment, at most 4 levels deep, 12 reads, and 5 errors ([find error codes](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-resource-manager/troubleshooting/find-error-code)). `DEPLOY.TEMPLATE`'s message gives the innermost errors, each with its resource type and name, code, and message, redacted (`Protect-DemoText`) and cut to 300 characters: "The template create failed: Microsoft.Consumption/budgets budget-dbhub-demo: 400: Start date of budgets cannot be updated. Please delete and create a new budget." The evidence keeps them and the Azure CLI's own error. When the operations can't be read, the message is the CLI's error, as before. A validation error, such as `InvalidTemplateDeployment`, already names its cause, so nothing is read for it. In GitHub Actions, the IDs in the text are masked as in any other message (ADR 0022). T3's free-offer retry and T4's Burstable fallback read the CLI's error as before, and tests prove both still trigger.
- **This error's fix:** the remediation says to delete `budget-<prefix>-<environment>` at the scope of `rg-<prefix>-<environment>` (Cost Management > Budgets, or [Budgets - Delete](https://learn.microsoft.com/rest/api/consumption/budgets/delete)), then run `./scripts/deploy.ps1` again, which creates it anew from the first day of this month, with the same amount and alerts. It was checked against the documentation and the code, not live, since this work made no Azure calls. After the delete, the read answers 404, so the template sends the current month's first day to a budget that doesn't exist, which [Budgets - Create Or Update](https://learn.microsoft.com/rest/api/consumption/budgets/create-or-update) accepts. Destroy's `DESTROY.BUDGET` deletes the same budget, and the next deployment creates it again (live, 2026-10-22). Other failures keep the generic remediation.
- **A lasting outage costs one request's retries, not one per item or poll (review A):** `Invoke-DemoRestRequest`'s answer, `Get-DemoArmResource`, and `Invoke-DemoHubFabricGet` now say when a network error stopped a request (`NetworkError`).
  - The wait for a new workspace's capacity (`Wait-DemoFabricCapacityAssignment`) counts elapsed time, its reads included, and stops at the first read a network error stopped. `FABRIC.RESOLVE.WORKSPACE` (or `FABRIC.RESOLVE.CAPACITY`) is then a **Fail** with `networkError` in its evidence, so **NOT DONE** says to run `./scripts/deploy.ps1` again. With a mocked DNS failure: 4 requests, where the review measured 244 and about 56 minutes.
  - The policy inventory stops reading definitions after the first one a network error stopped, counts the rest as unreadable ("N more definition(s) and set(s), not read after the network error"), and logs one **Warning** that says why: 4 requests for 20 definitions, where the review measured 80 and about 17 minutes.
  - The notebook run's wait counts `-TimeoutMinutes` in elapsed time too, since a read that keeps getting HTTP 503 pays its retries on every poll.
  - Checked and fine: the Fabric and Hub list reads, which return at the first failure; the long-running operation and refresh polls, which return at the first answer that isn't a success, while a network error throws; the Hub telemetry and T3 readiness waits, which already count elapsed time; and the few calls made once per identity, member, or item (the teardown's group check, the T3 administrators group's adds, the activity control's deletes). No per-host circuit breaker in `Invoke-DemoTransientRetry`: a later, unrelated request in the same process still gets its own attempts.
- **R584's message (review B):** the monitoring state's reads tell a failed read from one that found nothing. An `az` call for the SqlIaasExtension, the VM, or a resource provider's registration that fails with anything but not found goes into `ReadErrors`, and `T1.SQLVM.MONITORING` is a **Fail**, "Azure couldn't be read for sqlvm01's monitoring: the SqlIaasExtension on sqlvm01: ...", with the redacted error, and "Run ./scripts/deploy.ps1 again: it resumes at T1's Monitoring step (or run ./scripts/configure-monitoring.ps1 again)." It had said "sqlvm01 has no SqlIaasExtension", "has no system-assigned managed identity", or "Microsoft.AzureArcData is Unknown". The read after the agent's restart returns that **Fail**, with the restart in its evidence.
- **The network error's wording (review C):** "A network error stopped it (requests that are safe to repeat were retried).", in `NOT DONE`'s next step, a Fabric script step's, an import's, and the new workspace and capacity **Fails**, where it had said "lasted through the retries", which isn't true for a request that was sent once.

The audit for the same kind of bug, a date read from parsed JSON, then cast to `[string]` and matched, compared as text, or parsed as ISO, in `scripts/`: `Get-DemoBudgetStartDate` was the only one that fed logic wrongly. Correct as they are: T4's refusal record, the opt-in Key Vault secret's expiry, the Fabric app's latest day, the readers that go through `ConvertTo-DemoUtcDateTime`, `ConvertTo-DemoUtcDate`, or the Run Command time conversion, the golden snapshot's age, the policy cache (CliXml, not JSON), the run's own times (in memory), and the date-only `expiresOn` tag, which stays text. The resource group's state tags (the guided stop's, the host password's, T2's build), Bastion's expiry tag, the golden snapshot's tags, and the guest's evaluation expiry are written with `Z`: they come back as a UTC `[datetime]` whose `[string]` the readers' invariant, assume-UTC parse reads as the same time. Left as they are, because they're only shown or kept as evidence: the status's stage time, Bastion's "stays until", the Fabric capacity's resume time, `DEPLOY.TIMING`'s times, and the Arc usage upload time. Left for a follow-up: `Get-DemoResourceGroupTag` casts each tag's value to `[string]`, so a template redeploy writes an ISO date in a tag, a person's own tags included, back as "MM/dd/yyyy HH:mm:ss" (`existingResourceGroupTags`); every reader in the module parses both forms, and the helper has about a dozen callers.

Changed files: the module's `DeployHelpers.ps1`, `AzContext.ps1`, `PolicyHelpers.ps1`, `HubFabricHelpers.ps1`, `FabricResolveHelpers.ps1`, `SqlVmMonitoringHelpers.ps1`, `TransientRetry.ps1`, `TrackHelpers.ps1`, and `DeployExperience.ps1`, and `Invoke-DemoTemplateDeployment`, `Invoke-DemoPreflight`, `Get-DemoPolicyConstraint`, `Resolve-DemoFabricConfig`, `Invoke-DemoFabricNotebookRun`, `Import-DemoFabricItem`, and `Invoke-DemoSemanticModelRefresh`; the tests for the lifecycle, preflight, the retries, the tracks, Fabric resolution, and the Fabric track; and the CHANGELOG, this report (R582, R584, and R585), troubleshooting, deployment, and references. No Bicep changed.

Validation, 2026-11-02: the 22 new or changed tests that reproduce a change failed on c7c86ec and pass now: `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle` 11 (the budget's start date 4, the failed template's cause 5, and the monitoring read 2), `DbHubDemo.TransientRetry` 7 (the outage 4, the wording 3), and `DbHubDemo.Tracks`, `DbHubDemo.Preflight`, `DbHubDemo.FabricResolve`, and `DbHubDemo.FabricTrack` 1 each. Two new tests pass on both versions, because they guard what didn't change: a budget that doesn't exist yet stays quiet, and T3's free-offer retry still triggers. `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check PSScriptAnalyzer, Pester, Markdown` with strict mode: PSScriptAnalyzer **Pass** (no findings in 326 files), Pester **Pass** (2,859 tests passed, none failed, 7 skipped by their own conditions), and markdownlint **Pass** (no issues in 128 files). The 23 affected test files (the six above, Arc, the policies, Fabric, its items, the Hub's estate, Power BI, one command, the progress model, the Actions masks, redaction, the free offer, T4, the conventions, function names, neutral wording, the lifecycle scripts, and the walkthrough), with `GITHUB_ACTIONS` and `CI` set: **Pass** (1,040 tests). The public push scan of `origin/main..HEAD` found 0 hits.

Decided differently from the requests:

- **The budget is read even when the resource group doesn't exist,** not only when it does: a budget at the group's scope outlives the group.
- **The operations are read only for `DeploymentFailed`.** A validation error already names its cause, and the deployment record of the same name can then be an older run's.
- **Loops of the same shape** got caller fixes, with a third one the review didn't list (the notebook run's wait), rather than a per-host short-circuit.
- **R584** got the fix, not only new wording: it was small.

**The review of 76d10b2 (2026-11-02, the same pull request).** Checked live on 76d10b2: the existing budget's start date reads as 2026-10-01, and a resource group that doesn't exist gives nothing, with no **Warning**. The independent review of 76d10b2 (ready to merge once the live run passes) found two Low regressions in it (9/10 each), fixed with Pester tests that reproduced them first:

- **The capacity wait gave up on a short outage:** `Wait-DemoFabricCapacityAssignment` returned at the first read a network error stopped, after about 50 seconds with instant failures. So a Fabric outage longer than that, right after the workspace was created or assigned, failed the Fabric step (on the assign path, a **Fail** where it had been the **Warning** "still being assigned"), where the wait had read on through its 300 seconds and recovered. Now a read that fails, a network error included, counts as unknown, and the wait goes on; its elapsed-time deadline still bounds it, at about 300 seconds plus one read's attempts. `NetworkError` comes only when the deadline passes with no read that came back, so otherwise the old **Pass** and **Warning** apply. With a fake clock and instant DNS failures, a 70-second outage (6 failed attempts) is waited out, and the assignment passes at 75 seconds; one that outlasts the window ends with `NetworkError` at 325 seconds, after 24 requests (R582 updated).
- **A Microsoft.Sql registration that couldn't be read failed T1's monitoring:** `Get-DemoSqlVmMonitoringState` put a failed `az provider show --namespace Microsoft.Sql` in `ReadErrors`, so `T1.SQLVM.MONITORING` became the **Fail** "Azure couldn't be read", which stops the T1 Monitoring step and blocks DONE, while an unregistered Microsoft.Sql is only a **Warning**. That read is out of `ReadErrors` again: it stays 'Unknown', with that **Warning**. Microsoft.AzureArcData's read stays in: an unregistered Microsoft.AzureArcData is a **Fail** too, so a failed read isn't worse, and its message says what happened, with running `deploy.ps1` again as the fix, rather than registering a provider that may well be registered (R584 updated).

Changed files: the module's `FabricResolveHelpers.ps1` and `SqlVmMonitoringHelpers.ps1`, and `Resolve-DemoFabricConfig` (comments); the tests for the retries, Fabric resolution, and the lifecycle; and the CHANGELOG, this report (R582 and R584), deployment, and troubleshooting.

The 15 affected test files (the three above, Arc, the tracks, the Fabric track, Fabric, one command, the Actions masks, redaction, the conventions, function names, neutral wording, the lifecycle scripts, and the walkthrough), with `GITHUB_ACTIONS` and `CI` set: **Pass** (787 tests). The public push scan of `origin/main..HEAD` found 0 hits.

### Phase 28 follow-up: destroy's leftover check, tags, and control Run Commands

2026-11-02. A proof environment with environment `demo` and another prefix was destroyed with `destroy.ps1` in a subscription that also holds the owner's `rg-dbhub-demo`. The destroy itself was correct: it deleted only the proof's Arc resources and resource group, and `rg-dbhub-demo` saw no delete operation. But the residual check, `DESTROY.RESIDUAL`, failed on false positives. The coordinator also found a managed Run Command from a 2026-10-27 run of the on-demand activity control still on `sqlvm01`, and two minor defects came up the same day. No Azure, Fabric, or Graph call was made for this work; the coordinator runs the live checks.

**Work summary.**

1. **Only this environment** ([cleanup](cleanup.md#only-this-environment)): `TEARDOWN.RESIDUAL.TAGGED` had listed 24 resources and groups of the other environment (`rg-dbhub-demo`, its VMs, its PostgreSQL server, and its shutdown schedules), matched by `solution` `fabric-database-hub-demo` with `environment` `demo`, and `.ARC` its Arc machine `arcsql01`. The check now matches the exact group name `rg-<prefix>-<environment>` and the manifest's `deploymentId` tag only, and Arc resources also by their group or hosting machine (`containerResourceId`); never the solution and environment tags alone, never a resource's name alone. Without a manifest, `TAGGED` looks for the group by name and is a **Warning**. `validate.ps1 -Area Teardown` had the same query: it now matches the `deploymentId` only, and is a **Warning** after a destroy without `-DeploymentId`.
2. **Resource Graph lag** ([cleanup](cleanup.md#resource-graph-lags-behind-deletes)): after the group was deleted, Resource Graph listed its ten `sqlserverinstances/databases` rows for more than 30 minutes, which failed `GROUPRESOURCES` and `ARC`. Once the group is gone, each row is confirmed with Azure Resource Manager ([Resources - Get By Id](https://learn.microsoft.com/rest/api/resources/resources/get-by-id), through `Invoke-DemoRestRequest`), at most 25 reads for the whole check (`-MaxResourceReads`), with the API version pinned in `config/versions.json` or the provider's newest stable one ([Providers - Get](https://learn.microsoft.com/rest/api/resources/providers/get), now pinned at 2021-04-01, one read per namespace). HTTP 404 with `ResourceNotFound`, `ResourceGroupNotFound`, or `ParentResourceNotFound` ([not-found errors](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-resource-manager/troubleshooting/error-not-found)) makes the row a stale index record, reported under `TEARDOWN.RESIDUAL.RECORDS` ("Only index records remain"); a resource Resource Manager returns is a **Fail**; a row it couldn't confirm within the bound, or answered with anything else, is a **Warning**. Rows found gone don't keep the poll waiting.
3. **A role assignment a tenant policy made** ([cleanup](cleanup.md#role-assignments-a-tenant-policy-made)): what `assignRoles` creates was checked. For the demo's managed identities, only the Arc onboarding identity's Azure Connected Machine Onboarding on the group (`infra/modules/archost01.bicep`); its other role assignments go to presenters (Reader on the subscription and the Hybrid Database Administrator read-only role on the group), Key Vault operators and readers on the vault, the GitHub identity, and the control's identity, none of them in the manifest. So an assignment at or under the group, or one for a manifest identity in `rg-<prefix>-control` or `rg-<prefix>-golden`, is still a **Fail**; one for a manifest identity anywhere else is a **Warning** that names its scope (evidence `orphaned`) and says that an Owner, User Access Administrator, or Role Based Access Control Administrator can remove it ([remove role assignments](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/role-based-access-control/role-assignments-remove), ["Identity not found"](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/role-based-access-control/troubleshooting#symptom---role-assignments-with-identity-not-found)). Nothing deletes it.
4. **A read-only rerun:** `tests/teardown/Invoke-TeardownTest.ps1 -ConfigPath <file> -ManifestPath <file>` existed, but as Pester tests, which turn the status model into pass or fail. `./scripts/destroy.ps1 -ConfigPath <file> -ResidueOnly -ManifestPath <file>` (a parameter set of its own) runs only the residual check with the saved manifest: `DESTROY.MANIFEST` (**Pass**; **Fail** for another group's manifest or an unreadable one, and then nothing runs; **Warning** without one), the `TEARDOWN.RESIDUAL.*` checks, and `DESTROY.RESIDUAL`, written to `out/destroy-residue-<environment>.jsonl`, exit code 1 only on a **Fail**. It deletes, asks, and writes nothing else. The live Pester tests mark unconfirmed rows inconclusive and accept the orphaned assignment's **Warning**.
5. **Tags round-trip:** `Get-DemoResourceGroupTag` read the group's tags with PowerShell 7's `ConvertFrom-Json`, which turns ISO 8601 text into dates, then cast them to text, `MM/dd/yyyy HH:mm:ss`; a redeploy's template carried that back, a person's own tags included. It now reads `az group show`'s JSON with `System.Text.Json` (`ConvertFrom-DemoJsonStringMap`), so every value comes back as Azure holds it, and still throws on JSON it can't read. The readers of the demo's date tags (`ConvertTo-DemoRunCommandUtc`, the host restart guard) accept both forms, tested in `de-DE` and a non-UTC time zone.
6. **`ConvertTo-DemoDeploymentOutput`** threw under strict mode for an empty `outputs` object (member enumeration of `.Name` on nothing); it returns no outputs now.
7. **The control's Run Commands:** the dispatch already deleted the earlier `DbHubControl-*` Run Commands on each VM a run or reset used (`Clear-DbHubOldRunCommand`), but never on a VM no later run used, and the stop path never touches Run Command ([ADR 0020](adr/0020-on-demand-activity-control.md)). Now a run or reset also deletes the finished ones (execution state Succeeded, Failed, TimedOut, or Canceled, or an hour past the time in the name) on the other demo VM when it runs, without waiting; `start-demo.ps1` deletes the finished ones on the VMs that run (`START.CONTROL.RUNCOMMANDS`: a **Warning** when a list or delete fails, for example on a deallocated VM, retried next time, never a **Fail**); and `status.ps1` reports any `DbHubControl-*`, and any `DbHubDemo-*` other than the bootstrap loader, as a **Warning** with the `az vm run-command delete` command (`STATUS.RUNCOMMANDS`, read-only). The control's custom role already has `Microsoft.Compute/virtualMachines/runCommands/delete`. A VM allows 25 managed Run Commands ([Run Command overview](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/virtual-machines/run-command-overview); [List By Virtual Machine](https://learn.microsoft.com/rest/api/compute/virtual-machine-run-commands/list-by-virtual-machine); [Delete](https://learn.microsoft.com/rest/api/compute/virtual-machine-run-commands/delete)).

**What the control's Run Commands carry.** The runbook's PUT body has `location` and `properties` with only `source.script`, `asyncExecution`, `treatFailureAsDeploymentFailure`, and `timeoutInSeconds`: no `parameters`, no `protectedParameters`, no blob URIs, and no run-as user, which a new unit test pins. The script holds the module's workload-control template and the validated request (allow-listed scenario names, durations, end times, and the requester), no secret. That matches the coordinator's read of the leftover. The operator's Run Commands (`Invoke-DemoVmRunCommand`) put a sensitive value only in `protectedParameters`, written to a private temporary file and registered for redaction, and refuse public values outside a safe character set. Nothing sensitive needs to move.

**Changed files.** `scripts/destroy.ps1`; the module's `Test-DemoTeardownResidue`, `Remove-DemoEnvironment`, `Start-DemoEnvironment`, and `Get-DemoEnvironmentStatus`, and `Private/TeardownHelpers.ps1`, `ArcAzureHelpers.ps1`, `DeployHelpers.ps1`, `RunCommandHelpers.ps1`, and `ValidationSecurity.ps1`, and `Kql/ResourceGraph.Residual.kql`; `automation/runbooks/DbHubControl.Common.ps1`; `config/versions.json` (`Microsoft.Resources/providers`); the tests `DbHubDemo.Teardown.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`, `Automation.Runbooks.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Validation.Tests.ps1`, `Repo.LifecycleScripts.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.T4.Tests.ps1` (its mock of `az group show`), and the live `tests/teardown/Teardown.Residue.Tests.ps1` with its README; and the README, CHANGELOG, this report (R339, R586–R591, acceptance item 9), cleanup, troubleshooting, deployment, the automation support matrix, `automation/README.md`, and ADRs 0002, 0012, and 0020.

**Validation.** Static, 2026-11-02: `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check PSScriptAnalyzer, Pester, Markdown -CI`, with strict mode as in CI: PSScriptAnalyzer **Pass** (no findings in 326 files), Pester **Pass** (2,885 tests passed, none failed, and 7 skipped by their own conditions), and markdownlint **Pass** (no issues in 128 files). The affected test files again with `GITHUB_ACTIONS` and `CI` set to `true`: **Pass** (625 tests in 10 files: teardown, lifecycle, runbooks, validation, lifecycle scripts, T4, the activity control, conventions, neutral wording, and function names). The 28 new or changed tests that reproduce a change failed on `origin/main` (9f1babe) and pass now: teardown 14 (another prefix's environment, the exact scope, no manifest, the demo's own role assignments, the policy's orphaned one, index records, the bound of 25 reads, a resource that's still there and another error, the remediation when the check can't run, and five for `-ResidueOnly`), lifecycle 9 (the tags in `de-DE` with a non-UTC time zone, the empty outputs, `start-demo.ps1`'s cleanup, `status.ps1`'s list of checks, and five for the leftover Run Commands), runbooks 2 (after a run, and after a reset), validation 2, and lifecycle scripts 1. On `origin/main`, 26 existing start and status tests fail too, only because they now mock the new helpers; the test of both tag forms and the audit of the Run Command's body pass on both, as guards. Bicep, PSRule, the .NET tests, and the Fabric App weren't changed and weren't run locally; CI runs them. The public push scan of `origin/main..HEAD` found 0 hits. Live, 2026-11-02 (the coordinator, on 7fb941e): `./scripts/destroy.ps1 -ConfigPath <the proof's configuration> -ResidueOnly -ManifestPath <its manifest>`, with `rg-dbhub-demo` present, passed.

**Decided differently from the brief, and why.**

- `DbHub-Stop` still doesn't delete Run Commands: ADR 0020 keeps the stop path free of Run Command, deletes took more than 10 minutes under load (Phase 25a), and a deallocated VM can't have one removed. The cleanup happens at the start of the next run or reset, on every running demo VM, and in `start-demo.ps1`.
- "The dispatch never deletes its Run Commands" was only partly so: it deleted the earlier ones on the VMs each run used. The leftover on `sqlvm01` stayed because no later run used that VM.
- `validate.ps1 -Area Teardown` had the same scope as the residual check, so it's fixed too.
- Role assignments aren't confirmed with Azure Resource Manager; only Resource Graph's resource rows are.

**Risks.** R339 (updated) and R586–R591.

**The review of 7fb941e (2026-11-02, the same pull request).** The independent review found one Medium issue (8/10, reproduced) and two small items; everything else does what the pull request says, and the coordinator's live `-ResidueOnly` check on 7fb941e passed. Each code fix came with Pester tests that reproduced it first:

1. **The manifest guard didn't check the subscription.** `Read-DemoTeardownManifest` and `Resolve-DemoTeardownManifest` compared only the resource group, but the default path, `out/destroy-manifest-<environment>.json`, is shared by every subscription with the same prefix and environment. So another subscription's destroy of `rg-dbhub-demo` could leave its manifest there: `-ResidueOnly` then passed `DESTROY.MANIFEST` and checked that environment's `deploymentId` and identities, and a destroy's rerun reused them. Now a manifest whose `SubscriptionId` isn't the configured subscription (compared without regard to case) is never used: `-ResidueOnly` reports `DESTROY.MANIFEST` **Fail**, naming both subscriptions (masked in GitHub Actions, ADR 0022), and checks nothing; a destroy replaces it, with a **Warning** in its log; and the live Pester tests stop on it. A manifest without `SubscriptionId` is still judged by its resource group.
2. **Which Run Commands count as finished.** `start-demo.ps1` counted an empty or Unknown execution state as finished, so it could delete a `DbHubControl-*` Run Command that a control run had created seconds earlier. Now it deletes one only when the state is terminal (Succeeded, Failed, Canceled, or TimedOut), or empty or Unknown more than an hour after the time in its name (`Test-DemoRunCommandFinished`), and `status.ps1` doesn't count an unfinished one on a running VM.
3. **README wording:** the default path (`assignRoles` false) creates no role assignments; it had said that the demo creates none.

Changed files: the module's `Private/TeardownHelpers.ps1` and `RunCommandHelpers.ps1` and `Remove-DemoEnvironment`, and `scripts/destroy.ps1` (its help and a comment); the tests for teardown and the lifecycle, and the live `tests/teardown/Teardown.Residue.Tests.ps1` with its README; and the README, CHANGELOG, this report (R589 and R591), cleanup, troubleshooting, deployment, `automation/README.md`, and ADR 0020.

Validation, 2026-11-02: `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check PSScriptAnalyzer, Pester, Markdown -CI` with strict mode: PSScriptAnalyzer **Pass** (no findings in 326 files), Pester **Pass** (2,903 tests passed, none failed, 7 skipped by their own conditions), and markdownlint **Pass** (no issues in 128 files); the affected test files again with `GITHUB_ACTIONS` and `CI` set to `true`: **Pass** (502 tests in 7 files: teardown, lifecycle, lifecycle scripts, runbooks, conventions, neutral wording, and function names). The 3 new tests that reproduce the review's findings failed on 7fb941e and pass now: teardown 2 (another subscription's manifest of the same group, with `-ResidueOnly`, where `DESTROY.MANIFEST` passed, and on a destroy's rerun, which checked deploymentId `zzz999`) and lifecycle 1 (a Run Command an overlapping control run has just created, which 7fb941e deleted). The 12 cases of the new `Test-DemoRunCommandFinished` pass now; 3 tests that a manifest of this subscription, in another case or without its ID, is still used pass on both. The public push scan of `origin/main..HEAD` found 0 hits.

Decided differently from the review's suggestions: the default manifest path keeps its name. Putting the subscription in it would move the manifests already written, the proof's included, away from where the scripts and docs look, and need a fallback. With the subscription checked, a shared path costs the other environment its saved manifest, never a false **Pass**, and the docs say to give each environment its own `-ManifestPath` (R591). The module's rule for a finished Run Command is the review's: Pending and Running never count. The runbooks keep theirs, unchanged in this round: once the hour has passed, they count any state that isn't terminal, so they also clear a stale Pending or Running one (R589).

### Phase 28 follow-up: T4's General Purpose fallback

2026-11-02. One subscription in West US 3 refused both of T4's Burstable sizes with `SkuNotAvailable` on every run from 2026-10-29 to 2026-11-02, so Phase 28's fallback always finished without T4, while an environment whose configuration set `t4SkuName` to `Standard_D2ds_v5` ran fine on the General Purpose tier. The owner's decision (2026-11-02): when both Burstable sizes are refused, fall back automatically to General Purpose `Standard_D2ds_v5`, within four guardrails ([ADR 0019](adr/0019-t4-postgresql-flexible-server.md#2026-11-02-the-general-purpose-fallback-phase-28-follow-up)).

**Work summary.**

- **Only on `SkuNotAvailable` (guardrail 1):** the template step moves the fallback on only when the T4 deployment's failed operations say `SkuNotAvailable` for the size the attempt sent: `Standard_B1ms`, then `Standard_B2s`, then `Standard_D2ds_v5`, then no T4 (`Refused`). A quota limit, a policy denial, or a transient error fails the template as before. A `t4SkuName` the configuration sets always wins (`Test-DemoT4SkuExplicit`: a size the fallback fills in carries `t4SkuSource`, which the schema doesn't allow in the file), and `t4Enabled: false` reads no record and prices nothing.
- **Priced before creating (guardrail 2):** right before every General Purpose creation on the fallback's behalf, `Get-DemoT4GeneralPurposeCost` runs the demo's estimate with T4 at `Standard_D2ds_v5` from the Retail Prices API, with the subscription's Defender for Cloud plans and the T2 golden snapshots. `ConvertTo-DemoT4FallbackCost` allows it only with a compute price, the light-use month plus the rest of the month's T4 compute within `monthlyCeilingUsd` (the worst case, because a stopped server starts again on its own after seven days; T4 alone is its fixed costs plus 730 hours), and a session within `sessionCeilingUsd`. What the API had no price for is named, and so is Defender for Cloud when its plans weren't read. Otherwise T4 is left out (`DEPLOY.T4.SKU` **Warning**, record `None`) with the ceiling and the value to raise it to. Preflight's `PREFLIGHT.T4.FALLBACK` prices it ahead: from the run's own estimate when the fallback starts there (a record, or a region that lists neither Burstable size), with the ceilings then checked without T4 when it doesn't fit, so preflight doesn't fail; otherwise from a second estimate that shares the first one's prices (`Get-DemoCostEstimate -PriceCache`), a **Pass** or a **Warning**.
- **Said loudly (guardrail 3):** a console line (`Write-DemoLog`, a Warning), the status page's Infrastructure step, and the **DONE** and **NOT DONE** banners say `T4 created as General Purpose Standard_D2ds_v5 because Burstable sizes were refused here; about <price> USD an hour while running (list price in <region>, Azure Retail Prices API)`, with the price the check read, or for a later run one read then (`Complete-DemoDeploymentRun`); the banner adds the 7-day restart. `stop-demo.ps1` (`STOP.T4.STOP`) and `status.ps1` (`STATUS.T4`) give the date and time Azure starts a stopped T4 again, the stop plus seven days (an earlier stop from the activity log), what it then bills at its own size, and whether the on-demand control's daily safety stop stops it again; `STATUS.T4` also names a General Purpose size and its hourly price.
- **Converges (guardrail 4):** `DbHubT4Sku` records `Standard_D2ds_v5` in the resource group's tags at once and in the tags the template carries, and `Resolve-DemoEffectiveConfig` gives later runs that size, so a redeploy never tries `Standard_B1ms` again. An existing server keeps its own size, whatever the record says and whatever created it, unless `t4SkuName` is set (`DEPLOY.T4.SKU` **Pass** when they differ), and a record that doesn't match it is cleared (the review of 20e855c, below; it had followed the server). A Phase 28 record of `None` gets the fallback, cost check first, when the run deploys the template; otherwise T4 stays out, so `start-demo.ps1`'s recreation of T3 no longer tries to create T4. The template was checked for both tiers: the tier follows the size, and 32 GiB of Premium SSD at P4 without auto-grow, 7-day backups without geo-redundancy, high availability off, no IOPS setting, and Query Store off are valid on both, so only descriptions changed.

**Changed files.** New: `tests/unit/DbHubDemo.T4Fallback.Tests.ps1`, one Describe per guardrail. Changed: the module's `T4Helpers.ps1`, `DeployExperience.ps1`, `ParallelDeployment.ps1`, `ValidationAreas.ps1`, and `DeployHelpers.ps1` (a comment), and `Invoke-DemoTemplateDeployment`, `Invoke-DemoPreflight`, `Invoke-DemoDeployment`, `Get-DemoCostEstimate` (`-PriceCache`), `Complete-DemoDeploymentRun`, `Get-DemoEnvironmentStatus`, `Start-DemoEnvironment`, `Test-DemoT4`, and, in their help, `Stop-DemoEnvironment` and `Get-DemoDeploymentState`; the help of `scripts/status.ps1` and `scripts/stop-demo.ps1`; `t4SkuName`'s description in `config/environment.schema.json`, `infra/main.bicep`, `infra/modules/demo.bicep`, and `infra/modules/pgflex01.bicep`, and `azuredeploy.json` rebuilt with Bicep 0.47.16; the tests for T4, preflight, cost, the lifecycle, and the tracks; and the README, CHANGELOG, this report (R441 updated, R592 to R600), cost management, deployment, troubleshooting, limitations, the architecture, the automation support matrix, and ADRs 0018 and 0019 with the ADR index.

**Validation.** Static, 2026-11-02: `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check PSScriptAnalyzer, Pester, Markdown, Bicep`, with strict mode as in CI: PSScriptAnalyzer **Pass** (no findings in 327 files), Pester **Pass** (2,907 tests passed, none failed, and 7 skipped by their own conditions), Bicep **Pass** (`infra/main.bicep` and the two standalone templates in `infra/control` build and lint cleanly, and `azuredeploy.json`, rebuilt with the pinned Bicep 0.47.16, is in sync), and markdownlint **Pass** (no issues in 128 files). The new behavior has 45 new tests, 39 of them in `DbHubDemo.T4Fallback.Tests.ps1`, and 10 tests changed with it. The 19 affected test files (the fallback, T4, preflight, cost, the lifecycle, the tracks, one command, the progress model, validation, the free offer, disk drift, the Actions masks, redaction, the configuration, the walkthrough, the conventions, function names, neutral wording, and the lifecycle scripts), with `GITHUB_ACTIONS` and `CI` set: **Pass** (896 tests). The whole unit suite also ran once with the Retail Prices request helper replaced by one that fails and logs every call: no test reached the API. The public push scan of `origin/main..HEAD` found 0 hits. Live: not run for the create path; the owner merged after the review below and a free preflight check (R600, R601).

**Decided differently from the request, and why.**

- **`estimate-cost.ps1` prices the configuration as written:** it never signs in and doesn't read the resource group's record, so it prices `t4SkuName` or the default; `deploy.ps1` (its preflight and its template step) and `start-demo.ps1` price the size the fallback chose, and cost management says how to see the General Purpose price with the script. A standalone `preflight.ps1` doesn't read the record either (review of 20e855c, below).
- **Preflight prices the General Purpose step even when the run starts at a Burstable size,** as a **Pass** or a **Warning** (`PREFLIGHT.T4.FALLBACK`), because West US 3 listed the Burstable sizes and still refused them at creation; `deploy.ps1` prices it again right before it would create it, which is what decides. Its second estimate shares the first one's prices, so it adds one price read, not a full estimate's.
- **A session over `sessionCeilingUsd` leaves T4 out** like the monthly ceiling, as the request says, although a session above it is only a **Warning** elsewhere.
- **A Phase 28 record of `None` is checked again** on the next run that deploys the template, rather than kept: the owner's decision covers environments that already finished without T4. And a new record value, `Refused`, keeps the fallback from trying again after Azure refuses `Standard_D2ds_v5` too.
- **No Azure, Fabric, or Graph call was made** for this work: the figures here are the dated examples of 2026-10-23 and 2026-10-26, labeled as such, and the unit tests stub the Retail Prices API.

**The review of 20e855c (2026-11-02, the same pull request).** The independent review of 20e855c passed the full suite (2,907 tests), found that no test reached the network and that the rebuilt `azuredeploy.json` is byte-identical, and found two Medium bugs and two Low items, fixed in one commit, b677393, each with Pester tests that fail on 20e855c; `main` was then merged in after pull request 10. The coordinator's free preflight check passed live (below). **The owner chose to merge after the review and that check, without a live proof:** the fallback's create path (B1ms, then B2s refused, then `Standard_D2ds_v5` created) and keeping the size on a redeploy haven't run live, so the first real fallback run is to be watched (R600, R601).

- **A failed extra estimate no longer drops a monthly-ceiling Fail (Medium):** `Invoke-DemoPreflight` added the run's own cost results only after `Get-DemoT4FallbackPreflight` returned, and that function's second `Get-DemoCostEstimate` had no catch. So an HTTP 429 after the retries left only a `PREFLIGHT.COST.SESSION` **Warning**: a `PREFLIGHT.COST.MONTHLY` **Fail** already computed was lost, preflight had no **Fail**, and `deploy.ps1` went past the hard ceiling. Now the run's own estimate and the fallback's check each have their own catch, and the run's own results are always added. The fallback's estimates don't throw any more: on a Burstable start, a failed estimate is a `PREFLIGHT.T4.FALLBACK` **Warning** with `skipReason` `Price` ("deploy.ps1 prices its General Purpose fallback, Standard_D2ds_v5, right before it would create it; right now, its price couldn't be read from the Azure Retail Prices API (...)"); on a General Purpose start over a ceiling, a failed estimate without T4 keeps the ceilings checked with T4 at `Standard_D2ds_v5`, and the **Warning** says so; anything else the check throws is a `PREFLIGHT.T4.FALLBACK` **Warning** with `skipReason` `Price`, beside the run's own results.
- **"Created" only with evidence that the server exists (Medium):** the record `Standard_D2ds_v5` is written once the cost check passes, before the attempt, and convergence needs it that early. But **DONE**, **NOT DONE**, and the status page read only the record, so a creation that then failed for another reason (a quota, a policy denial, a transient error) was announced as "T4 created as General Purpose ...", with its price, on every later run. Now `Get-DemoT4ServerEvidence` reads this run's results and the T4 track's record: a `DEPLOY.TEMPLATE` **Pass** with T4 in the template, a `T4.SERVER` **Pass** or **Warning**, a `HUB.ESTATE.T4` or `METRICS.T4` **Pass**, or a T4 track that finished a step mean the server exists; a `T4.SERVER` **Fail**, or this run's fallback creating T4 with a `DEPLOY.TEMPLATE` **Fail**, mean it doesn't. When nothing says, `Complete-DemoDeploymentRun` reads it ([Servers - Get](https://learn.microsoft.com/rest/api/postgresql/servers/get), through `Get-DemoT4State`). Only a server that exists gets the "created" line. Otherwise: "T4 isn't created yet: the fallback chose General Purpose Standard_D2ds_v5 because Burstable sizes were refused here, about `<price>` USD an hour while running (...), but this run's template deployment failed (DEPLOY.TEMPLATE says why). The next ./scripts/deploy.ps1 that deploys the template creates it, after checking its price against the ceilings again.", and a read that fails says it couldn't be confirmed. `status.ps1` already reported a missing server; it now adds the size the fallback chose. And the template no longer sets a record to an existing server's size: a record that doesn't match the server is cleared, so only the fallback ever writes a size, and a `Standard_D2ds_v5` server created through `t4SkuName`, later removed, has no record and no "refused" claim (`STATUS.T4`: "It's General Purpose Standard_D2ds_v5: ...").
- **The cost check again after the T3 free offer's retry (Low):** when Azure rejects the T3 free offer and the template step deploys again without it, a General Purpose check that already ran is reset, and its `DEPLOY.T4.SKU` result removed, so the next attempt prices T4 again without the free offer's allowance and leaves T4 out (record `None`) when that's over a ceiling.
- **The safety stop from its schedule (Low):** `Get-DemoT4SafetyStopState` reads the schedule `dbhub-t4-safety-stop` and its link to `DbHub-Stop` as `CONTROL.SAFETYSTOP` did ([Schedule - Get](https://learn.microsoft.com/rest/api/automation/schedule/get), [Job Schedule - List By Automation Account](https://learn.microsoft.com/rest/api/automation/job-schedule/list-by-automation-account)): `Scheduled`, `NotScheduled`, `Unknown` (a read that failed), or `Off` (`control.enabled` false; nothing is read). `CONTROL.SAFETYSTOP`, `stop-demo.ps1` (`STOP.T4.STOP`), and `status.ps1` (`STATUS.T4`) use it. "until the on-demand control's daily safety stop stops it again, within a day" comes only with `Scheduled`; otherwise they say it isn't scheduled, with `setup-activity-control.ps1` to schedule it, or that it couldn't be read. Their evidence's `dailySafetyStop` is true only with `Scheduled`, and `safetyStop` gives the state. **DONE**'s note, which reads no schedule, says it as a condition: "stops it again within a day when its schedule is in place".
- **Preflight and the record (docs):** only `deploy.ps1`'s preflight starts from the record, because `deploy.ps1` resolves the configuration first; a standalone `preflight.ps1` doesn't read it, so it prices an existing server's own size, or the configuration with the General Purpose step ahead. Cost management, deployment, ADR 0019, and this report say so.

Changed files: the module's `T4Helpers.ps1`, `ActivityControlHelpers.ps1`, and `DeployExperience.ps1`, and `Invoke-DemoPreflight`, `Invoke-DemoTemplateDeployment`, `Complete-DemoDeploymentRun`, `Get-DemoEnvironmentStatus`, and `Test-DemoActivityControl`; the tests for the fallback, preflight, T4, the lifecycle, and the activity control; and the README, CHANGELOG, this report (R596 and R600 updated, R601), cost management, deployment, troubleshooting, and ADR 0019. No Bicep changed.

Validation, 2026-11-02:
