- **The coordinator's free preflight check, live, on 20e855c** (a preflight, which creates nothing): exit 0, 27 **Pass**, no **Fail**. `PREFLIGHT.T4.FALLBACK` **Pass**: `Standard_D2ds_v5` at about 0.178 USD an hour in West US 3 from the Retail Prices API, read live on 2026-11-02; its worst case 154.23 USD a month for T4 and 223.38 USD for the demo's month, against the 250 USD ceiling; a session 10.30 USD, against 15 USD.
- **Reproduced first:** 27 tests are new or changed. 25 of them fail on 20e855c and pass now: the fallback 13, the activity control 5, preflight 3, T4 3, and the lifecycle 1. The other 2 pass on both, because they guard what didn't change: a record that matches the server stays, and a Burstable server's General Purpose record is cleared.
- `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check PSScriptAnalyzer, Pester, Markdown` with strict mode, on b677393, before the merge: PSScriptAnalyzer **Pass** (no findings in 327 files), Pester **Pass** (2,926 tests passed, none failed, 7 skipped by their own conditions), and markdownlint **Pass** (no issues in 128 files).
- The 20 affected test files (the 19 of 20e855c, and the activity control), with `GITHUB_ACTIONS` and `CI` set, on b677393, before the merge: **Pass** (991 tests).
- **After merging `main` at e795e51 (pull request 10):** `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check PSScriptAnalyzer, Pester, Markdown, Bicep` with strict mode: PSScriptAnalyzer **Pass** (no findings in 327 files), Pester **Pass** (2,967 tests passed, none failed, 7 skipped by their own conditions), Bicep **Pass** (`azuredeploy.json` in sync), and markdownlint **Pass** (no issues in 128 files). The 22 affected test files (the 20 above, teardown, and the runbooks, which pull request 10 changed too), with `GITHUB_ACTIONS` and `CI` set: **Pass** (1,160 tests).
- The public push scan of `origin/main..HEAD` found 0 hits.

Decided differently:

- **R601 is the only new risk number:** the other fixes correct this pull request's own code before it merged, so they update R596 and R600 instead.
- **The ending reads the server only when nothing else says:** this run's results and the T4 track's record decide first, so a run that just created T4, or failed to, makes no extra call.
- **`status.ps1`'s missing server names the fallback's choice** ("deploy.ps1 creates it as General Purpose Standard_D2ds_v5, which the fallback chose ..."), so it matches the **NOT DONE** note.
- **DONE's restart sentence stays a condition:** the ending makes no Azure call for it, so it says "when its schedule is in place" and points to `stop-demo.ps1` and `status.ps1`, which read the schedule.

### Phase 28 follow-up: a T4 server that a failed attempt created

2026-11-03. An independent review of 64c28b2, after pull request 11 merged, found two Low issues with one cause: T4's server (`pgflex01`) depends only on the network module (`infra/modules/demo.bicep`), so a template attempt that fails because of another resource can still have created it, which the template step already assumed for the VMs. The reviewer reproduced both in memory with the fallback's Pester harness, changing one thing only: the `Standard_D2ds_v5` attempt creates the server before the template fails. The owner asked to fix both now. No Azure, Fabric, Graph, or other network call was made for this work.

**Work summary.**

1. **NOT DONE no longer says T4 "isn't created yet" when it was (Low, 8/10):** `Get-DemoT4ServerEvidence` returned `$false` for this run's fallback creating T4 (`DEPLOY.T4.SKU` next `Standard_D2ds_v5`) with a `DEPLOY.TEMPLATE` **Fail**, and for a `T4.SERVER` **Fail**, so `Complete-DemoDeploymentRun` skipped its Servers - Get, and **NOT DONE** said "T4 isn't created yet ... The next ./scripts/deploy.ps1 ... creates it" while a General Purpose server ran and billed. `Test-DemoT4` also reports `T4.SERVER` **Fail** when the read itself fails, not only for a missing server. Both now give `$null`, ahead of the T4 track's record, which can be older, so the ending's [Servers - Get](https://learn.microsoft.com/rest/api/postgresql/servers/get) decides: a server it finds is "T4 created as General Purpose Standard_D2ds_v5 ..., about `<price>` USD an hour while running", NotFound is "T4 isn't created yet" with `DEPLOY.TEMPLATE`'s reason, as before, and a read that fails is "couldn't be confirmed". `Get-DemoDeploymentOutcome` without `-T4Created`, which reads nothing, says "couldn't be confirmed" for both, never "created".
2. **A server that a failed attempt created counts as existing (Low, 6/10):** a run that started at `Standard_D2ds_v5` (preflight's start, or a `None` record) sent it with the T3 free offer. When Azure rejected the offer and `pgflex01`'s own deployment succeeded, pull request 11's repeated cost check priced T4 again without the offer's allowance and, over a ceiling or without a price, turned T4 off and recorded `None`: **DONE** said the General Purpose fallback "wasn't created", `status.ps1` and `validate.ps1` took the record for T4 off and didn't report the server, and it billed until `stop-demo.ps1` (which ignores the record) or the next deploy. Now `Invoke-DemoTemplateDeployment` reads T4 again (`Get-DemoT4State`) after every failed attempt that sent it while the fallback is choosing, as it reads the VMs before every attempt. A server that exists is treated as existing from then on: it keeps its own size and the record that goes with it (`Standard_D2ds_v5` here), isn't priced again because it exists and bills (`DEPLOY.T4.SKU` adds that its check counted the free offer's allowance, evidence `freeOfferCounted`), is never recorded `None` or `Refused`, and the next attempt deploys it as the existing server it is (no zone). The console says so. A read that fails is taken as missing, as before (R602).
3. **The Burstable steps, checked for the same assumption:** the fallback moves on only for `SkuNotAvailable` from `Get-DemoT4CreateFailure`, which reads the T4 deployment's failed operations. A refused creation leaves no server (Azure drops it; ADR 0019's live finding of 2026-10-26), and an attempt that created the server has a T4 deployment that succeeded, so on their own a server and a refusal don't meet. One way was left: a `SkuNotAvailable` answer to the update of a server that an earlier attempt of the same run had created, after the free offer's retry. 64c28b2 took it for a refusal of a new server and sent the next size to the existing one, `Standard_B1ms` to `Standard_B2s`, or `Standard_B2s` to `Standard_D2ds_v5` after a cost check: a resize. With the read above, the fallback stops once the server exists: no refusal is read for it and no size follows, and the template fails as it does for any other error. A unit test pins both sizes.
4. **Defense in depth, small:** when the record (`DbHubT4Sku` `None` or `Refused`) says T4 is off, `status.ps1` (`STATUS.T4`) and `validate.ps1` (`T4.SERVER`) now read the server, and report one that exists as a **Warning** (`New-DemoT4RecordedOffResult`): "T4 (`<server>`) exists and bills, although the resource group's record says T4 is off (DbHubT4Sku None): it's Ready, General Purpose Standard_D2ds_v5." The remediation: the next `./scripts/deploy.ps1` keeps it at its own size and clears a `None` record; with `Refused` it keeps it but leaves it out of the demo until `t4SkuName` sets its size; meanwhile `./scripts/stop-demo.ps1` stops it with the rest of the demo, and `./scripts/destroy.ps1` removes everything. Without a server, both report what they did before. The record isn't redesigned, and neither script changes it.

**Changed files.** The module's `Private/T4Helpers.ps1` (`Get-DemoT4ServerEvidence`, the new `New-DemoT4RecordedOffResult`, and help) and `Private/ValidationAreas.ps1` (`Invoke-DemoValidationT4`), and `Public/Invoke-DemoTemplateDeployment.ps1`, `Public/Get-DemoEnvironmentStatus.ps1`, and `Public/Complete-DemoDeploymentRun.ps1` (help and a comment); the tests `DbHubDemo.T4Fallback.Tests.ps1`, whose harness can now have an attempt create the server whatever else fails in it, fail another resource, and answer an existing server's update with a size's code, and `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`; and the CHANGELOG, this report (R601 updated, R602, still to verify), troubleshooting, cost management, deployment, and ADR 0019. No Bicep changed.

**Validation.** Static, 2026-11-03, with strict mode as in CI: `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check PSScriptAnalyzer, Pester, Markdown`: PSScriptAnalyzer **Pass** (no findings in 327 files), Pester **Pass** (2,979 tests passed, none failed, 7 skipped by their own conditions), and markdownlint **Pass** (no issues in 128 files). The 19 affected test files (the fallback, T4, preflight, cost, the lifecycle, the tracks, one command, the progress model, validation, the free offer, disk drift, the Actions masks, redaction, the configuration, the walkthrough, the conventions, function names, neutral wording, and the lifecycle scripts) again with `GITHUB_ACTIONS` and `CI` set to `true`: **Pass** (945 tests). 15 tests are new or changed (12 new, 3 changed). 14 of them fail on 64c28b2 and pass now: the fallback 12 (the free offer's retry 1, the evidence without a read 2, a template that failed for another resource 2, a `T4.SERVER` **Fail** 3, the Burstable steps 2, and `validate.ps1` 2) and the lifecycle 2 (`status.ps1`). The other one, the free offer's control case, in which the rejected attempt didn't create the server, passes on both. The whole unit suite also ran once with the Retail Prices request helper replaced by one that logs and refuses every call that would reach the API: none did. Bicep, PSRule, the .NET tests, and the Fabric App weren't changed and weren't run locally; CI runs them. The public push scan of `origin/main..HEAD` found 0 hits. Live: not run, and no Azure, Fabric, or Graph call was made.

**Decided differently from the request, and why.**

- **A read that fails after an attempt is taken as missing, as on 64c28b2,** not as an existing server: taking it for one would create a General Purpose server without its cost check when none exists, while a hidden server is what the **Warning** in `status.ps1` and `validate.ps1` now catches (R602).
- **The skipped check is said, not silent:** `DEPLOY.T4.SKU` keeps the first check's numbers, which counted the T3 free offer's allowance, and now says so, since the server bills from then on.
- **With `Refused`, the Warning says what `deploy.ps1` really does:** it keeps the server (a deployment never removes or resizes one) but leaves it out of the demo while that record holds, and `t4SkuName` set to its size brings it back; with `None`, the next `deploy.ps1` keeps it at its own size and clears the record.
- **This changes one point of the review of 20e855c:** a run whose template failed while the fallback was creating T4 now makes one Servers - Get in its ending, which it skipped before.

**Risks.** R601 (updated: what to watch when the first fallback run's template fails for another resource) and R602 (new).

### Phase 28 follow-up: the README's blank sign-in with no dialog

2026-11-03. A tester's setup showed a blank **Use Azure Login** window in the guest with no Internet Explorer dialog to select **Add...** in. The README covered only the dialog; `docs/troubleshooting.md` already had this case.

**Work summary.** The README's guided stop (step 4) and "What you must do, and when" now say what to do when the window stays blank and no dialog comes. IE Enhanced Security Configuration was still on when setup started, and a change made while setup is open doesn't reach it. So: cancel setup, set it to **Off** for **Administrators** in **Server Manager** > **Local Server**, start setup again, and redo the **Edition** and **Azure Extension for SQL Server** pages. They also say that the dialog can open behind setup's window, and that "Requires all input values for AZURESERVICEPRINCIPAL" only means the sign-in hasn't happened. No code changed.

**Changed files.** `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, and this report.

**Validation.** `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check Markdown`: **Pass**. The Pester files that read the README, the CHANGELOG, or this report (`Agent.PromptPack`, `DbHubDemo.Bundle`, `DbHubDemo.OneCommand`, `Repo.Conventions`, `Repo.NeutralWording`, and `Docs.Walkthrough`), with strict mode: **Pass** (722 passed, 0 failed).

**Risks.** None new.

### Phase 28 follow-up: business-day by default

2026-11-03. A tester opened Database Hub's **Performance** page (Microsoft SQL) on a new deployment and found its problem panels empty: **High CPU resources over time**, **CPU spikes over time**, and **Databases limited by service tier** (the names as the preview showed them). They list only resources that have that problem, and the default activity profile, `baseline`, never causes one. The owner made `business-day` the default ("The whole point is to showcase it"), the coordinator added three requirements, and later that day the owner added one more, verbatim: "We should not default to dry runs anywhere. users should be able to just click run all and then it should run." Two agents did the work in turn in one worktree: the first one's uncommitted change was reviewed, kept, and finished by the second ([ADR 0010](adr/0010-workload-generator-technology.md#activity-profiles-business-day-by-default-2026-11-03), [ADR 0014](adr/0014-notebook-role-and-capacity.md#amendment-2026-11-03-no-dry-run-defaults), [ADR 0016](adr/0016-cost-profiles.md#activity-profiles-business-day-by-default-2026-11-03)). No Azure, Fabric, or Graph call was made; the cost check read the public Azure Retail Prices API.

**Work summary.**

1. **The default:** `business-day` in `config/environment.schema.json`, the example configuration that `init.ps1` copies, `demo-control.yml`'s `scenario` input, and the live-activity notebook's `PROFILE`. `Get-DemoConfig` leaves `activityProfile` unset, and `Get-DemoActivityProfile` resolves it: `-ActivityProfile`, then the configuration, then the schema's default. An explicit value, `baseline` included, always wins.
2. **No incident slows a run's own Run Commands** (the coordinator): `deploy.ps1`, both paths, and `start-demo.ps1` set `baseline` where they set the profile and note each VM they set it on; once their own Run Commands on a VM are done, after **DONE**'s checks or at the warm-up's start, they switch its targets with one managed Run Command per VM, `DbHubDemo-Profile`, both VMs within 3 minutes (`DEPLOY.INCIDENTS_*`, `START.INCIDENTS_*`: **Pass**, **Not selected**, or a **Warning** with the command, never a **Fail**). A VM that's already running gets `baseline` first, sent without waiting, so an incident an earlier session left running ends early in the run, and a VM the run starts gets it as soon as it runs, and the run's own Run Commands there wait for it (the review and re-review fixes below). A run that's stopped (Ctrl+C) or fails sends the switch without waiting (`Complete-DemoDeploymentRun`, `start-demo.ps1`'s `finally`), and the console gives the command when even that fails. The template's install and boot profile is `baseline` in place of a profile with incidents, so a VM never starts an incident by itself. **DONE**'s requirements are unchanged.
3. **`stop-demo.ps1` stops on time** (the coordinator): the profiles go off in one managed Run Command per VM, both at once, waited for at most 180 seconds (`Stop-DemoEnvironment -ProfileOffWaitSeconds`); the guest's clean shutdown has the same bound; then both VMs are deallocated anyway. A profile that isn't off in time is a **Warning**, reported after the deallocation, that says the VM was deallocated anyway, which stops the incident with it.
4. **Configurations that don't set `activityProfile`** (the coordinator): the same ceiling checks, with `business-day` (`PREFLIGHT.COST.*`, `START.COST.*`, `estimate-cost.ps1`), and one notice per run on the console and as `PREFLIGHT.ACTIVITYPROFILE`, `DEPLOY.ACTIVITYPROFILE`, or `START.ACTIVITYPROFILE`. No script writes the configuration.
5. **Validation while incidents run:** **DONE**'s checks read Database Hub, Azure, and Fabric, never a target through its own load, and run before the switch; a test pins that its requirements hold no such check. In `validate.ps1`, a runner status read (`WORKLOAD.<target>.COMMAND`) or a `T3.PROPERTY` T-SQL check that fails the way load makes reads fail (a time-out, a 409, a lock, or a resource limit) while one of `business-day`'s incidents is due on that target, from its start minute to 3 minutes after its end (or on T1, for the reads `sqlvm01` makes for T3 and T4), and a host lane (`.HOST`) that fails while the target's own status says it runs `business-day` and an incident is due, are a **Warning** that names the incident and when to run the area again (`Get-DemoScheduledIncident`, `ConvertTo-DemoIncidentAwareResult`; narrowed in the review fixes below). A fault no incident causes and a property that's read and wrong stay a **Fail**.
6. **The cost:** the estimate prices `business-day`'s T3 incidents at the maximum vCores for their 10 minutes an hour, over the share `t3ActiveShareOfMaxVcores` leaves out, from the serverless vCore meter at run time; with the shipped share, 1.0, that's 0, and `PREFLIGHT.COST.*` say so. The check for the new default, a dated estimate (2026-11-03, West US 3, list prices from the Azure Retail Prices API; `lowest-cost`, private networking, T3 as regular serverless, all five attributed Defender plans on Standard), gave the same figures for `baseline` and `business-day`: $9.49 a session and $105.82 for the month as the ceiling check counts it with T4 on `Standard_B1ms`, and $10.30 and $223.35 on the `Standard_D2ds_v5` fallback, all **Pass** against the hard monthly ceiling of $250 (a **Fail** above it) and the session ceiling of $15 (a **Warning** above it). Without Defender plans, `Standard_B1ms` gave $8.92 and $64.74. What no estimate counts, T3 between its 0.5-vCore minimum and 1-vCore maximum for the incidents' minutes ($0.521758 per vCore-hour) and T2's E10 disk operations under `io-pressure` at the disk's 600-IOPS burst limit ($0.002 per 10,000), is at most $0.51 a session and $1.01 a light-use month; T3's extra log backups stay within the free backup storage of the database's 32 GB maximum size.
7. **The notebooks run on Run all** (the owner): `CONFIRM = True` by default in `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity` and `NB_DatabaseHub_Deployment`. **Run all** of the live-activity notebook sends `business-day` on every target for 240 minutes (`DURATION_MINUTES`, 30 before) through `demo-control.yml` with P9, or prints the link and command without it; `STOP_ACTIVITY` and `PURGE_DEMO_TABLES` stay `False`, so **Run all** never stops activity or drops a table, and `CONFIRM = False` gives a read-only run. The notebooks' safety text, the history report's empty-state text, and `STOP.FABRIC.SAVEHISTORY`'s manual step say so; the scripts' own notebook runs still pass `CONFIRM` false. Outputs stay stripped and the metadata unchanged.
8. **The messages:** **DONE**, `start-demo.ps1`, and the status page add an `Activity:` line; `status.ps1`'s `STATUS.PROFILE_*` gives the target's hourly incidents, or how to choose `business-day`, or that a run hasn't switched a `baseline` yet; `STATUS.RUNCOMMANDS` counts `DbHubDemo-Profile` as the scripts' own.

**Changed files.** New: `scripts/modules/DbHubDemo/Private/ActivityProfileHelpers.ps1` and `tests/unit/DbHubDemo.ActivityProfile.Tests.ps1`. Changed: `config/environment.schema.json` and `config/demo.config.example.json`; the module (`DbHubDemo.psm1`, `DeployExperience.ps1`, `FabricCapacityHelpers.ps1`, `LifecycleHelpers.ps1`, `ParallelDeployment.ps1`, `ProgressHelpers.ps1`, `RunCommandHelpers.ps1`, `TrackHelpers.ps1`, `ValidationAreas.ps1`, `WorkloadHelpers.ps1`, and `Complete-DemoDeploymentRun`, `Enable-DemoT3Workload`, `Enable-DemoT4Workload`, `Get-DemoConfig`, `Get-DemoCostEstimate`, `Get-DemoDeploymentParameter`, `Get-DemoEnvironmentStatus`, `Invoke-DemoDeployment`, `Invoke-DemoPreflight`, `Start-DemoEnvironment`, and `Stop-DemoEnvironment`); `scripts/deploy.ps1`, `start-demo.ps1`, and `stop-demo.ps1`; the parameter's description in `infra/main.bicep` and `infra/modules/demo.bicep`, with `azuredeploy.json` rebuilt with Bicep 0.47.16 (only descriptions changed: the template's own default stays `baseline`, the VMs' install and boot profile); `.github/workflows/demo-control.yml` and `schedule-start.yml`; both notebooks in `fabric/notebooks`, and the history model's `Incident Windows.tmdl` with its spec; the tests for the activity profiles, cost, the lifecycle, preflight, the tracks, the workload, the lifecycle scripts, validation, and the notebooks; and the README, CHANGELOG, this report (R603 to R611), cost management, deployment, the demo walkthrough, the automation support matrix, `fabric/README.md`, and ADRs 0010, 0011, 0014, and 0016.

**Validation.** Static, 2026-11-04 (UTC), on the final tree after the re-review fixes, with strict mode as in CI: `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1`: PSScriptAnalyzer **Pass** (no findings in 329 files), Pester **Pass** (3,110 tests passed, none failed, and 7 skipped by their own conditions), Bicep **Pass** (`infra/main.bicep` and the two standalone templates in `infra/control` build and lint cleanly, and `azuredeploy.json`, rebuilt with the pinned Bicep 0.47.16, is in sync), PSRule for Azure **Pass** (273 rules), the workload generator's .NET build and tests **Pass**, the Fabric App **Pass**, and markdownlint **Pass** (no issues in 128 files). The 30 affected test files (the activity profiles, cost, the lifecycle, preflight, the tracks, validation, the workload, the notebooks, the lifecycle scripts, the configuration, one command, the history model and its report, the conventions, neutral wording, the walkthrough, the prompt pack, the bundle, the Fabric items, Hub readiness, the Fabric track, the first run, the dashboard, activity control, the VM's workload steps, T4, and the runbooks, and for the re-review also the VM scripts' compatibility, the function names, and the VM's workload), with `GITHUB_ACTIONS` and `CI` set to `true`: **Pass** (1,959 passed, none failed, 3 skipped by their own conditions). The first round added 57 tests, 5 of them in place of tests renamed for the new default, and changed 11. The review fixes add 17 tests (45 cases with their examples; one in place of a test renamed for its new result) and change 7: 46 of those 53 cases fail on the reviewed head (`fbf4841`) and pass now, and the other 7 are controls that pass on both (load failures that were already a **Warning**, a switch that returns normally, and the windows' coverage). The re-review fixes add 12 tests (14 cases with their examples; one in place of a test renamed for its new result) and change 7: 18 of those 21 cases fail on `1dfc0ac` and pass now (run there with inert stand-ins for the two new functions, so that the old module takes the tests' mocks), and the other 3 are controls that pass on both (the interim send's records, a status of one run, which the relay keeps whole, and output without a profile). The relay's new function also ran in Windows PowerShell 5.1 on the 2-run and 5-run statuses. CI's Ubuntu job then failed the 4 size checks of those statuses (2,058 and 3,469 characters there): PowerShell writes LF line breaks on Linux, and the runner writes CRLF on the Windows VMs (System.Text.Json's indented output uses `Environment.NewLine`), so the test statuses now use CRLF on every platform, in one test-only commit; the two test files pass again (109 passed, none failed). An earlier full run, at 23:4x UTC on 2026-11-03, had 2 failures in `Vm.WorkloadSteps.Tests.ps1` (`EnableT3` and `EnableT4`): those tests set the auto-shutdown to 23:59 UTC and read the clock, and the VM's script sets no profile in the last 10 minutes before an auto-shutdown, so they fail from 23:49 to 23:59 UTC on `origin/main` too. The re-review changes that test file and that script, but not those tests or that check; they pass outside that window. On the first agent's tree, the whole unit suite also ran once with `Invoke-RestMethod` and `Invoke-WebRequest` replaced by stand-ins that log and refuse every call: none reached the Retail Prices API or another endpoint. The public push scan of `origin/main..HEAD` found 0 hits. Live: not run, and no Azure, Fabric, or Graph call was made.

**Decided differently from the request, and why.**

- **One notice per run, not once ever:** no script writes the configuration and a run keeps no memory of earlier ones; the notice is one line and one **Pass** (R611).
- **The live-activity notebook's `DURATION_MINUTES` is 240:** with `CONFIRM = True`, **Run all** with the old 30 would replace `start-demo.ps1`'s profile with one that ends after 30 minutes, one or two incidents per target; 240 is `demo-control.yml`'s maximum and the duration the scripts' own messages give (R610).
- **`validate.ps1` reads the schedule, not the VM:** a read that failed can't say which profile runs, so the configured profile and the clock decide, and only to explain a **Fail**, never to pass a check (R609).
- **The stop keeps the guest's clean shutdown,** with its own bound of the same 3 minutes, after the profiles' off, rather than skipping it when a VM is busy: in the worst case a stop waits about 6 minutes before the deallocation.
- **Disk operations and backups stay out of the estimate:** it counts no transactions for any profile, so the dated upper bound is in cost management instead (R608).
- **The panels' names are marked as observed:** Learn's [Monitor a Microsoft SQL database](https://learn.microsoft.com/fabric/database/hub/monitor-sql) describes the Performance page but not these panels, so the README, the walkthrough, and ADR 0010 say the names are as the preview showed them on 2026-11-03.
- **ADR 0014 is amended, not contradicted:** its "dry-run by default where meaningful" is superseded in part by the owner, its decision source; the on-demand control's notebook (`fabric/control`) isn't changed here.
- **A host lane goes by the VM's own profile; a status read and `T3.PROPERTY` go by the failure's text** (review fixes): a read that failed has no status to read the profile from, so it can say only whether it failed the way load makes reads fail; a text the check doesn't know stays a **Fail** (R612).
- **The interim for a VM a run starts goes out as soon as that VM runs, inside `Wait-DemoStoppedStart`,** not once it returns: T4's start, which it waits for next, can take 15 minutes.
- **A VM the interim send couldn't reach stays noted when something else had noted it** (`activityProfileOnBoot`, or a stage of the run), so its switch still comes; one noted only for that send is forgotten again, so a VM that isn't running gets no switch **Warning**.
- **The VM tries the interim again, and the run waits once for both VMs** (re-review): not `Set-DemoTargetProfile`'s three tries 120 seconds apart, one VM after the other. Each VM's own Run Command tries every 15 seconds, so the baseline lands within about 15 seconds of SQL Server answering, both VMs try at once, and the bound is the request's own 10-minute expiry, after which the script changes nothing.
- **T3's and T4's interim first:** their profiles are files on T1 that need no SQL Server (`CommandLine.cs`, `Store`), so they're set within seconds of the Run Command's start, and only the last target, T1 on `sqlvm01` or T2 on `archost01`, is ever tried again.
- **The relay reads the profile, not the runner** (re-review): a runner that wrote its profile last would need a new build and `runner.zip` published again, and a longer tail doesn't fit: the result line escapes the output's quotes and line breaks, so a status of five runs alone takes 4,148 characters there, past the 4,096 bytes an action Run Command returns. The relay's change is a few lines of the bundle's Windows PowerShell, which the next `deploy.ps1` sends to each VM because the bundle's hash changed (R615).

**Review of PR 16 (2026-11-04): merge after fixes.** The reviewer found four problems. Each is fixed on the same branch, with tests that fail on the reviewed head (`fbf4841`) and pass now.

1. **Medium: `validate.ps1` downgraded real faults by configuration and clock.** With `activityProfile` unset, so `business-day`, any **Fail** of `WORKLOAD.<target>.COMMAND`, `.HOST`, or an unread `T3.PROPERTY` during the incidents' windows was a **Warning**, and `validate.ps1` exited 0. The windows cover 33% of each hour on T1, 70% on T2, 43% on T3, and 58% on T4. That held on a VM running `baseline` or `off`, and for faults no incident causes. The reviewer reproduced it with "settings.json is missing" and with a host whose `profile.name` was `off`. Now:
   - `.HOST` counts only when the target's own status says it runs an unexpired `business-day` and its host process is alive (`Get-DemoRunnerActiveProfile`).
   - `.COMMAND` and `T3.PROPERTY` count only for failures load causes (`Test-DemoLoadFailureText`): a time-out, a 409, a lock, or a resource limit.
   - settings.json missing, no runner, a refused login, a host whose process ended, and any failure the check doesn't recognize stay a **Fail** (R609, R612).
2. **Medium: VMs a run starts got no interim baseline.** A stored `business-day` lasts 8 hours from its switch, or until 2 minutes before the next auto-shutdown. So it can outlive the bounded stop, the on-demand control's ARM-only stop, or a deallocation in the portal, and the profile host resumes it about 2 minutes after the boot, while the run's own Run Commands go on. Now:
   - `start-demo.ps1` sends the interim `baseline` to each VM it booted, or found stopping or starting, as soon as `Invoke-DemoVmPowerAction` reports it running.
   - `deploy.ps1` does the same inside `Wait-DemoStoppedStart`, in both paths, before any of the run's Run Commands there (R613).
3. **Low: Ctrl+C could lose a switch silently.** `Send-DemoPendingActivitySwitch` forgot the VMs before any request was in. A stop skips every catch block, so the fallback found nothing to send and said nothing. `Invoke-DemoActivityQuiet` noted the VMs only once the whole send had returned. Now:
   - A VM is forgotten once Azure accepts its request (`Send-DemoProfileRunCommand -OnSent`), and the rest once the switch returns.
   - The interim send notes each VM before its request goes out, and forgets one Azure refused only when nothing else had noted it.
   - The tests stop the send with the exception Ctrl+C raises, in a pipeline of their own, so every catch block is skipped as in a real stop (R605, R614).
4. **Low: a guest stop that ran out of time on 409s reported a Fail.** `Invoke-DemoActionRunCommand` returned the last 409 when its bound ran out between retries. So `STOP.GUEST` was a **Fail** and `stop-demo.ps1` exited 1, although the deallocation went on. It now returns the same time-out as the Azure CLI's (`ExitCode` -1), so `STOP.GUEST` is the **Warning** that ADR 0010 describes (R606).

The review fixes changed `ActivityProfileHelpers.ps1`, `ValidationAreas.ps1`, `LifecycleHelpers.ps1`, `ParallelDeployment.ps1`, `WorkloadHelpers.ps1`, `Invoke-DemoDeployment.ps1`, and `Start-DemoEnvironment.ps1`. They also changed the tests for the activity profiles, validation, the lifecycle, and the tracks, and these docs: the CHANGELOG, deployment, the demo walkthrough, ADR 0010, and this report (R603, R605, R606, R609, and R612 to R614).

**Re-review of `1dfc0ac` (2026-11-04).** The reviewer confirmed findings 1, 3, and 4 fixed, found finding 2 only partly fixed, and found one new Low. Both are fixed on the same branch, with tests that fail on `1dfc0ac` and pass now.

1. **Medium (A): the interim baseline on a VM the run had just started was one attempt that usually lost the race.** T1's profile lives in its own SQL Server, which is still starting after the boot, and T2's in the guest's, which boots after its host. The runner retries only transient errors, and a SQL Server that isn't running yet isn't one. When that attempt failed, the stored `business-day` resumed about 2 minutes after the boot, under the run's own Run Commands. Now:
   - That VM's Run Command sets T3's and T4's profiles, files on T1, at once. It then tries T1's or T2's again every 15 seconds, each attempt bounded at 120 seconds, until the runner sets it, says the target isn't set up, or the request's 10 minutes pass (`Get-DemoProfileRunCommandScript -RetrySeconds`).
   - `start-demo.ps1`, and `deploy.ps1` in `Wait-DemoStoppedStart` (both paths), wait for it before their first Run Command on that VM, both VMs together, at most those 10 minutes plus 150 seconds (`Wait-DemoActivityInterim`).
   - Each target reports `START.INTERIM_<target>` or `DEPLOY.INTERIM_<target>`: **Pass** with its seconds and attempts, **Not selected** when it has no runner yet, or a **Warning** when it wasn't set in time, failed, or wasn't accepted. The **Warning** gives `./scripts/validate.ps1 -Area Workload -Target <target>` and, if the run's switch didn't pass, `./scripts/demo-control.ps1 -Target <target> -Scenario business-day -Duration 240` (R613, R616).
2. **Low (B): the host lane's downgrade almost never fired.** The VM's relay keeps the last 1,800 characters of the runner's output. The runner's indented status writes the profile near the top and the host node last, so once a status lists two runs (2,128 characters without sessions), the kept tail has no profile, and `.HOST` stayed a **Fail** during incidents. The unit tests had passed only with `"runs":[]`. Now:
   - The relay reads the active profile's name and expiry from the whole output before the cut and adds them to its result's evidence (`Get-DemoControlRunnerProfile`).
   - `validate.ps1` takes them first (`Get-DemoRunnerActiveProfile -ProfileNode`).
   - The tests use realistic statuses of 2 and 5 runs (2,128 and 3,584 characters), on the VM's side and in `validate.ps1`, and the VM's side was also checked in Windows PowerShell 5.1 (R609, R615).

The re-review fixes changed `ActivityProfileHelpers.ps1`, `LifecycleHelpers.ps1`, `ValidationAreas.ps1`, `Start-DemoEnvironment.ps1`, `vm/bootstrap/Invoke-DemoWorkloadControl.ps1`, and the help of `deploy.ps1` and `start-demo.ps1`. They also changed the tests for the activity profiles, the lifecycle, the tracks, validation, and the VM's workload steps, and these docs: the CHANGELOG, deployment, the demo walkthrough, ADR 0010, and this report (R603, R609, R613, R615, and R616).

**Risks.** R603 to R616 (new).

### Phase 28 follow-up: every start brings every target back

2026-11-03 (R620–R628). The owner starts the demo only from the Fabric control. A run at 22:07:57 UTC started both VMs, and its Run Commands showed four failures that nothing reported: T2's relay failed after 12 minutes ("The virtual machine arcsql01 is not in running state"), because a golden capture on 10-28 had left the source air-gapped and only `start-demo.ps1` or `deploy.ps1` released it; T1's `profile set` failed with SQL error 2 at 22:09:26, while SQL Server accepted connections only at 22:10:42; T1's monitoring plugin reported `NotRunning`, and the runbook, which had read it right after the start, sent no restart; and the 15-minute wait for T4 stood in front of every dispatch. Both were repaired by hand at 22:37. No Azure, Fabric, Graph, or other live call was made for this work.

**Work summary.**

1. **The capture returns its source to service** (`GOLDEN.CAPTURE.SOURCE`, `Restore-DemoGoldenCaptureSource`): once Prepare has run, steps 6 to 8 run in a block whose `finally` starts `archost01` when it's deallocated, releases the guest as a source only (`Invoke-DemoGoldenSourceRelease`, which tags `dbhubSourceReleasedUtc`; the golden Release itself when no snapshot names the host yet), waits up to 30 minutes until `arcsql01` reads Connected after the air gap (a later `lastStatusChange`, 30 minutes after the air gap, or no air gap at all, which the Release now reports as `before`), and returns the host to the power state found (STOP.GUEST, then deallocation; never by force). `-WhatIf` and the steps before Prepare are unchanged. ADR 0018 sections 4 to 6 were checked: nothing but `GOLDEN.TEST.SOURCE` assumed a quiet source, which now counts the source's status only while its host stays deallocated for the whole test (a **Warning** otherwise) and measures a Connected source from the latest of the disk check, the release, and the host's deallocation, against the documented 30 minutes. Section 4 item 9, the policy-deny remediation, and `capture-t2-golden.ps1`'s help no longer say the source stays air-gapped until `start-demo.ps1`.
2. **The control's T2 relay** (`Invoke-DemoControlGuestReady` in `vm/bootstrap/Invoke-DemoWorkloadControl.ps1`, which the bundle carries) dot-sources the installed `Invoke-DemoGoldenGuest.ps1` and reuses its functions: an unreleased capture record whose source is this host (IMDS) gets the golden Release (released and `releasedUtc` written); a copy that isn't reset, or a host whose Azure VM ID can't be read, is never started (**Fail** with `deploy.ps1`); an off guest without a record gets the automatic start `Start` and starts; then, within `readySeconds` (20 minutes), heartbeat OK, PowerShell Direct, SQL Server started if its delayed start hasn't, and connections accepted. The runbook's heartbeat preamble no longer waits 12 minutes for a guest that's off and won't start by itself. The relay can't tag the snapshot; the later `start-demo.ps1` release is idempotent and tags it.
3. **No cold-start race:** before `profile set` (other than `off`) or `run`, the VM-side control runs the runner's `warmup` against the target until it answers, at most `readySeconds` (10 minutes, 20 for T2), retrying the documented transient errors 5 seconds apart, doubling to 60 (SqlClient's built-in list, the network-related and instance-specific errors 2, 26, 40, 53, -1, 10061, Azure SQL Database's transient faults with 40613, PostgreSQL's 08000, 08001, 08003, 08004, 08006, 08007, 57P03, and 53300 (the review of 2026-11-04 took out the rest of class 08 and 57P01 and 57P02), and the managed identity endpoint right after a boot). A target that doesn't answer in time is a **Fail**, and nothing is set.
4. **T4 doesn't hold the others back:** `Start-DbHubActivityRun` polls every 20 seconds and dispatches each VM as soon as it runs (at most 15 minutes), T4 with `sqlvm01`'s activities when it's Ready already, or alone (`DbHubControl-sqlvm01-t4-<time>`) once it's Ready (at most 20 minutes); T4 that's Missing, Stopping, Disabled, or Dropping is skipped at once. Each request carries `readySeconds`, and each Run Command's timeout covers the waits (at most 50 minutes).
5. **T1's monitoring plugin:** once `sqlvm01` runs, `Step-DbHubMonitor` waits up to 5 minutes for the plugin's first status, leaves `Running` alone, and otherwise restarts the SQL IaaS Agent service and reads it again for up to 5 minutes (Restarted or StillNotRunning); a plugin without its feature flag is left alone. For long runs, the new runbook `DbHub-Monitor` does the same every 30 minutes, started by one-time schedules `dbhub-monitor-<n>` that the run creates with its auto-stop and that every stop path removes through Azure Resource Manager only (Stop now, the auto-stop, the next run, `stop-demo.ps1`, `start-demo.ps1`); it does nothing once the run has ended or while `sqlvm01` doesn't run. The role is unchanged.
6. **`status.ps1` and `validate.ps1`:** the health read (sent as content) adds `t2Guest` on the Hyper-V host; `T2.GUEST.STATE` reports it while `archost01` runs (**Fail** for an unreleased capture record, an off guest, or a disconnected adapter, with the exact command; **Warning** for an automatic start other than `Start` or a heartbeat not OK yet; **Not selected** before the guest exists). `T2.ARC.CONNECTED` fails after 10 minutes Disconnected while the host runs, from the later of the last status change and the host's start (`Get-DemoVmPowerInfo`). `T1.SQLVM.MONITORING` keeps a reported `NotRunning` a **Warning** with the exact restart. ADR 0012 records the statuses and why.
7. **Rollout:** `./scripts/deploy.ps1` sends the changed bundle to both VMs; `./scripts/setup-activity-control.ps1 -SkipFabric` publishes the three runbooks, and `CONTROL.SETUP` is **Manual action required** while `DbHub-Monitor` is missing. ADR 0020, `fabric/control/README.md`, troubleshooting, and the CHANGELOG say so.

**Changed files.** The runbooks (`DbHubControl.Common.ps1`, `DbHub-Control.ps1`, `DbHub-Stop.ps1`, and the new `DbHub-Monitor.ps1`); the VM-side `Invoke-DemoWorkloadControl.ps1`, `Invoke-DemoGoldenGuest.ps1` (the Release's `before`), and `vm/health/Get-DemoHealth.ps1`; the module's `ActivityControlHelpers.ps1`, `ArcAzureHelpers.ps1`, `ArcChecks.ps1`, `GoldenHelpers.ps1`, `SqlVmMonitoringHelpers.ps1`, and `ValidationAreas.ps1`, and `Get-DemoEnvironmentStatus`, `Get-DemoVmHealth`, `Invoke-DemoActivityControlSetup`, `New-DemoT2GoldenSnapshot`, `Test-DemoActivityControl`, and `Test-DemoT2GoldenSnapshot`; the help of `capture-t2-golden.ps1` and `test-t2-golden.ps1`; the tests `Automation.Runbooks`, `DbHubDemo.ActivityControl`, `DbHubDemo.Arc`, `DbHubDemo.Golden`, `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle`, `DbHubDemo.Validation`, and `Vm.WorkloadSteps`; and ADRs 0012, 0018, and 0020, the automation support matrix, deployment, troubleshooting, `fabric/control/README.md`, the CHANGELOG, and this report (R620–R628). No Bicep, no role, and no notebook changed.

**Validation.** Static, on the final tree (2026-11-04 UTC), with strict mode as in CI: `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1`: PSScriptAnalyzer **Pass** (no findings in 328 files), Pester **Pass** (3,069 tests passed, none failed, 7 skipped by their own conditions), Bicep **Pass** (`azuredeploy.json` in sync), PSRule **Pass** (273 rules passed), the .NET tests **Pass**, the Fabric App **Pass**, and markdownlint **Pass** (no issues in 128 files). The first full run found 2 failures, both in the runbooks' fake Azure Resource Manager, which read a re-check link's missing parameters in a way strict mode refuses; the fake now reads them by index. The 32 affected test files (`Automation.Runbooks`, `Agent.PromptPack`, `DbHubDemo.ActivityControl`, `.Arc`, `.Bundle`, `.DashboardData`, `.FabricResolve`, `.FirstRun`, `.Golden`, `.Lifecycle`, `.OneCommand`, `.Progress`, `.Results`, `.SecretScan`, `.SignInData`, `.T4Fallback`, `.Teardown`, `.Tracks`, `.Validation`, `.Workload`, `Docs.Walkthrough`, `Fabric.ControlNotebook`, `Repo.Conventions`, `.FunctionNames`, `.Gitleaks`, `.LifecycleScripts`, `.NeutralWording`, `Vm.Compatibility`, `.GoldenGuest`, `.GuidedSetup`, `.Loader`, and `.WorkloadSteps`) again with strict mode and with `GITHUB_ACTIONS` and `CI` set to `true`: **Pass** (1,911 tests passed, none failed, 4 skipped). Bicep, PSRule, the .NET tests, and the Fabric App weren't changed. The public push scan of `origin/main..HEAD` found 0 hits. Live: not run, and no Azure, Fabric, or Graph call was made.

**Decided differently from the request, and why.**

- **The long-run re-check is a third runbook,** not more `DbHub-Control` jobs: its jobs never count as an active run, so they can't refuse the presenter's next request, and the stop paths delete its schedules through Azure Resource Manager only.
- **The readiness probe is the runner's own `warmup`,** not a new connection test, so T1, T3, and T4 use the runner's connection strings and identities, and T2's runs in the guest as before.
- **`GOLDEN.TEST.SOURCE` changed with the capture,** because its "not Connected 20 minutes after the capture" rule assumed the source stays air-gapped (ADR 0018, amended); its threshold is now the documented 30 minutes.
- **A reported `NotRunning` stays a Warning in `T1.SQLVM.MONITORING`:** T1's database works, the restart that fixes it runs in `start-demo.ps1` and every Fabric run, and `TELEMETRY.T1` reports the missing data.

**Risks.** R620 to R628 (new).

**Review fixes (2026-11-04).** The review of 9b645de asked for four fixes before the merge. No Azure, Fabric, Graph, or other live call was made for them.

1. **High: a T2 start during a golden capture undid the air gap just before the snapshot.** From Prepare (which writes the record and lets go of the bootstrap lock) to the deallocation, the source's record says unreleased and names this host, which is what the relay releases; a start waiting for that lock could get it before the capture's next poll, reconnect the guest, set its automatic start, start it, and write `released`, so the snapshot and every copy would have kept a connected guest with the captured identity. Now: (a) Prepare first writes the capture marker `golden\capture-in-progress.json` (the snapshot, the time, and this host's Azure VM ID), and only the capture's own Release (`EndCapture`, at its step 3 and in `GOLDEN.CAPTURE.SOURCE`) removes it; the relay changes nothing while the marker counts (6 hours at most, and only on the host it names: a copy carries the source's), changes the guest only under the bootstrap's lock, on the record, the marker, and the guest read again under it, and `T2.GUEST.STATE` is a **Warning** that a capture is in progress, with when the marker stops counting, instead of pointing to a Fabric start; (b) `capture-t2-golden.ps1` changes nothing while a `DbHub-Control` or `DbHub-Stop` job is queued or running or an auto-stop is pending (`GOLDEN.CAPTURE.CONTROL`, reusing the control's job read, now `Get-DemoActivityControlActiveJob`); (c) `Get-DemoControlGoldenDecision` takes a released record at its word only on the source (its VM ID) or a copy with `resetUtc`, and refuses any other copy, or a host whose VM ID can't be read; the health read reports the role for such a record too, and `T2.GUEST.STATE` fails such a copy with `deploy.ps1`.
2. **Medium: `start-demo.ps1` stalled 34 to 64 minutes when T1 or T2 stayed down.** The module never sent `readySeconds`, so each of its three attempts waited the VM side's 10 or 20 minutes. Now `Invoke-DemoWorkloadCommand -ReadySeconds` defaults to 0, and `Set-DemoTargetProfile` spends one budget across its attempts, (attempts - 1) x the retry pause: 4 minutes for T1 and T2 in `start-demo.ps1`, none for one attempt; what the VM side waited counts against it. The worst case per target is about the budget plus the Run Commands' own time (each attempt's profile set has 5 minutes), as before 2026-11-03; a host whose bundle predates `readySeconds` fails at once and gets the same retries and pauses as before.
3. **Low: a T2 guest without SQL Server was a Warning.** The relay's SQL wait found no `MSSQLSERVER` service before the setup wizard's step and said "SQL Server's service ... is Missing"; it now says "T2 isn't set up yet: SQL Server isn't installed in arcsql01 yet", which `Test-DemoT2NotSetUpText` turns into **Not selected**.
4. **Low: the T4 transient pattern retried a permanent error.** `08[0-9A-Z]{3}` matched 08P01 (protocol violation); the list is now explicit: 08000, 08001, 08003, 08004, 08006, 08007, 57P03, and 53300 ([PostgreSQL error codes](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/errcodes-appendix.html)).

**Changed files (review fixes).** `vm/bootstrap/Invoke-DemoGoldenGuest.ps1` (the marker, `Get-GoldenCaptureProgress`, `EndCapture`), `vm/bootstrap/Invoke-DemoWorkloadControl.ps1` (`Get-DemoControlGoldenDecision`, `Get-DemoControlGoldenState`, `Get-DemoControlGoldenRefusal`, the lock, the SQL wait's wording, the PostgreSQL codes), `vm/health/Get-DemoHealth.ps1`; the module's `ActivityControlHelpers.ps1` (`Get-DemoActivityControlActiveJob`), `GoldenHelpers.ps1` (`Get-DemoGoldenCaptureControlResult`, `EndCapture`, `T2.GUEST.STATE`), `LifecycleHelpers.ps1` (`Set-DemoTargetProfile`), `Invoke-DemoWorkloadCommand.ps1`, `New-DemoT2GoldenSnapshot.ps1`, and `Start-DemoEnvironment.ps1` (help); `scripts/capture-t2-golden.ps1` (help); the tests `Vm.GoldenGuest`, `Vm.WorkloadSteps`, `DbHubDemo.Golden`, and `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle`; and ADRs 0012, 0018, and 0020, troubleshooting, `fabric/control/README.md`, the CHANGELOG, and this report.

**Validation (review fixes).** Static, on the final tree (2026-11-04 UTC), with strict mode as in CI: `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1`: PSScriptAnalyzer **Pass** (no findings in 328 files), Pester **Pass** (3,099 tests passed, none failed, 7 skipped by their own conditions), Bicep, PSRule, the .NET tests, and the Fabric App **Pass**, and markdownlint **Pass** (no issues in 128 files). The same 32 affected test files, with strict mode and `GITHUB_ACTIONS` and `CI` set to `true`: **Pass** (1,941 tests passed, none failed, 4 skipped). New tests cover each fix: the race (a capture's Prepare that runs while the relay waits for the lock wins, and nothing changes), a capture in progress, a marker older than 6 hours, a copy's marker, an unknown VM ID, a copy whose record says released without a reset, the marker's write and its removal by `EndCapture` only, `GOLDEN.CAPTURE.CONTROL` (each job, a pending or passed auto-stop, a failed read, the control off), the capture refusing during a run, the budget across attempts (a target that stays down, an old host, one attempt, a failure after the target answered), a guest without SQL Server, and the PostgreSQL codes (08P01 isn't retried). The 2 tests that pinned the old rule for a released record failed until they were changed. The public push scan of `origin/main..HEAD` found 0 hits. Live: not run, and no Azure, Fabric, or Graph call was made.

**Risks (review fixes).** R629 to R632 (new).

**Re-review fixes (2026-11-04).** The re-review of 3065771 confirmed the four fixes and found two Low issues that kept ADR 0018's "nothing releases the source while its capture runs" from being true everywhere. No Azure, Fabric, Graph, or other live call was made for them.

1. **L1: the Release that `start-demo.ps1` and `deploy.ps1` run neither honored nor ended the marker.** During a capture, an older untagged snapshot that names the host made them wait for Prepare's lock, then reconnect and start the guest; and after a restore that failed before its Release, they released the guest and tagged the snapshot but left the marker, so for up to 6 hours `START.PROFILE_T2`, Fabric starts, and `T2.GUEST.STATE` said the guest was still air-gapped. Now `Invoke-GoldenRelease` itself refuses, and changes nothing, while a marker that names its host counts, unless it ends the capture (`EndCapture`); `Invoke-DemoGoldenSourceRelease` passes the snapshots it found (`CapturedSnapshots`), and the Release ends a marker that names one of them, because the protected window is over once that snapshot exists; a refusal is a `T2.GOLDEN.SOURCE` **Warning**. `GOLDEN.CAPTURE.SOURCE`'s remediation, the help of `capture-t2-golden.ps1` and `Invoke-DemoGoldenSourceRelease`, and ADR 0018 section 4 item 9 now say which release ends an interrupted capture and when.
2. **L2: the marker's time depended on the host's culture, and an unreadable one failed open.** Windows PowerShell 5.1 on fi-FI wrote `2026-11-04T09.44.28Z`, which the invariant parse rejects, so the marker was ignored. The time is now written with the invariant culture (`Format-GoldenUtc`) and read with the exact format (or as the date PowerShell 7's `ConvertFrom-Json` already made of it), and a marker that exists but names no host, can't be parsed, or isn't JSON fails closed (`Unreadable`): the relay, the golden Release, and `T2.GUEST.STATE` treat it as a capture in progress. Checked on Windows PowerShell 5.1 under fi-FI as well: the old format wrote `09.58.41`, the new one reads back as a capture in progress, and the old one as unreadable.

**Changed files (re-review fixes).** `vm/bootstrap/Invoke-DemoGoldenGuest.ps1` (`Format-GoldenUtc`, `Get-GoldenCaptureProgress`, `Get-GoldenCaptureRefusal`, the Release's gate, `CapturedSnapshots`), `vm/bootstrap/Invoke-DemoWorkloadControl.ps1` (an unreadable marker, the messages), `vm/health/Get-DemoHealth.ps1`; the module's `GoldenHelpers.ps1` (`Invoke-DemoGoldenSourceRelease`, `GOLDEN.CAPTURE.SOURCE`'s remediation, `T2.GUEST.STATE`) and `New-DemoT2GoldenSnapshot.ps1` (help); the tests `Vm.GoldenGuest`, `Vm.WorkloadSteps`, and `DbHubDemo.Golden`; and ADRs 0012, 0018, and 0020, troubleshooting, the CHANGELOG, and this report.

The same 32 affected test files, with strict mode and `GITHUB_ACTIONS` and `CI` set to `true`: **Pass** (1,948 tests passed, none failed, 4 skipped). New tests: the golden Release refusing during a capture with an older snapshot found and with an unreadable VM ID, ending the marker once its snapshot is found, `EndCapture` ending any marker, an expired marker and a copy's marker left alone, an unreadable marker refused (bad time, not JSON) by the Release, the relay, and the health read, the marker's time under fi-FI, `T2.GOLDEN.SOURCE`'s **Warning**, `T2.GUEST.STATE`'s unreadable marker, and `GOLDEN.CAPTURE.SOURCE`'s corrected remediation. The public push scan of `origin/main..HEAD` found 0 hits. Live: not run.

**Rebase onto PR 16 (2026-11-04).** PR 16 (`business-day` by default) merged first, at 9a2ee93; this branch was rebased onto it with its 11 commits in order, and the integration below added on top. No Azure, Fabric, Graph, or other live call was made.

Six conflicts, each resolved by keeping both sides:

- `vm/bootstrap/Invoke-DemoWorkloadControl.ps1`: the header's Runner text keeps PR 16's sentence on the active profile in the evidence beside `readySeconds` and the guest's release; the Runner's evidence keeps PR 16's profile, read from the whole output (`Get-DemoControlRunnerProfile`), beside the readiness note and its "accepted connections after N seconds".
- `scripts/modules/DbHubDemo/Private/LifecycleHelpers.ps1`: `Set-DemoTargetProfile`'s help keeps PR 16's `-SwitchTo` beside the readiness budget; its code merged without a conflict.
- `scripts/modules/DbHubDemo/Public/Start-DemoEnvironment.ps1`: the profile loop's comment keeps the budget beside PR 16's interim and switch.
- `docs/implementation-report.md`, in three commits: both phases' entries, still-to-verify bullets, and risk rows, PR 16's first; this branch's later commits replace only their own earlier bullet and R620 row. `CHANGELOG.md` merged without a conflict.

The integration:

1. **No multiplied waits.** `Get-DemoProfileRunCommandScript` sends `readySeconds` 0 with every target, so PR 16's Run Commands (the interim `baseline` on a running VM and on one the run started, the switch, and `stop-demo.ps1`'s off) never get this branch's VM-side wait (10 minutes per attempt, 20 for T2) on top of their own 15-second retries; T2's relay keeps its 60-second minimum to make the guest ready. Its reads of the relay's evidence are strict-mode safe (a refusal has no `exitCode`). `start-demo.ps1` gives a T1 or T2 that stayed down through its interim's wait (`TimedOut`, or `Failed` after the VM's retries) one profile attempt without a wait, and `Set-DemoTargetProfile` doesn't try T2 again while its relay holds the guest for a golden capture.
2. **The first start or deploy after a capture (the Low from PR 16's review).** The interim on `archost01` came before `Invoke-DemoGoldenSourceRelease`, so it could try an air-gapped guest for 10 minutes, hold `sqlvm01`'s steps, and end in a misleading Warning. Chosen: the release first, and a stop in the VM's retries. `start-demo.ps1` and `deploy.ps1` (`Wait-DemoStoppedStart`) run the release as soon as `archost01` runs, before its interim: it reconnects a source still air-gapped, ends a marker whose snapshot exists, and tags the snapshot, so the interim then reaches a guest that boots. When the release leaves the guest off (`captureInProgress`, or a release that failed), `START.`/`DEPLOY.INTERIM_T2` is **Not selected** with the reason, and nothing is sent or waited for there (`Get-DemoGoldenHeldInterimResult`). A skip alone wouldn't do: a capture in progress whose snapshot doesn't exist yet is known only to the host (its marker), and this branch's relay already releases a source and starts an off guest by itself. So the VM's loop also stops at once when the relay holds the guest (the golden reason in its evidence: `CaptureInProgress`, `CaptureMarkerUnreadable`, or `CopyNotReset`) or PowerShell Direct finds the guest not running, and keeps that reason in its line, which keeps only the last 300 characters of the message; the module reports it as `GuestOff` (`Get-DemoT2GuestOffReason`): **Not selected** for the interim, the switch, and `stop-demo.ps1`'s off ("nothing to turn off"), never a Warning. A guest that's still booting is still tried every 15 seconds.

**The combined worst case when T1 or T2 never answers** (not measured live): a `start-demo.ps1` that boots the VMs waits the interim's at most 10 minutes plus 150 seconds (both VMs at once), then one profile attempt per such target without a wait (one Run Command, about a minute; the runner's own bound is 5 minutes), then the switch (at most 3 minutes, both VMs at once): about 15 minutes, about 19 at most when both never answer. One that finds the VMs running has no interim wait: the 4-minute budget per such target, about 5 minutes with its Run Command, plus the switch: about 8 minutes, 13 for both. A `deploy.ps1` that starts stopped VMs: the interim's 12.5 minutes, then its one-attempt profiles (no wait) and the switch: about 15 minutes. Before PR 16 this branch's budget alone cost about 5 minutes per target; T1's monitoring step keeps its own bounded wait, unchanged.

**Changed files (rebase).** `scripts/modules/DbHubDemo/Private/ActivityProfileHelpers.ps1` (`readySeconds` 0, the loop's stop and the golden reason in its line, `GuestOff`, `Get-DemoGoldenHeldInterimResult`), `scripts/modules/DbHubDemo/Private/LifecycleHelpers.ps1` (`Get-DemoT2GuestHoldReason`, `Get-DemoT2GuestOffReason`, `Set-DemoTargetProfile`, `ConvertTo-DemoStopProfileResult`, `Wait-DemoStoppedStart`), `scripts/modules/DbHubDemo/Public/Start-DemoEnvironment.ps1` and `Stop-DemoEnvironment.ps1`; tests in `tests/unit/DbHubDemo.ActivityProfile.Tests.ps1` and `tests/unit/DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`; ADR 0010, ADR 0018, ADR 0020, `docs/deployment.md`, this report, and the changelog.

**Validation (rebase).** Static, on the final tree (2026-11-04 UTC), after the rebase, with strict mode as in CI: `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1`: PSScriptAnalyzer **Pass** (no findings in 330 files), Pester **Pass** (3,255 tests passed, PR 16's and this branch's together, none failed, 7 skipped by their own conditions), Bicep **Pass** (`azuredeploy.json` in sync), PSRule **Pass** (273 rules), .NET **Pass**, the Fabric App **Pass**, and markdownlint **Pass** (no issues in 128 files). The 33 affected test files (the 32 of the earlier runs and PR 16's `DbHubDemo.ActivityProfile`), again with `$env:GITHUB_ACTIONS` and `$env:CI` set: 2,092 passed, none failed, 4 skipped (pandas, and gitleaks not installed). 18 tests are new: `readySeconds` 0 in every profile request; the VM's retries stopping for each golden hold and for a guest not running, with the reason kept past the line's 300-character cut, and a booting guest still tried again; `GuestOff` for each hold, and a failure for any other reason or target; `GuestOff` as **Not selected** for the interim, the switch, and `stop-demo.ps1`'s off (reported before the deallocation); `Get-DemoGoldenHeldInterimResult`; the release before `archost01`'s interim in `start-demo.ps1` and in `deploy.ps1`'s stopped start, and no interim when the release leaves the guest off; one profile attempt without a wait after an interim that stayed down; and no retry of a held T2 in `Set-DemoTargetProfile`. Six order assertions in PR 16's tests changed with the release's new place. CI's `windows-latest` PowerShell job had failed 3 of this branch's relay tests since 9b645de, which passed locally and on `ubuntu-latest`: that runner has the Hyper-V module, so the tests' stand-ins for its cmdlets weren't created, and Pester mocked the real `Connect-VMNetworkAdapter`, whose `-VMNetworkAdapter` takes only a real adapter, so the relay's release failed on the tests' fake one. The tests now mock the golden script's own `Connect-GoldenAdapter`, as `Vm.GoldenGuest` does; with stand-ins typed as on that runner, the file passes locally (83 tests), as it does without them.

**Risks (rebase).** R634 (new).

### The showcase: Run all fills the Database Hub visuals

2026-11-03. The owner asked that **Run all** in the control notebook run, with no dry run, a showcase on all four targets that fills every Database Hub visual and replaces a run that's active; that the README say what visual thresholds there are and when they will and won't populate; and that it explain the baseline run and why it matters or doesn't. The coordinator approved the design with seven decisions: ADR 0016's ceilings of 2026-10-20 ($250 a month, hard; $15 a session, a **Warning**); 90 minutes by default and 120 at most; T4's `max_connections` lowered so **Failed connections** fills on every size; `plan-regression` on T3; the newest request wins, and targets it doesn't list are switched off; the run check from the telemetry only, with no new role; and the Activator alert templates on T3 as an optional presenter step. This part is everything that doesn't touch the runbooks, the relay, the control notebook, the control's setup, or the status and validation code, which wait for `control-start-reliability`.

**Work summary.**

1. **Dated cost example, business hours** (the owner's direction: "Just use 9-5 business hours as the amount it is on for the estimate"): 8 hours on 22 weekdays, 176 hours a month. `./scripts/estimate-cost.ps1 -LightUseHoursPerMonth 176` (lowest-cost, private mode, Defender for Cloud, T4 on `Standard_D2ds_v5`, T3 without the free offer) gives $436.98: $2.02 an hour times 176, plus $81.23 a month that bills anyway. PostgreSQL autonomous tuning's minimum (`Standard_D4ds_v5`) adds $31.33, SQL Server Enterprise on T1 and T2 $387.20, and both make $855.51. That's above the $250 hard ceiling even without them, so a business-hours schedule needs `monthlyCeilingUsd` at about $450, or about $875, and the budget deployed again. List prices from the Retail Prices API on 2026-11-03, labeled as dated examples; no default changes.
2. **T4's `max_connections` at 50 on every size** ([ADR 0019](adr/0019-t4-postgresql-flexible-server.md#2026-11-03-max_connections-at-50-on-every-size-and-connection-surge-past-the-limit-the-showcase)): before, the size's default (50, 429, or 859) left the larger sizes far above `connection-surge`'s 60 connections. The template sets 50 from `config/versions.json` (allowed 25 to 5,000; 15 of the 50 are kept back by `reserved_connections` and `superuser_reserved_connections`). It's static, so `Invoke-DemoT4PendingRestart` restarts T4 when the value waits (`isConfigPendingRestart`; Servers - Restart restarts the engine, not the compute), in T1's T4 step and `-Sequential`'s T4 stage, before T1 sets T4 up, and waits up to 10 minutes (`DEPLOY.T4.RESTART`). `Test-DemoT4` adds `T4.MAXCONNECTIONS`, a **Warning** while it waits.
3. **`connection-surge` past the limit, bounded** (the coordinator's bounds, 2026-11-03): the overshoot used to take only the free slots, and PostgreSQL keeps its reserved slots for administrators, so the server never refused the runner. Now, at the limit, `SurgeBurst` holds the free slots for 5 seconds at most and makes attempts that the server refuses: at most 5 in any minute and 30 in a run (`SurgeBounds`, stated in `scenario.json` and its README), inside the scenario's maximum, only as `ContosoCare.Demo/connection-surge/<run>` to `contosocare`, and every connection is closed in a `finally`. Only SQLSTATE 53300 counts as refused; a sign-in failure (class 28) stops the surge, so nothing looks like a password attack to Defender. T4's other lanes treat 53300 as transient and retry for up to 90 seconds. `longest_burst_ms` records the hold.
4. **`plan-regression`** (T3 only): `demo.usp_visit_summary_lookup`'s plan follows the first patient range it's compiled for. The run uses its seek plan, then recompiles it once for every patient, so the cached plan scans, and the narrow lookups go on regressed; with `FORCE_LAST_GOOD_PLAN` on, Azure SQL Database's default, automatic tuning forces the earlier plan back once the estimated CPU gain is over 10 seconds. 10 minutes by default and 20 at most, 1 to 4 sessions, lookups that only read; it refuses SQL Server (50168) and a `goodPercent` outside 10 to 80 (50169). Session 1 unforces an earlier run's forced plan at the start, and `reset` does too (`forced-plan-unforced`), for the lookup's queries only. The catalog, the live-activity notebook's scenario list, two prompts, and the walkthrough's cheat sheet have it.
5. **The `showcase` profile and the runner** ([ADR 0010](adr/0010-workload-generator-technology.md#the-showcase-anchors-cycles-tiers-request-stamps-the-hosts-priority-and-plan-regression-2026-11-03)): the baseline plus 22 slots in a 30-minute cycle, at most two incidents at once per target. Profiles gain `anchor` (`profileStart`), slots gain `cycles` and `tier`, and `profile set` gains `--tier`. Request stamps (`--if-newer`, `--request-id`) on `profile set`, `stop --all`, and `reset` make the newest request win: `demo.usp_profile_set` compares and writes under `UPDLOCK, HOLDLOCK`, and the file store holds `activity.json.lock`. `profile run` raises itself to above-normal priority on Windows and records it in the heartbeat.
6. **Prompts:** `showcase.in-hub.md` and `showcase.skills.md`, a tour of the estate while the showcase runs, and `plan-regression`'s two, with their rows in the prompt README and the live coverage record (**Manual action required** until a rehearsal records them).
7. **Docs:** the README's "What fills each Database Hub visual, and when" (the owner's item 8: per visual and target, the threshold with its Learn citation or *observed* date, the slot that fills it, when it populates with the telemetry's lag, and when it doesn't) and "The baseline run: why it matters, and when it doesn't" (item 9); deployment's T4 bullet and "Update an existing environment"; limitations' "Database Hub's visuals in one session" and what isn't proven live; cost management's showcase session; ADRs 0010 and 0019; the scenario, runner, and prompt READMEs; and the walkthrough's `plan-regression` entry.

**Changed files.** `config/versions.json`; `infra/modules/pgflex01.bicep` and `demo.bicep`, and `azuredeploy.json` rebuilt with Bicep 0.47.16; the module's `Private/T4Helpers.ps1` and `Private/TrackHelpers.ps1`, and `Public/Invoke-DemoDeployment.ps1` and `Public/Test-DemoT4.ps1`; `database/common/110-demo-control.sql` and `120-scenario-procedures.sql`; `scenarios/activity-profiles.json` and its schema, `scenarios/connection-surge/`, and the new `scenarios/plan-regression/`; the runner's `Catalog/ActivityProfiles.cs`, `Cli/CommandLine.cs`, `Profiles/ProfileHost.cs`, `Profiles/HostLanes.cs`, the new `Profiles/HostPriority.cs`, `Sql/DemoDatabase.cs`, `Postgres/PgScenarioRunner.cs`, and the new `Postgres/ConnectionSurge.cs`; the tests `CatalogTests`, `ProfileSchedulerTests`, `ProfileHostTests`, `RunnerTests`, `SqlScriptTests`, and the new `ProfileStoreTests` and `ConnectionSurgeTests`, and `tests/unit/DbHubDemo.T4.Tests.ps1`; `fabric/agent/README.md`, `fabric/agent/prompts/README.md`, `prompt.schema.json`, and four new prompt files; `fabric/notebooks/NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity.ipynb`; and `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, this report (R640 to R648), cost management, deployment, limitations, the walkthrough, ADRs 0010 and 0019, and the scenario and runner READMEs.

**Validation.** Static, 2026-11-03, on this part's tree: `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check PSScriptAnalyzer, Pester, Bicep, DotNet, Markdown` with `CI` set: PSScriptAnalyzer **Pass** (no findings in 327 files), Pester **Pass** (3,032 tests passed, none failed, 7 skipped by their own conditions), Bicep **Pass** (`infra/main.bicep` and the two standalone templates in `infra/control` build and lint cleanly, and `azuredeploy.json`, rebuilt with the pinned Bicep 0.47.16, is in sync), DotNet **Pass** (500 tests: 483 passed, none failed, 17 skipped, the opt-in SQL Server and PostgreSQL integration tests), and markdownlint **Pass** (no issues in 133 files). Each commit was tested on its own tree too: `plan-regression` with the .NET tests (447: 430 passed, 17 skipped) and the prompt-pack, notebook, and activity-control Pester files (625 passed, none failed, 2 skipped); the runner with the .NET tests (500: 483 passed, 17 skipped) and the whole Pester suite (3,010 passed, none failed, 7 skipped); and the prompts with the prompt-pack and conventions files (567 passed). The new tests cover the bounds the coordinator set for the surge (`ConnectionSurgeTests`: the per-minute and per-run caps, the 5-second hold, 53300 against a sign-in failure, the other lanes' retry), the restart and the check (`DbHubDemo.T4.Tests.ps1`), the stamps and the lock (`ProfileStoreTests`), the anchors, cycles, and tiers (`ProfileSchedulerTests`), the showcase's slots (`CatalogTests`), and the procedure's comparison (`SqlScriptTests`). PSRule and the Fabric App weren't changed and weren't run locally; CI runs them. The public push scan of `origin/main..HEAD` found 0 hits before each push. Live: not run. No Azure, Fabric, or Graph call was made; the dated prices came from the public Retail Prices API, which needs no sign-in.

**Decided differently from the request, and why.**

- **The rollout is `-FromStage Infrastructure`, not `-Resume`:** after a run that ended **DONE**, `deploy.ps1 -Resume` continues after the last stage that finished, the validation, so it applies no template, runner, or T-SQL. Deployment now says what each command applies (the coordinator accepted the finding, 2026-11-03).
- **The lower limit alone didn't fill Failed connections:** PostgreSQL refuses an ordinary user once only the reserved slots are free, so the old overshoot never reached a refusal on any size; the bounded burst does (accepted, with the bounds above).
- **The business-hours example names the check that fails:** preflight's own monthly check counts the 10-hour light-use month, so it's `estimate-cost.ps1 -LightUseHoursPerMonth 176` that reports `PREFLIGHT.COST.MONTHLY` as a **Fail**; the budget's alerts fire, and `start-demo.ps1`'s `START.COST.ACTUAL` fails once the month's actual cost reaches the ceiling.
- **`plan-regression` takes `slow-consumer`'s place in the third cycle,** the design's optional slot, so T3's Network IO waits come in the first cycle only.
- **The docs describe the finished showcase,** Run all and the newest-wins rule included, which the control's part delivers before the pull request opens. *(Changed on 2026-11-04 by the split the owner chose: the pull request ships without the newest-wins rule, and the docs say that a second Run all is refused while a run is active; see the next section.)*

**Risks.** R640 to R648 (new).

### The showcase, PR-A: Run all on T1 to T4, the 4-vCore T4, and a fresh plugin status

2026-11-04. The owner chose to split the showcase and get a working one today: this pull request (PR-A) carries everything on the branch, the PostgreSQL decisions of the morning, and the control pieces that let **Run all** run the showcase on all four targets; the newest-wins rule, the notebook's run check, the T4 tuning check in `status.ps1` and `validate.ps1`, and `showcase` in the local scripts wait for PR-B. The owner's decisions, verbatim: "Lets do the upgraded the postgres then and skip the enterprise sql" (07:25), "Change the default to not deploy burstable but the 4 core" (07:31), and "Raise the default to $375, and count a restart as at most 1 day where the safety stop exists" (about 07:45). The live check of #17 the same day added the monitoring plugin's freshness fix.

**Work summary.**

1. **The $375 ceiling and the restart rule** ([ADR 0016](adr/0016-cost-profiles.md#2026-11-04-user-directed-the-4-vcore-t4-default-and-a-375-ceiling)): `monthlyCeilingUsd` defaults to 375, still hard. T4's 7-day restart counts as about a day per restart (at most 96 hours a month) only when the `dbhub-t4-safety-stop` schedule is enabled, A1 is on the demo group, and the last `DbHub-Stop` job didn't fail; otherwise the rest of the month, with a **Warning** that names the missing piece and its fix. The monthly check's message gives the three ways to stay cheap.
2. **T4 is `Standard_D4ds_v5` by default, with autonomous tuning** ([ADR 0019](adr/0019-t4-postgresql-flexible-server.md#2026-11-04-user-directed-general-purpose-standard_d4ds_v5-by-default-with-autonomous-tuning)): `t4AutonomousTuning` (unset means on), its preflight check (`PREFLIGHT.T4.TUNING`: a **Warning** that turns it off for an explicit smaller size, a **Fail** when it's set to `true` with one), the four tuning parameters in the template, the fallback (another zone, `Standard_D4ads_v5`, then `Standard_D2ds_v5` with tuning off after its cost check), the move of an existing T4 (`PREFLIGHT.T4.SIZE`, `DEPLOY.T4.SIZE`), and the record tag `DbHubT4Size`.
3. **The runner:** `profile set --vcores` scales T4's CPU-bound slots by its vCores, 6 `cpu-pressure` sessions on 4 vCores; `missing-index` runs on T4 as top-level parameterized lookups that Query Store captures, in the showcase's General Purpose slot at minutes 20-29.
4. **The control** ([ADR 0020](adr/0020-on-demand-activity-control.md#amendments-2026-11-04-run-all-runs-the-showcase-and-t1s-plugin-counts-only-a-fresh-status)): the runbook accepts `showcase` and `off`, sends the showcase with the run's end and T4's tier and vCores, and setup bakes the profiles into the settings and the notebook's catalog; the notebook runs the showcase on T1 to T4 for 90 minutes by default, and its Safety text says that a second Run all is refused while a run is active.
5. **T1's plugin counts only a fresh status** (the live finding of 2026-11-04): `MainServiceLastReportedTime` later than the VM's start, in the runbooks and in `Restore-DemoSqlVmMonitoringPlugin`; the wait is 8 minutes (5 before), then one restart.
6. **Docs:** the README (the High CPU row "may not appear; observed intermittent" for T1 and T2 with the 2026-11-04 data, T4's CPU target per size, the autonomous tuning row, the $375 ceiling and why, how to stay cheap, the refusal of a second Run all, and the re-checks), cost management's dated example of the 4-vCore default and the ceiling, deployment (the 4-vCore default and its fallback, and the rollout), limitations, the walkthrough's "T4 autonomous tuning in the Azure portal", troubleshooting's "T1 missing right after a cold start from Fabric", ADRs 0010, 0016, 0019, and 0020, the scenario READMEs, and the CHANGELOG.
