The product's creation and fallback path is unchanged (the move's cost check runs only for an existing server); the mock now reads the bound parameters (`PesterBoundParameters`), with the function's own default. The full Pester suite with strict mode, as in CI (`./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check Pester -CI`): **Pass** (3,403 passed, none failed, 7 skipped).

**Decided differently from the request, and why.**

- **The tuning parameters with tuning off:** the template leaves them alone, as asked, except that a Burstable T4 gets `pg_qs.query_capture_mode` `NONE`, which is the server's default, so Learn's Burstable caution holds even if someone turned Query Store on.
- **`TOP`, not `ALL`:** the runner sends the lookups as top-level statements, so `TOP` captures them; `ALL` would add the functions' nested statements, which the recommendations don't need.
- **The record tag is new (`DbHubT4Size`):** a record of the earlier fallback (`DbHubT4Sku`, set when Burstable sizes were refused) would have kept such a server at `Standard_D2ds_v5`; the owner's "existing configs inherit" moves it, unless `t4SkuName` sets a size (R651).
- **The cost check before a move runs in preflight,** so `-FromStage Infrastructure`, which skips preflight, moves T4 without it; deployment says to run `preflight.ps1` or `estimate-cost.ps1` first.
- **Without `MainServiceLastReportedTime`,** a status counts as fresh once the VM's start is more than 30 minutes old, so a cold start gets its restart while a VM that has run a while isn't restarted on every look; and after a restart, a status without the time is taken at its word.

**The review of PR 20 (2026-11-04, at `a0c3112`): merge after fixes.** Two Medium findings, both fixed here with tests; the six Low findings are filed as issues, not fixed here.

- **M1, an `off` started the VM or T4 it was meant to turn off:** `Start-DbHubActivityRun` put `off` activities in the VMs to start and read T4 for them, so "T1 and T3 showcase, T2 off, T4 off" booted `archost01` (the guest, and Arc SQL pay-as-you-go billing) and started the 4-vCore T4, waited up to 15 and 20 minutes, then sent `profile set --name off`, and both billed until the auto-stop (the reviewer reproduced the two starts against a mocked Azure Resource Manager). Now only an activity that isn't `off` starts a VM or reads, starts, or waits for T4; a VM whose only activities are `off` gets them only when it already runs (otherwise they're skipped: "off not sent: archost01 is deallocated, so nothing runs there"); T4's `off` goes in `sqlvm01`'s own Run Command with `readySeconds` 0; T1's plugin is looked after, and re-checks scheduled, only when `sqlvm01` has an activity that isn't `off`; and a run whose only dispatched activities are `off` stops what it started. Tests: the reviewer's request (no `/start` for `archost01` or T4, no read of T4, and the job done in under 2 minutes instead of 20), offs to a running `sqlvm01` with no start and no plugin look, offs skipped on a stopped one, and a run that could only send an `off`.
- **M2, the documented update command scaled T4 with no ceiling check:** `-FromStage Infrastructure` skips preflight, and the template step scaled an existing T4 without `t4SkuName` to `Standard_D4ds_v5` (the reviewer's case: `monthlyCeilingUsd` 250, no `t4SkuName`, no control, so light use plus the restart worst case is $312.21, a **Fail**, scaled anyway). Now the template step prices a move to a larger size (`Test-DemoT4SizeIncrease`) with the 2-vCore fallback's estimate at that size (`Get-DemoT4GeneralPurposeCost -SkuName`, with the safety stop's `T4RestartCoverage`); over either ceiling, or without a price, T4 keeps its size and `DEPLOY.T4.SIZE` (outcome `Ceiling`) names the ceiling to raise, and nothing is recorded, so the next run checks again. With `-ScaleT4`, an explicit `t4SkuName` changed to a larger size on an existing server is priced the same way, then scaled or kept; a smaller size can only lower the cost and isn't priced. Tests: over the ceiling keeps the size (the month, a session, and no price), under it scales and says the check passed, a move between the two 4-vCore sizes isn't priced, and the explicit change is priced, then kept or scaled.

**Risks.** R649 to R660 (new); R641, R646, and R651 updated.

### The showcase hotfix: T1 and T2's profile-name check

2026-11-04. The live check of PR 20 on the owner's environment found Run all's showcase refused on T1 and T2. `runner profile set` failed with error 547, "The UPDATE statement conflicted with the CHECK constraint 'ck_activity_profile_name'", while T3 and T4 took the showcase. PR 20 added `showcase` to `demo.usp_profile_set`'s list, but not to the table's check. `010-schema.sql` has created that check since the first release with `off`, `baseline`, `business-day`, and `incident:%` only. T3's and T4's profiles are files on T1, so they never reach the table, and CI has no SQL Server, so no test ran the procedure. The control's job still said Dispatched for all four targets; that's issue [#28](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/28) (R661).

**Work summary.**

1. `database/common/010-schema.sql` creates the check with `showcase`.
2. `database/common/110-demo-control.sql` widens the check in a database created before the showcase. When `sys.check_constraints` holds a definition without `'showcase'`, it drops the check and adds it again, with the same names as `010-schema.sql`, in one transaction, so a second run changes nothing. The runner embeds the script, so its hash changes. `deploy.ps1 -FromStage Artifacts` (or the fuller update) installs the new runner, and its `setup` applies the script on T1, T3, and T2.
3. Test: `TheProfileCheckAllowsEveryProfileTheSetAccepts` (the runner's SQL tests). The check in `010-schema.sql`, the check that `110-demo-control.sql` adds, and the procedure's list must hold the same names, including `showcase`. Every profile in `scenarios/activity-profiles.json`, plus `off`, must be among them.
4. Docs: the troubleshooting entry for the 547 (its symptom, how to read the Run Command's result, and the update that fixes it), the database README, and the CHANGELOG.

**Changed files.**

- `database/common/010-schema.sql`, `110-demo-control.sql`, and `README.md`
- `workload-generator/tests/ContosoCare.Workload.Tests/SqlScriptTests.cs`
- `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/troubleshooting.md`, and this report

**Validation.**

- **Static (2026-11-04, `CI` set):**
  - `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check DotNet,Markdown -CI`: .NET **Pass** (518 tests: 501 passed, 17 skipped without a test server); markdownlint **Pass** (0 issues in 133 files).
  - Pester 5.9.1 on `Repo.Conventions`, `Docs.Walkthrough`, `DbHubDemo.Workload`, `Vm.Workload`, and `DbHubDemo.Bundle`: **Pass** (282 passed).
  - CI runs the full suite.
- **Live, on the owner's T1 (SQL Server 2022 Standard),** in one transaction that was rolled back:
  - before the migration, the check had no `showcase`;
  - after the migration, it had `showcase`;
  - after a second run, it was unchanged;
  - an update to `showcase` changed 1 row, and an update to an unknown name was refused with 547;
  - after the rollback, the check and the profile were as before.
- The fix itself reaches the owner's environment through `deploy.ps1 -FromStage Artifacts` after the merge, followed by Run all.

### The showcase hotfix, follow-up: the migration's guard and the update's order

2026-11-04. The review of PR 29 found no bugs and two Low findings, filed as [#31](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/31) and [#30](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/30) and fixed here. This entry also records PR 29's live rollout.

**Work summary.**

1. **#31:** the migration's guard looked only for `'showcase'`. A profile name added later would skip every database that had already run the migration, and `profile set` would fail with 547 again. The guard now runs when the definition lacks any allowed name. The test derives the guard's names and compares them with the check's and the procedure's.
2. **#30:** troubleshooting's entry for the 547 put the update before Stop now. The run's pending auto-stop, which `deploy.ps1` doesn't cancel, could deallocate the VMs in the middle of the update. The entry now gives the order the live rollout used: `stop-demo.ps1`, which waits until the VMs are deallocated (or Stop now, then a wait until they are, because Stop now returns before they stop; the review of this pull request), then the update, which starts the stopped VMs and T4, then Run all.

**Changed files.**

- `database/common/110-demo-control.sql`
- `workload-generator/tests/ContosoCare.Workload.Tests/SqlScriptTests.cs`
- `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/troubleshooting.md`, and this report

A mutation that removed `showcase` from the guard failed the test (expected `baseline`, `business-day`, `off`, and `showcase`; actual without `showcase`), and the restored file passed. markdownlint on the changed files: **Pass**.
- **Live, the new guard,** on the owner's T1 after PR 29's rollout, in one transaction that was rolled back:

- **Live rollout of PR 29** on the owner's environment (2026-11-04), in this order:
  - `stop-demo.ps1` (all **Pass**): it removed the pending auto-stop, set every profile off, deallocated both VMs, and stopped T4.
  - `deploy.ps1 -FromStage Artifacts`: **DONE** in 41 minutes. It started `sqlvm01`, `archost01`, and T4, and installed the new runner on both VMs.
    - Afterward, T1's check read `([profile_name]='showcase' OR [profile_name]='business-day' OR [profile_name]='baseline' OR [profile_name]='off' OR [profile_name] like 'incident:%')`.
    - A policy-installed `MDE.Windows` update held `archost01`'s Run Commands for about 25 minutes (R49). `T2.ARC.ONBOARDING` was a **Warning** (the guest didn't answer while it was blocked; Arc stayed Connected), and the run waited it out.

### The Insights app in every deployment

2026-11-05. The owner: "I need to make the Fabric app apart of the default deployment and not an optional piece. If it fails to create that is fine, but we should try and bubble up the error in the HTML status and progress page", then "Make sure a failure doesn't stop the deployment of everything. It should move on and continue." Before, the Fabric track ran the app's step, but a default run usually didn't add the app: only `configure-fabric-app.ps1 -SignIn` ran the Rayfin CLI's browser sign-in (P14), and the status page showed the app **Skipped**, "Optional". This supersedes two Phase 28 decisions: the app's step showing **Skipped** (item 2 of the live proof's fixes), and the sequential Fabric stage leaving the app out "because it's optional" (the review's decisions).

**Work summary.**

1. **The sign-in starts at the beginning of the run, and nothing waits for it** (`FabricAppSignInHelpers.ps1`). In a session that can take keyboard input, after the stopped VMs' start and before preflight, `Start-DemoFabricAppSignIn` reads the stage's sign-in (`rayfin login status --json`) and, unless the CLI is signed in to the demo's tenant, starts the pinned CLI's `rayfin login --tenant` through `npx` without waiting (`Start-DemoRayfinSignInProcess`): a console window of its own on Windows, the same terminal elsewhere. `deploy.ps1` settles it while it waits for the tracks (`Complete-DemoFabricAppSignIn`), ends it at its 15-minute deadline, when the tracks are done (`-Final`), when the run ends, or on Ctrl+C (`Stop-DemoFabricAppSignIn`), and reports `DEPLOY.FABRIC.APPSIGNIN` and the status page's **Sign-in for the Insights app** row, never a **Fail**. Only the app's step waits for it (`Wait-DemoFabricAppSignIn`, from the progress model), at most until that deadline. `-Sequential` starts it the same way and waits for it right before its Fabric stage's app step.
2. **The app's step is contained** (`Invoke-DemoInsightsAppStep`): the wait, the step, its summary, and its progress update are in one `try`, so an exception, a timeout, or a network error becomes the app's own `DEPLOY.FABRIC.APP` record (`ConvertTo-DemoInsightsAppErrorRecord`); only a cancellation of the run passes through. The Fabric track's outcome still comes only from the notebooks, the dashboard, the model, and the report, so `DEPLOY.TRACK.FABRIC` never fails on the app.
3. **Its records never block, and they show as Failed** (`ConvertTo-DemoInsightsAppRecord`, `Test-DemoNonBlockingResult`, `Get-DemoInsightsAppSummary`): an error is **Manual action required** with `reportedStatus` and `nonBlocking` (`insightsApp`) in its evidence, so the DONE gate, `DEPLOY.SUMMARY`, and the exit codes never see a **Fail**; a network error's fix is `configure-fabric-app.ps1 -SignIn`, never a rerun of the whole deployment, and the outcome's next step never comes from a non-blocking record. The status page shows the app's step as **Failed** with the actual error and fix (up to 1,200 characters), "(doesn't block DONE)" beside its label, and a red **Needs attention** box under the banner in every state (`Get-DemoProgressProblem`, the model's `nonBlocking` section); `status.ps1 -Watch` prints `NEEDS ATTENTION:`, and **DONE** and **NOT DONE** add `Failed (doesn't block DONE): the Insights app. ...` (`Format-DemoNonBlockingLine`). **DONE** names the app among what Fabric has only when it's deployed and working.
4. **Preflight reads P12 and P13** (`Test-DemoFabricAppTenantSetting`, `PREFLIGHT.FABRIC.APPSETTINGS`, after P11): **Pass** or a **Warning**, never a **Fail**; `configure-fabric-app.ps1 -FabricAdmin` matches the settings the same way (`Find-DemoFabricAppTenantSetting`).
5. Docs: the README (with the presentation surfaces and the cost), the app's guide, deployment, prerequisites (P12 to P14 in their own section), permissions, troubleshooting, presentation, limitations, the security model, cost management (the app's capacity units stay outside the ceilings and every estimate), the automation support matrix (rows 173 and 174), ADRs 0012, 0018, and 0023, the CHANGELOG, and `init.ps1`'s next steps no longer call the app optional, and say when the sign-in happens and what a skipped or failed one does.

**Changed files.**

- `scripts/deploy.ps1`; in `scripts/modules/DbHubDemo/`: `Private/FabricAppSignInHelpers.ps1` (new), `Private/DeployExperience.ps1`, `Private/DeployHelpers.ps1`, `Private/FabricAppHelpers.ps1`, `Private/ParallelDeployment.ps1`, `Private/ProgressHelpers.ps1`, `Private/TrackHelpers.ps1`, `Public/Complete-DemoDeploymentRun.ps1`, `Public/Initialize-DemoConfig.ps1`, `Public/Invoke-DemoDeployment.ps1`, `Public/Invoke-DemoPreflight.ps1`, `Public/Start-DemoDeploymentRun.ps1`, `Public/Test-DemoFabricAppPrerequisite.ps1`, and `Public/Write-DemoDeploymentOutcome.ps1`
- `tests/unit/DbHubDemo.FabricAppSignIn.Tests.ps1` (new), `DbHubDemo.FabricTrack.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.OneCommand.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Preflight.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Progress.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`
- `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `fabric/app/README.md`, `docs/deployment.md`, `docs/prerequisites.md`, `docs/permissions.md`, `docs/troubleshooting.md`, `docs/presentation.md`, `docs/limitations.md`, `docs/security-model.md`, `docs/cost-management.md`, `docs/automation-support-matrix.md`, ADRs 0012, 0018, and 0023, and this report

**Validation.**

- **Static (2026-11-05, offline, PowerShell 7.6.9, Pester 5.9.1, PSScriptAnalyzer 1.25.0, Node.js 26.13.0):** `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check PSScriptAnalyzer, Pester, Markdown`: PSScriptAnalyzer **Pass** (no findings in 332 files); Pester **Pass** (3,438 passed, 17 skipped, 0 failed, in 946 seconds); markdownlint **Pass** (0 issues in 133 files).
  - The new and changed tests cover an exception, a timeout, a network error, and a failed or skipped sign-in in the app's step: each leaves the other tracks running, the Fabric track's outcome unchanged, the run **DONE**, and the app's row **Failed** with the actual error on the status page (`DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.FabricTrack.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.OneCommand.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Progress.Tests.ps1`); the sign-in's start, settlement, deadline, and cleanup, and preflight's P12 and P13 check (`DbHubDemo.FabricAppSignIn.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Preflight.Tests.ps1`); and `-Sequential` (`DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`).
  - Found on the way and fixed: the guard scan flagged the sign-in's `rayfin login status` argument list as an Azure CLI sign-in (it's now a named array, as `Test-DemoFabricAppPrerequisite` has it); the sequential deployment's tests ran the real app step, whose `configure-fabric-app.ps1` reloads the module and dropped the tests' guard context for the rest of the file (they mock it now); and two new tests counted an empty result in a way that fails under `Set-StrictMode -Version Latest`, which an earlier test file turns on in a full run.

### The nightly safety stop

2026-11-05. The owner asked, "Can we have safety stops for all the databases?", and chose a daily safety stop in every deployment at the nightly shutdown time, with its role assigned by the deployment when the deployer may, as an exception to ADR 0008 ([ADR 0024](adr/0024-nightly-safety-stop.md)). The owner's standing direction holds: one-click and fast, and the Fabric capacity left to its users.

**Work summary.**

1. **The runbook:** `DbHub-Stop` gains `-Scope Nightly` (both VMs deallocated unless deallocated or deallocating; a 409 Conflict read again and counted as done once the VM is deallocating, at most three tries 30 seconds apart; T4 stopped when Ready, and waited for up to 10 minutes while starting, updating, restarting, or provisioning) and `-Scope T3Check` (one Databases - Get, judged Paused, Pausing, Missing, AutoPauseOff, TooEarly, Awake, or Unread). Both say `NotGranted` when the identity can't read the demo group, and fail after the result line when they couldn't do their work. Azure Resource Manager only: no start, no Run Command, no database connection.
2. **The resources and setup:** `infra/safetystop/safetystop.bicep` (the identity and the Automation account, Basic, local authentication off) and `Invoke-DemoSafetyStopSetup`, which `deploy.ps1` runs on every run: the template, the runbook (published only when its content differs), the two schedules (created again when disabled, moved, or unlinked), and the custom role "DbHub Demo Safety Stop" with its assignment when the caller may (`SAFETYSTOP.*`, Warnings only). `scripts/grant-safety-stop.ps1` is the Owner's one command.
3. **What reads it:** `STATUS.SAFETYSTOP`, `COST.SAFETYSTOP`, and **DONE**'s note (`Get-DemoSafetyStopNote`); `Get-DemoT4RestartStopState` for `STOP.T4.STOP` and `STATUS.T4`; `Get-DemoT4RestartCoverage`, which counts the nightly safety stop first and the control's daily T4 safety stop second; the estimate's 120 job minutes; `CONTROL.SAFETYSTOP` passes while the nightly one is in place; `SECURITY.RBAC.ROLEASSIGNMENTS` accepts its role at exactly the demo group; `destroy.ps1`'s `DESTROY.SAFETYSTOP.ROLE`.
4. **Docs:** the README (At a glance, where it goes, before you start, the steps, automated vs manual, P16, the activity control, the baseline run's month, Cost with a dated example and "What stops or pauses on its own", and Clean up), cost management (a 2026-11-05 dated example from `estimate-cost.ps1`'s prices, the same as 2026-11-04's), deployment, permissions, prerequisites, configuration, troubleshooting, the FAQ, limitations, the automation support matrix (rows 175 and 176), the security model, cleanup, architecture, `automation/README.md`, ADR 0024, amendments to ADRs 0008, 0012, 0016, 0019, and 0020, and the CHANGELOG.

**Changed files.**

- New: `infra/safetystop/safetystop.bicep`, `scripts/grant-safety-stop.ps1`, `scripts/modules/DbHubDemo/Private/SafetyStopHelpers.ps1`, `scripts/modules/DbHubDemo/Public/Invoke-DemoSafetyStopSetup.ps1`, `tests/unit/DbHubDemo.SafetyStop.Tests.ps1`, and `docs/adr/0024-nightly-safety-stop.md`
- `automation/runbooks/DbHub-Stop.ps1` and `DbHubControl.Common.ps1`
- The module: `DbHubDemo.psd1`, `Private/ParallelDeployment.ps1`, `DeployExperience.ps1`, `T4Helpers.ps1`, `ValidationAreas.ps1`, `ValidationSecurity.ps1`, and `LifecycleHelpers.ps1`, and `Public/Invoke-DemoDeployment.ps1`, `Get-DemoEnvironmentStatus.ps1`, `Get-DemoCostEstimate.ps1`, `Test-DemoActivityControl.ps1`, `Remove-DemoEnvironment.ps1`, `Invoke-DemoPreflight.ps1`, `Invoke-DemoTemplateDeployment.ps1`, and `Stop-DemoEnvironment.ps1`
- `scripts/estimate-cost.ps1` and `stop-demo.ps1` (help text), and `tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1` (builds and lints the standalone template)
- Tests: `Automation.Runbooks`, `DbHubDemo.T4`, `.Cost`, `.Lifecycle`, `.Tracks`, `.Teardown`, `.Validation`, `.Preflight`, `.T4Fallback`, and `Repo.Conventions`
- The docs listed above, and this report

  - `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1`: Pester **Pass** (3,489 tests: 3,472 passed, 17 skipped, none failed; 2,197 s). The script then stopped at its Bicep check, because this machine has no Azure CLI, so PSRule, DotNet, FabricApp, and Markdown didn't run here; CI runs them.
  - `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check PSScriptAnalyzer`: **Pass** (no findings in 334 files), after fixing the five that the first run found in the new code: an unused parameter, two parameters used only inside a script block, a plural noun, and the orchestrator's ShouldProcess, suppressed with its justification because each step it calls has its own.

### T2 from Arc Jumpstart's image: `JumpstartImage` by default

2026-11-05, user-directed: "I want a one click deployment that works without anything and fast", for public users of the repository in their own subscriptions, "to reuse the arc jumpstart process and images". The owner confirmed that this use of Jumpstart's images is approved for Arc. T2's former default, `PaygWizard`, needed a person once per guest build ([ADR 0006](adr/0006-sql-editions-and-licensing.md)).

**Verification first (read-only).**

- **The image:** `https://jumpstartprodsg.blob.core.windows.net/arcbox/prod/ArcBox-SQL-STD.vhdx`, public, 36,645,634,048 bytes, ETag `0x8DDD21BE1292CD1`, with its own Content-MD5. About 80 MB of HTTP range reads of its VHDX, GPT, NTFS, and registry hives, without a full download, showed a Generation 2 dynamic disk with a 100 GiB maximum, Windows Server 2022 Standard (build 20348.3932, not an evaluation, specialized), and SQL Server 2022 Standard 16.0.1140.6 on the default instance `MSSQLSERVER`, with Integration Services, ML Services, `AdventureWorksLT2022`, `C:\Jumpstart` (licensed SQL Server media), SSMS, and no Arc agent.
- **Arc Jumpstart** (`microsoft/azure_arc` at `e2c114e`, `Azure/arc_jumpstart_docs` at `71a945c`): ArcBox creates this guest as Generation 2 with 2 vCPUs and 6 GB, reaches it over PowerShell Direct with a published default credential, and installs the SQL extension with license type Paid. Standard or Enterprise is what its docs say gives "SQL Server performance metrics". Its FAQ scopes its environments to "evaluation, training, and demo purposes only". The code is MIT, the content CC BY 4.0, and the images have no license of their own.
- **Fit:** Database Hub's prerequisites for Arc ([add-sql](https://learn.microsoft.com/fabric/database/hub/add-sql#prerequisites-for-sql-server-enabled-by-azure-arc)) are all met with the demo's PAYG tag and `configure-arc.ps1`; `lowest-cost`'s 2 vCPUs and 8 GiB are at or above ArcBox's; the 128 GiB host OS disk holds the download plus a growth allowance, not the 100 GiB maximum (user decision: no shrink, an early warning instead).
- **What stays required:** no Bastion to deploy (optional for interactive access), no ISO media, no guided stop, and the golden snapshot only as a speed-up; the guest hardening, Arc onboarding, the sign-in data sweep, and everything after them stay.

**Work summary.**

1. `t2SqlInstallMode` `JumpstartImage`, the default in the schema, the example configuration, `infra/main.bicep` (and `azuredeploy.json`, rebuilt with Bicep 0.47.16), `init.ps1`, and the first run; `config/versions.json` pins the image (`media.jumpstartArcBoxSqlStd`: URL, size, ETag, Content-MD5, the verified facts) and the credential source (the script's URL at the pinned commit, its SHA-256, and two patterns; no value).
2. `vm/hyperv-guest/GuestJumpstart.ps1` and the host steps `GuestSource`, `GuestImage`, `GuestFirstBoot`, and `JumpstartImageCheck` ([vm/bootstrap](../vm/bootstrap/README.md)): the resumable, hashed, fail-closed download; the guest created with its adapter disconnected; the first boot over PowerShell Direct (the published credential read at run time and replaced, `arcsql01` and UTC, SQL Server's rename, `BUILTIN\Administrators` as sysadmin, SQL logins disabled, the sample database, Integration Services, Launchpad, and `C:\Jumpstart` removed, any Arc identity reset); the image checks; and no fallback (user decision).
3. The space rule (`GrowthAllowanceGB` 20) with `STATUS.T2.DISKSPACE`, `STATUS.T2.IMAGE` for a newer image (user decision: accepted after the checks, with a **Warning**), the guest-source guard against switching an existing guest's mode, and the health fields.
4. The evaluation guard for the build modes' guests: `slmgr.vbs /rearm`, a restart, and `/ato` at 30 days or fewer, at most daily, never during an activity profile or a golden capture.
5. The orchestrator: `JumpstartImage` in every install-mode default and set, its Arc expectation (Standard, PAYG, monitoring), its start banner and status page steps (no guided stop, estimates labeled as such), `PREFLIGHT.T2.MODE` for the evaluation-only VM, the mode-aware P3 public IP message, golden snapshots of a guest that isn't an evaluation (tag `dbhubEvalExpiresUtc` `none`), and step labels for every mode.
6. Docs: the README (At a glance, the steps, the guided stop as an opt-in, the modes, Codespaces, the AI assistant, automated vs manual, prerequisites, the quick start, cost, clean up, and sign-in), deployment, troubleshooting, limitations, the FAQ, prerequisites, cost management with dated prices from the Retail Prices API (2026-11-05), the other affected docs, ADRs 0006, 0018, 0009, and 0003 with dated amendments (and notes where others were affected), the VM READMEs, and the CHANGELOG.

**Deviations from the plan.** The download is in `GuestJumpstart.ps1`, its own seam, not in `vm/bootstrap/lib/Download.ps1`, so the VM module's exports are unchanged. Preflight doesn't compare the mode with an existing host's guest (it can't read a deallocated host cheaply); the host's `GuestSource` step refuses a mismatch before any change (R689).

**Changed files.**

### The three 2026-11-05 changes combined

2026-11-05, the owner: "Merge tonight after offline tests and CI; live test afterward", then "I will not be able to live test until tomorrow morning so affter the offline tests and CI stop before the live test", and "Make sure to update all the readme information to be accurate with all the changes and steps and documentation as well as cost and things like that." The owner's standing direction holds: the Fabric capacity is left to its users, so no estimate or automatic stop includes it. Three finished branches, each with its own offline tests, became one pull request to `main`: [the Insights app in every deployment](#the-insights-app-in-every-deployment), [the nightly safety stop](#the-nightly-safety-stop), and [T2 from Arc Jumpstart's image](#t2-from-arc-jumpstarts-image-jumpstartimage-by-default).

**Work summary.**

1. **Real merges, in this order:** the Insights app, the nightly safety stop, then `JumpstartImage`, each with `git merge --no-ff`, so every branch's commit stays in the history.

A default deployment's only steps for a person after `az login` are the Insights app's browser sign-in at the start of a run in a terminal you type in, and, when the deployer isn't an Owner, an Owner's `./scripts/grant-safety-stop.ps1`.

### The Insights app's sign-in: `BROWSER`, and a sign-in that already exists

2026-11-06. The owner's live test of the combined 2026-11-05 changes, run from another worktree, found two problems with the Insights app's sign-in. The owner asked, "Okay so this will be fixed and work the next time correct?" The standing goal: "I want a one click deployment that works without anything and fast."

1. **No browser opened.** The terminal's `BROWSER` held `"C:\Program Files (x86)\Microsoft\Edge\Application\msedge.exe" --inprivate %s`, a workaround for `az login`: Python's `webbrowser` module takes that form. When `BROWSER` is set, the Rayfin CLI 1.36.2 opens its sign-in page with `exec('"<BROWSER>" "<url>"')`; otherwise it uses `start "" "<url>"` on Windows. It doesn't check the result (`dist/auth/rayfin-auth.js`, read from the pinned package). So the command was malformed, nothing opened, and the sign-in waited until its 15-minute deadline. Its `login` always shows the account picker (`forceAccountSelect`), so the default browser picks the right account too.
2. **A false Warning.** After that window was closed (exit code -1), `Complete-DemoFabricAppSignIn` recorded `DEPLOY.FABRIC.APPSIGNIN` as a **Warning**: "without the sign-in the Insights app isn't created". But a separate `rayfin login` had signed the CLI in to the configured tenant (`rayfin login status --json` said `ok`), so the app's step would create the app. The status page's message was wrong and alarmed the owner.

**Work summary.**

1. **`BROWSER`** (`Get-DemoRayfinSignInEnvironment`, `Test-DemoBrowserCommand`). The environment for the CLI's sign-in process keeps what it had: the telemetry off, no ambient `RAYFIN_TOKEN`, and no colors. It now also sets `BROWSER = $null` when `BROWSER` is set but is neither the path of an existing file (a relative one counts from the CLI's working directory) nor a program on `PATH` (`Get-Command -CommandType Application`, with wildcards taken literally). One **Warning** line in the log names the value. Both sign-ins use it: `Start-DemoRayfinSignInProcess` for `deploy.ps1`, and `Invoke-DemoRayfinInteractive` for `configure-fabric-app.ps1 -SignIn`. The caller's environment never changes (`Start-Process -Environment`). The CLI's other calls use `--json`, which never opens a browser, so they're unchanged.
2. **The CLI's own status** (`Get-DemoRayfinSignInState`). It reads `rayfin login status --json` for the configured tenant, at most 60 seconds. When the stage's pinned CLI is installed, that CLI answers (`Invoke-DemoRayfinCli`). Otherwise the same version answers through `npx --yes --prefer-offline --package=@microsoft/rayfin-cli@<pinned> -- rayfin login status --json`, because a first run has no stage until the app's step. It returns `SignedIn`, `NotSignedIn` (not signed in, or to another tenant), or `Unknown`, and never throws. `Invoke-DemoFabricAppProcess` now takes `npx`. The CLI's JSON parsing moved to `ConvertFrom-DemoRayfinOutput`, which `Invoke-DemoRayfinCli` shares.
3. **Before a Warning** (`Complete-DemoFabricAppSignIn`). For a window that ended with another exit code than 0, or was stopped (at its deadline, with `-Final`, or with `-Wait`), the status is read once:
   - `SignedIn` is **Pass**: "The Rayfin CLI is signed in to the demo's tenant (its sign-in window was closed, with exit code -1, but a sign-in already exists); the Insights app's step uses it."
   - `NotSignedIn` keeps the Warning's text.
   - `Unknown` is a **Warning** that says the status couldn't be read, and that the app's step uses a sign-in the machine already has, if any.

   Nothing reads it after Ctrl+C. The sign-in's state now records its tenant (`TenantId`).
4. Docs: troubleshooting (a row for `BROWSER`, and the sign-in row), deployment, the app's guide (its manual one-time command doesn't get the `BROWSER` removal, so it says what to do), ADR 0023's amendment, the CHANGELOG, and this report.

**Changed files.**

- `scripts/modules/DbHubDemo/Private/FabricAppSignInHelpers.ps1`, `FirstRunHelpers.ps1`, and `FabricAppHelpers.ps1`
- `tests/unit/DbHubDemo.FabricAppSignIn.Tests.ps1`
- `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/troubleshooting.md`, `docs/deployment.md`, `docs/adr/0023-first-run-automation.md`, `fabric/app/README.md`, and this report

**Validation.**

- **The failure, reproduced offline** (2026-11-06, Windows, Node.js 24.22.2). The CLI's `openBrowser` code ran with Node's `exec`, unchanged except for a callback that reports the result:
  - The live `BROWSER` value failed with `'""C:\Program' is not recognized as an internal or external command` (exit code 1) and opened nothing.
  - The path of an existing file, and a program name on `PATH`, each ran and received the URL.
- **The guard, checked:** in PowerShell 7.6.9, `Test-DemoBrowserCommand` was false for the live value and for odd values such as a name with arguments, a list, a quoted name, a wildcard, a folder, and `nul`, without an exception. It was true for Edge's path, `cmd`, and relative paths that exist. A real child process started with the environment lacked `BROWSER`, while the caller kept it.
- **The status read, through the real CLI and isolated:** `RAYFIN_CONFIG_DIR` pointed the CLI at an empty folder, so no shared token cache was read and nothing called Microsoft Entra ID or Fabric. Through npx it returned `{"status":"not-signed-in",...}` in 7.4 to 12.1 seconds, and in 8.8 to 10.0 seconds with `--prefer-offline`. A cold first call took more than 30 seconds, hence the 60-second bound; that timeout came back as `Unknown`, without an exception.
- **Pester 5.9.1, the sign-in's file** (`DbHubDemo.FabricAppSignIn.Tests.ps1`, 44 tests, 25 of them new): **Pass**. Mutations checked that the new tests fail when the code regresses: without the `BROWSER` removal (9 failed), with `-SignIn`'s old environment (1), with the caller's `BROWSER` cleared (8), with the status ignored (2), without the tenant comparison (1), and without the Ctrl+C guard (1).
- **Static (2026-11-06, Windows, PowerShell 7.6.9, Pester 5.9.1, PSScriptAnalyzer 1.25.0, markdownlint-cli2 0.23.3 on Node.js 24.22.2):** `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check PSScriptAnalyzer, Pester, Markdown -CI` gave PSScriptAnalyzer **Pass** (no findings in 340 files), Pester **Pass** (3,600 passed, 17 skipped, 0 failed, in 908 seconds), and markdownlint **Pass** (0 issues in 134 files). Not run here: Bicep, PSRule, DotNet, and FabricApp, because nothing under `infra/` or `workload-generator/`, and no app code under `fabric/app`, changed. CI runs them.
- **CI on `ubuntu-latest`** (pull request 34): one test failed there, and only there: the real child process's. The child saw what it should, no `BROWSER` and the opt-out, but the file it was compared from ended with a newline. On Linux and macOS, `Start-Process -RedirectStandardOutput` writes each line the child prints with a line ending, from handlers that run as the output arrives (`OutputDataReceived` and `StreamWriter.WriteLine` in PowerShell 7.4.6's `Process.cs`); on Windows the file gets the child's bytes as written. Found on the way and fixed: the child now writes its own file, which reads the same on every platform. The module didn't change. On Windows the file passed again (44 tests), and the test still fails without the `BROWSER` removal and with the caller's `BROWSER` cleared. This machine has no Linux (no WSL or Docker), so CI's rerun is the Linux check.
- **Live:** none here, by the coordinator's direction, while the owner's live test ran from another worktree (R691, R692).

**Risks.** R691 and R692 (new).

### The Jumpstart image, live: the download's speed and resume, and the first boot's cleanup

2026-11-06, three live findings in the owner's live test of `JumpstartImage` ("I want a one click deployment that works without anything and fast"), on `archost01` (`Standard_E2s_v5` with an E10 Standard SSD OS disk):

- **Speed:** `GuestImage` had downloaded `ArcBox-SQL-STD.vhdx` for about 55 minutes at 0.2 MB/s, with the bootstrap's `powershell.exe` (Windows PowerShell 5.1) at 99% of a core, HTTPS to Jumpstart's storage working, and no PowerShell logging policy set. At that rate the 34.1 GiB would have taken about two days.
- **Resume:** with the speed fixed, the host's log said "resuming at 0.2 GiB", and a minute later the partial file held 0.011 GiB: the server had answered the range request with 200 and the whole image, and the host had started over without a word. No resume of this image had ever worked.
- **First boot:** with the speed fix, the host downloaded and verified the image, and `GuestFirstBoot` converged; then `JumpstartImageCheck` failed all 3 attempts (15:26, 15:30, and 15:35 UTC) with "The Jumpstart guest isn't ready yet: C:\Jumpstart is still there." In the guest, `C:\Jumpstart` was the image's own folder, with its SQL Server media, and nothing held it open: it had never been deleted.

**Causes.**

- **Speed:** the download loop and the resume loop called `Add-DemoJumpstartHashBlock` once per read, with the 4 MiB buffer (`JumpstartImage.BufferMB`) as a mandatory `[byte[]]` parameter. Windows PowerShell 5.1 checks a mandatory array argument for null elements one by one, so every call walked 4,194,304 boxed bytes however few the read returned, and an HTTPS response stream returns at most one TLS record, 16 KiB, per read. Measured on the authoring PC in Windows PowerShell 5.1: 380 to 650 ms per call with the mandatory parameter, with or without `[AllowEmptyCollection()]`, and 0.03 ms without `Mandatory`. PowerShell 6 and later skip the walk for value types ([PowerShell/PowerShell#5432](https://github.com/PowerShell/PowerShell/pull/5432)), so the unit tests, which run in PowerShell 7, didn't show it, and their `MemoryStream` returned the whole test image in one read.
- **Resume:** the GET asked for an open-ended range (`HttpWebRequest.AddRange(offset)`, `Range: bytes=N-`) and sent no `x-ms-version`. Blob storage serves an anonymous request without `x-ms-version` at its earliest service version ([versioning](https://learn.microsoft.com/rest/api/storageservices/versioning-for-the-azure-storage-services)), and only version 2011-08-18 and later take an open-ended range, while every version takes a closed one, `bytes=N-M` ([the Range header](https://learn.microsoft.com/rest/api/storageservices/specifying-the-range-header-for-blob-service-operations)). Probes of the image with curl on 2026-11-06: `bytes=1000000-` without `x-ms-version` got 200 (served at version 2009-09-19), the same with `x-ms-version: 2021-08-06` got 206, and the closed `bytes=1000000-36615798783` without it got 206. `x-ms-version` also changes the ETag's format (quoted), which the sidecar and the pin compare, so it isn't sent.
- **First boot:** `Invoke-DemoJumpstartFirstBoot` passed its cleanup block `[string]$settings.UninstallFeatures`, `[string]$Context.Guest.Sql.InstanceName`, and `[string]$expected['jumpstartFolder']` without parentheses. In argument mode, `[string]$x` is an expandable string, not a cast, so the guest got "[string]System.Collections.Hashtable[jumpstartFolder]" as the folder (and such text as setup's `/FEATURES` and `/INSTANCENAME` for the Integration Services uninstall); its `Test-Path` was false, nothing was deleted, and the log said "extras removed" regardless. The image check's own read of the guest put its casts in parentheses, so it saw the folder.

**Work summary.**

1. **Speed,** in `vm/hyperv-guest/GuestJumpstart.ps1`: both loops call `TransformBlock` on the MD5 and SHA-256 algorithms directly, and `Add-DemoJumpstartHashBlock` is gone. The `.partial` file's `FileStream` has a `BufferMB` buffer, so the network's small reads become 4 MiB writes: a Standard SSD counts each I/O up to 256 KiB as one, and an E10 allows up to 500 a second ([disk types](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/virtual-machines/disks-types#standard-ssd-size)).
2. **Resume:** `New-DemoJumpstartDownloadRequest` builds the GET with a closed range, `bytes=offset-(length-1)`, from the HEAD's length, and no `x-ms-version`; `Open-DemoJumpstartDownload` sends it. A server that still answers a range request with the whole image now gets a **Warning** in the host's log ("The server ignored the range request from byte ... so the download starts over"), where the host used to start over silently.
3. **First boot:** every typed argument of the cleanup's `Invoke-Command` is in parentheses. The cleanup returns the folder it acted on, and right after it runs, before a restart can end the pass, the host logs what it did: the services stopped and disabled, the uninstall's result (nothing to uninstall, or uninstalled or not, with setup's exit code and final result), the folder removed or absent, and an Arc identity reset; the last line says only that the first boot finished and the guest is on the switch. On a host that hit it, a new bootstrap generation's image check runs the first boot again, which deletes the folder and runs the uninstall with setup's real arguments.
4. **Unchanged:** the HEAD request and its ETag, the sidecar, hashing a partial file's bytes before the rest, the length and Content-MD5 checks (fail closed), the pinned SHA-256, the decisions, and the records.
5. **Tests:** `Vm.JumpstartImage` downloads through a stream that returns at most 16 KiB per read, fresh and resumed from 2.5 MiB on disk with a 1 MiB buffer, and checks with the PowerShell parser that the copy loops call no PowerShell command (the old code fails it); it checks the request's `Range` header (closed when it resumes, none from byte 0, no `x-ms-version`), that the GET goes out for the offset and length it was given, and that a 200 to a range request logs the **Warning** and still ends in a full, verified download; and it runs the first boot with `Invoke-Command` mocked, checking the cleanup's five values (the services, the feature, `MSSQLSERVER`, `C:\Jumpstart`, and the timeout) and what it logs in three outcomes, never "extras removed". `Repo.Conventions` fails a mandatory `[byte[]]` parameter anywhere in `vm/` or `automation/`, the Windows PowerShell 5.1 code, and a `[type]$value` command argument anywhere in the repository (a bare-word expandable string that starts with a type name and a variable; a bare type name such as `Should -BeOfType [string]` is fine), which flags the old line's three arguments and nothing else. `docs/troubleshooting.md` has rows for the new **Warning** and for an image check that finds first-boot work unfinished.
6. **The same patterns elsewhere:** no other mandatory `[byte[]]` parameter in the VM-side code; the loader's and the runner's chunk joins pass only paths to functions, and `vm/bootstrap/lib/Download.ps1` copies with `Stream.CopyTo`. The operator-side `ConvertTo-DemoSecretCheckItem` and `ConvertFrom-DemoSecretScanContent` take a mandatory `[byte[]]` once per file, but the `DbHubDemo` module requires PowerShell 7.4, where that costs at most 0.3 ms per call with 4 MiB (PowerShell 7.6.9), so they're unchanged. `Download.ps1` also asks for an open-ended range when it resumes, but only from Microsoft's download servers (`download.microsoft.com` and `software-static.download.prss.microsoft.com`), not Blob storage, so it's unchanged.
7. **Considered and not taken:** `Stream.CopyTo` through two `CryptoStream`s, entirely inside .NET. It was no faster (below), and flushing it finalizes the hashes, which the resume and a complete partial file would then have to track.

**Changed files.** `vm/hyperv-guest/GuestJumpstart.ps1`, `tests/unit/Vm.JumpstartImage.Tests.ps1`, `tests/unit/Repo.Conventions.Tests.ps1`, `docs/troubleshooting.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, and this report.

**Validation.**

- **Static (2026-11-06, on this branch at `06ac399`, Windows, PowerShell 7.6.9, Pester 5.9.1, PSScriptAnalyzer 1.25.0, markdownlint-cli2 0.23.3 from the live-test tools):** `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check PSScriptAnalyzer, Pester, Markdown -CI`: PSScriptAnalyzer **Pass** (no findings in 340 files), Pester **Pass** (3,612 passed, 17 skipped, 0 failed, in 1,788 seconds), and markdownlint **Pass** (no issues in 134 files). The two new convention guards flag the old code and nothing else. Bicep, PSRule, DotNet, and FabricApp weren't run, since nothing under `infra/`, `workload-generator/`, or `fabric/app` changed; CI runs them, and gitleaks, which isn't on this PC (the branch's added lines have no secret-shaped text).
- **Benchmark** (Windows PowerShell 5.1.28000 with .NET Framework 4.8.1 on the authoring PC; MD5 and SHA-256 with CNG, as on the host; the network replaced by a local file read through a stream that returns at most 16 KiB per read; the temporary files deleted afterwards):
  - The real `Save-DemoJumpstartImage`, `main` at `485c01a` against this branch: the download 0.07 MiB/s (2 MiB in 30.5 seconds, about 0.24 seconds of CPU per read, so 256 MiB would take about an hour) against 201 MiB/s (256 MiB in 1.27 seconds); the resume's hashing of a complete 256 MiB partial file 16.3 MiB/s (15.8 seconds, all of it CPU) against 1.1 seconds of CPU (7.6 seconds on this PC, waiting for its disk). MD5 and SHA-256 were verified in every run.
  - The copy loop alone over 1 GiB, twice: this branch's loop 240 and 269 MiB/s, `CopyTo` through `CryptoStream` 237 and 250 MiB/s, and `CopyTo` with no hashing about 2,050 MiB/s. Hashing is now the whole CPU cost, and it's faster than the host's E10 OS disk writes (up to 100 MB/s, and 150 MB/s while it bursts, for up to 30 minutes).
- **The request, against the image itself** (Windows PowerShell 5.1, the real `Get-DemoJumpstartRemoteFact` and `Open-DemoJumpstartDownload`, reading only the image's last 64 KiB): the HEAD's ETag, `0x8DDD21BE1292CD1` and unquoted, matches the pin; the GET sent `Range: bytes=36645568512-36645634047` and got 206 with `Content-Range: bytes 36645568512-36645634047/36645634048` and 65,536 bytes, which the resume's check accepts.
- **Live:** not run here. The owner's live test ran the speed fix alone (commit `6a8e04a`): the host downloaded and verified the image, and `GuestFirstBoot` converged, until the cleanup's arguments stopped `JumpstartImageCheck`. The first boot's cleanup with its real values and a resume aren't observed live yet (R685, R693, R694).

**Risks.** R693 and R694 (new); R685 stays open for the first boot's cleanup.

### The Insights app's sign-in: the start's read of the sign-in

2026-11-06. The owner's rerun at 15:05Z, after [#34](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/pull/34) merged, still asked for a sign-in nobody needed. It was `deploy.ps1 -FromStage Artifacts`, with the app's stage in `out/fabric-app` and the Rayfin CLI already signed in to the demo's tenant. The status page said "A browser opens for your sign-in", and the CLI's sign-in window started. That sign-in finished (**Pass**), so nothing broke, but the person was asked to sign in again for no reason. Run by hand right after, the stage's own `rayfin login status --json` answered `ok` for the demo's tenant in 44.0 seconds, and later in 9.2 and 9.3 seconds. So the first, cold read, or the token refresh it does, ran past the start check's 30 seconds. The check logged why only at Debug, so the console and the log said nothing. `rayfin login status --help` lists no option but `-h`. The owner's standing goal: "I want a one click deployment that works without anything and fast."

**Work summary.**

1. **The read starts with the run, alongside the guard** (`Start-DemoFabricAppSignInCheck`, in `Invoke-DemoParallelDeployment` and in `Invoke-DemoDeployment`'s sequential path). It starts `rayfin login status --json` without waiting, before the subscription guard, when the run is interactive, has a tenant, isn't a what-if, and its stages can reach the Fabric track (from `-FromStage` and `-StopAfter`). `Start-DemoFabricAppSignIn -Check` takes its answer where the sign-in always decided: after the stopped VMs' start, before preflight. The guard alone took 22 seconds in a timed run, so a warm read, about 9 seconds, is done by then. When the plan turns out not to include the Fabric track, or the guard fails, the run ends the read at once (`Stop-DemoRayfinSignInStatus`). The run's end ends a read nobody used (`Stop-DemoFabricAppSignIn`, from `Complete-DemoDeploymentRun`).
2. **It has 90 seconds from its start** (`$script:DemoRayfinStatusTimeoutSeconds`). While the run waits for it, the console and the status page's **Sign-in for the Insights app** row say "Checking whether the Rayfin CLI is already signed in to the demo's tenant, at most until HH:MM; a browser opens only if it isn't."
3. **The log says why** (`Receive-DemoRayfinSignInStatus` returns a state with a detail):
   - signed in: an Info line with the seconds the read took, and no browser;
   - not signed in, or signed in to another tenant: an Info line, then the sign-in;
   - failed or out of time: a **Warning** with the reason, such as "no answer within 90 seconds", "exit code 1: ...", or "it couldn't start: npx was not found on PATH ...", and the command to read it yourself; then the sign-in.
4. **No stage, still no needless browser:** before the app's stage exists, the same pinned version answers through `npx --yes --prefer-offline` (`Start-DemoRayfinSignInStatus`), as the end-of-window check from #34 did. That check now uses the same read (`Get-DemoRayfinSignInState`), and its **Warning** gives the reason too.
5. **A process start that doesn't wait** (`FabricAppHelpers.ps1`):
   - `Start-DemoFabricAppProcess` starts the process as before: standard input closed, the environment set for the child only, and npm and npx through cmd.exe on Windows.
   - `Receive-DemoFabricAppProcess` waits at most the time limit from the start, ends the process tree after it, and adds `Seconds` and `TimedOut` to the result.
   - `Stop-DemoFabricAppProcess` ends a process that's no longer needed.
   - `Invoke-DemoFabricAppProcess` is the start and the wait, as before.
6. **Docs:** deployment, troubleshooting (a row for a browser that opens though the CLI is signed in), the automation support matrix (row 173), ADR 0023's amendment, the CHANGELOG, and this report.

**Changed files.**

- `scripts/modules/DbHubDemo/Private/FabricAppSignInHelpers.ps1`, `FabricAppHelpers.ps1`, and `ParallelDeployment.ps1`; `scripts/modules/DbHubDemo/Public/Invoke-DemoDeployment.ps1`
- `tests/unit/DbHubDemo.FabricAppSignIn.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.FabricApp.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`
- `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/deployment.md`, `docs/troubleshooting.md`, `docs/automation-support-matrix.md`, `docs/adr/0023-first-run-automation.md`, and this report

**Validation.**

- **The pieces, with real processes** (Windows, PowerShell 7.6.9, Node.js 24.22.2):
  - a child started without waiting returned in 0.4 seconds and was read later, with its exit code, output, and environment;
  - one past its time limit was ended after 2.1 seconds;
  - a stopped one was gone;
  - a real read through `npx`, with `RAYFIN_CONFIG_DIR` pointed at an empty folder (no token cache, nothing sent to Microsoft Entra ID or Fabric), answered "not signed in" in 42 seconds. That cold read through npx also ran past the old 30 seconds.
- **End to end, with a real read** (the same machine, while another session's live test was using it). Only the browser sign-in itself was replaced, so nothing opened:
  - the early read through `npx` started in 0.6 seconds and answered "not signed in" after 61 seconds, within the 90. Meanwhile the run said "Checking whether the Rayfin CLI is already signed in ...". Then it logged "The Rayfin CLI is not signed in (rayfin login status through npx, 61 seconds), so its sign-in for the Insights app starts: a browser opens." and started the sign-in;
  - with the limit set to 5 seconds, the real read ran out of time, and the **Warning** gave the reason, "no answer within 5 seconds", and the command to read it yourself;
  - a read nobody used ended with the run (`Stop-DemoFabricAppSignIn`), and its process was gone.
- **Pester 5.9.1:** all **Pass**:
  - `DbHubDemo.FabricAppSignIn.Tests.ps1`: 61 tests;
  - `DbHubDemo.FabricApp.Tests.ps1`: 38 tests, 5 of them with real child processes (this PowerShell stands in for node);
  - `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1` and `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`: 320 tests.
- **Mutations:** each of ten failed at least one new test: the early read ignored, the old 30 seconds, the Warning at Debug, no "Checking" line, the run's end leaving the read, no npx without a stage, a time limit that doesn't end the child, the parallel path not passing the read, a failed guard leaving it, and the sequential path not starting it.
- **Static (2026-11-06, Windows, PowerShell 7.6.9, Pester 5.9.1, PSScriptAnalyzer 1.25.0, markdownlint-cli2 0.23.3 on Node.js 24.22.2):** `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check PSScriptAnalyzer, Pester, Markdown -CI`:
  - PSScriptAnalyzer: **Pass** (no findings in 340 files).
  - Pester: **Pass** (3,626 passed, 17 skipped, 0 failed, in 1,972 seconds, on a machine another session's live test shared).
  - markdownlint: one issue, a `<time>` placeholder in this entry that read as HTML. Written as HH:MM, it passed: **Pass** (0 issues in 134 files).
  - Not run here: Bicep, PSRule, DotNet, and FabricApp, because nothing under `infra/` or `workload-generator/`, and no app code under `fabric/app`, changed. CI runs them.
- **Live:** none here, by the coordinator's direction (R695 to R697).

**Risks.** R695 to R697 (new).

### The Insights app's sign-in: one answer for the sign-in and the app

2026-11-06. The owner's live run at 15:47Z, on `main` with #35 but without this branch's start read, still opened the sign-in's window: the app's stage was there, and the 30-second start check ran out of time again. Nobody answered the window, and the results contradicted each other:

- **Fabric.AppSignIn**, 11:02 ET, **Warning**: "Nobody finished the Rayfin CLI's sign-in within 15 minutes, so it was stopped. The deployment went on; without the sign-in the Insights app isn't created, and its step says why." That's the "not signed in" answer of the read in `Complete-DemoFabricAppSignIn` (`Get-DemoRayfinSignInState`).
- **Fabric.App**, 11:08 ET, **Manual action required**: "It's deployed: A person must confirm the app in the browser ...". So the CLI was signed in, and the app's step used that sign-in.
- At 16:37Z the stage's `rayfin login status --json` answered `ok` for the demo's tenant in 64.8 seconds, cold (44.0 cold and about 9 warm earlier that day), more than that read's 60 seconds.

**The cause, from the code.** Read in npm's cache: `@microsoft/rayfin-cli` 1.36.2 and its `@azure/msal-node-extensions` 1.5.32. No `rayfin login` was run or stopped against the person's token cache, which the clean live deployment that followed used.

- `rayfin login` doesn't clear or replace the token cache (`~/.rayfin/cache.bin`, encrypted with DPAPI on Windows) or `auth.json` when it starts. It sets up the cache, removes a stale lock file, and waits for the browser (`prompt=select_account`); it writes the cache and `auth.json` only after a token arrives. So stopping it while it waits changes nothing.
- `rayfin login status` says "not signed in" exactly when the cache has no account. It already asks twice, because "the token cache may have been empty on the first query", in its own words.
- The cache's file persistence rewrites the file in place (truncate, then write) when a token is renewed, and it reads an empty file as an empty cache ("Encrypted contents loaded from file were null or empty"), without an error. A cross-process lock file should keep reads and writes apart, but it gets its process ID only after it's created, and each CLI process, as it starts, removes a lock file whose process ID it can't read. So two processes that start together can both reach the cache.
- At the deadline, `deploy.ps1` stopped the window and read the status. Meanwhile the Fabric track's app step, which `Wait-DemoFabricAppSignIn` released at that same deadline, ran its own read (`Test-DemoFabricAppPrerequisite`), which renews the token. Those overlapping reads are the likely cause: nothing else explains "not signed in" from a cache that the stopped window didn't touch and that the app's step found signed in. Without the CLI's own logs, it can't be proven (R698).

**Work summary.**

1. **One read at a time** (`Wait-DemoFabricAppSignIn`): past the deadline, while the **Sign-in for the Insights app** row is still **Running**, the app's step waits for the run to settle it, at most `-GraceSeconds` (240) more. Its row says "The Rayfin CLI's sign-in reached its deadline; the run is checking whether the CLI is signed in, and the Insights app's step goes on once it has, at most at HH:MM." The run's read takes at most about 200 seconds there, so the step reads after it.
2. **The read after the window** (`Get-DemoRayfinSignInState`) has the start's 90 seconds (`$script:DemoRayfinStatusTimeoutSeconds`) instead of 60. With `-Attempts 2`, which `Complete-DemoFabricAppSignIn` passes, an answer of "not signed in", or one that couldn't be read, is read again after 10 seconds, with Info lines: "The Rayfin CLI's sign-in says it isn't signed in (rayfin login status through ..., N seconds); it's read again in 10 seconds, because a read while another Rayfin process rewrites its token cache can find no sign-in for a moment." and "The Rayfin CLI's sign-in, read again: ...". It doesn't read again after a time-out (another 90 seconds would only repeat it), a start failure, an answer for another tenant, or Ctrl+C.
3. **The app's records settle the row** (`Update-DemoFabricAppSignInFromApp`). When the app step's records include `FABRIC.APP.SIGNIN` **Pass** or `FABRIC.APP.DEPLOY` **Pass**, a `DEPLOY.FABRIC.APPSIGNIN` **Warning** becomes **Pass**: "The Rayfin CLI was signed in to the demo's tenant: the Insights app's step found it signed in and used that sign-in, so the sign-in this run started wasn't needed."
   - The new record replaces the **Warning** in the run's results, the row becomes **Done**, the evidence keeps `earlierStatus` and `earlierMessage`, and an Info line says so.
   - It runs in the parallel path's sign-in check, every 10 seconds while the tracks run and at their end, once the Fabric track's records are in; and in the sequential path right after the app's step.
   - It changes nothing else: a **Pass**, **Not supported**, or **Not selected** stays, and so does a **Warning** the records don't contradict. It never throws.
4. **Docs:** deployment, troubleshooting (a row for the two rows that disagreed), the automation support matrix (row 173), ADR 0023's amendment, the CHANGELOG, and this report (R692 updated; R698 to R701).
5. **`main` merged in** at `881d98b` (#35), with both sides' entries in this report and the CHANGELOG kept. The start read's risks, R693 to R695 on this branch, are now R695 to R697, after #35's R693 and R694.

**Changed files.**

- `scripts/modules/DbHubDemo/Private/FabricAppSignInHelpers.ps1` and `ParallelDeployment.ps1`; `scripts/modules/DbHubDemo/Public/Invoke-DemoDeployment.ps1`
- `tests/unit/DbHubDemo.FabricAppSignIn.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`
- `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/deployment.md`, `docs/troubleshooting.md`, `docs/automation-support-matrix.md`, `docs/adr/0023-first-run-automation.md`, and this report

**Validation.**

- **Pester 5.9.1:** all **Pass**:
  - `DbHubDemo.FabricAppSignIn.Tests.ps1`: 84 tests, 23 of them new;
  - `DbHubDemo.FabricApp.Tests.ps1`: 38 tests;
  - `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1` and `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`: 322 tests, 2 of them new.
- **Mutations:** each of thirteen failed at least one new test: the read back to 60 seconds; `Complete-DemoFabricAppSignIn` reading once; a second read after a time-out, after another tenant, or after Ctrl+C; a 1-second pause; any app **Pass** settling it; the run's results not replaced; a non-Warning settled too; no grace; the row not settled; and the parallel or the sequential path not settling it.
- **After the merge of `main`:** `Repo.Conventions.Tests.ps1`, with #35's new guards such as no `[type]$value` command argument, and `Vm.JumpstartImage.Tests.ps1`: 164 tests, **Pass**; markdownlint: **Pass** (134 files).
- **Static (2026-11-06, on this branch with `main` merged, Windows, PowerShell 7.6.9, Pester 5.9.1, PSScriptAnalyzer 1.25.0, markdownlint-cli2 0.23.3 on Node.js 24.22.2):** `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check PSScriptAnalyzer, Pester, Markdown -CI`:

### The live test of 2026-11-06: a clean first deployment

2026-11-06. The owner's live test: a clean first deployment from a fresh clone of `main` at `881d98b` (with #35, before #36), in West US 3, with the defaults: T2 in `JumpstartImage` mode, the `lowest-cost` profile, the Insights app, and the nightly safety stop. Docs and their user-facing text only: this entry records the results, and the docs, the start banner, the status page, and `init.ps1`'s next steps replace their estimates with them.

**Results.**

- **DONE in 91 minutes,** from 11:57 to 13:27 ET, with nobody needed during the run. The steps: preflight about 4.5 minutes, the template 12, T1's bootstraps about 6, T2's host build about 27 (the image's download and verification, its first boot, the first boot's cleanup, and `JumpstartImageCheck`), then T2's Arc onboarding with the SQL Server extension, PAYG, and monitoring about 33, and the validation about 4.
- **No browser opened** for the Insights app: the Rayfin CLI was already signed in.
- **The image's download,** in an earlier run that day, with [the speed fix](#the-jumpstart-image-live-the-downloads-speed-and-resume-and-the-first-boots-cleanup): the 34.1 GiB downloaded and verified in about 11 minutes, about 51 MiB/s, bound by the disk of `archost01` (`Standard_E2s_v5` with an E10 Standard SSD OS disk).

### The sign-in sweep: the LSA secret Azure's provisioning leaves

2026-11-06. Every live deploy that day reported `T2.ArcOnboarding` as a **Warning**: "Windows automatic sign-in was left armed on archost01, and the cleanup turned it off and deleted its LSA secret and Winlogon values". Read-only Run Command checks as SYSTEM, of presence and the secret's length only, found the same state on a freshly built `archost01` before `T2.SignInCleanup` and on `sqlvm01` after **DONE**: the LSA secret `DefaultPassword` present (24 bytes), and none of `AutoAdminLogon`, `AutoLogonCount`, `DefaultUserName`, or the Winlogon `DefaultPassword` value. `unattend.xml` had no `AutoLogon`, and the demo's logs never mention any of them. So Azure's Windows provisioning, or the image, leaves the secret with automatic sign-in off. The user's decisions: a lone secret isn't automatic sign-in left armed, so the cleanup still deletes it and reports a **Pass** with a short note; armed stays a **Warning** after the cleanup, or a **Fail** before it; and `sqlvm01` deletes it too.

**Work summary.**

1. **The sweep** (`vm/bootstrap/Invoke-DemoSignInResidue.ps1`, Windows PowerShell 5.1) counts the LSA secret alone apart (`autoLogonLoneSecrets`): `AutoAdminLogon` isn't 1, and there's no `AutoLogonCount`, no Winlogon `DefaultPassword` value, and no `DefaultUserName` where the sweep checks it. It still fails a scan, because it's a stored password nothing needs. `Clean` deletes only the secret (a new `DeleteSecret` mode) and reports `loneSecretDeleted`. The Run Command's compact summary carries `loneSecret` in its counts and its actions. `-AutoLogonOnly` checks automatic sign-in and nothing else.
2. **The result** (`Get-DemoSignInDataResult`) keeps its status and adds a note, in its message and its evidence (`note`), when the cleanup deleted the lone secret: "The cleanup deleted the LSA secret DefaultPassword that Azure's provisioning leaves on archost01; automatic sign-in was off." In the nested guest, which Azure doesn't provision, it says "a leftover LSA secret" instead. A scan's **Fail** names the secret accurately, not as automatic sign-in left armed. `T2.ARC.ONBOARDING`'s **Pass** carries the note.
3. **T1:** `sqlvm01`'s bootstrap dot-sources the sweep and has a new last step, `AutoLogonSecret` (`Test-DemoSqlVmAutoLogonSecret`, `Clear-DemoSqlVmAutoLogonSecret`). It deletes the lone secret with `-AutoLogonOnly`, fails closed when anything is left, and records `sign-in-secret.json`. `health.json` reports it (`details.signIn`), the health summary reads it (`signInFound`, `signInNote`), and `DEPLOY.T1.READY` adds the note to its **Pass**; it's a **Warning** when automatic sign-in was armed. It's last, so it never holds up the database.
4. **The bootstrap log** (`Format-DemoSignInSweep`) names the lone secret and its deletion.
5. **The sweep's other users:** the guided stop arms and clears automatic sign-in itself (`Invoke-DemoGuestAutoLogonAction`) and is unchanged. The evaluation-only VM's `SignInCleanup`, the host's `GuestSignInCleanup`, and the golden capture already deleted the lone secret through `Clean`; now their log and result say what it was. The golden test's read-only scan would still fail on it, as a scan should; its copy's OS disk comes from a captured host whose gate deleted it.

**Changed files.** `vm/bootstrap/Invoke-DemoSignInResidue.ps1`, `Invoke-SqlVmBootstrap.ps1`, and `README.md`; `vm/hyperv-guest/GuestSql.ps1`; `vm/health/Get-DemoHealth.ps1`; the module's `Private/SignInDataHelpers.ps1`, `Private/TrackHelpers.ps1` (only `DEPLOY.T1.READY`), and `Public/Invoke-DemoArcOnboarding.ps1`; the tests `Vm.SignInResidue`, `DbHubDemo.SignInData`, `DbHubDemo.Arc`, `Vm.SqlVmPlan`, and `DbHubDemo.Tracks`; and the CHANGELOG, this report, troubleshooting, the security model, and ADR 0009.

**Validation.**

- **Static (2026-11-06, Windows, PowerShell 7.6.9, Pester 5.9.1, PSScriptAnalyzer 1.25.0, markdownlint-cli2 0.23.3 on Node.js 26.13.0):**
  - PSScriptAnalyzer (`./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check PSScriptAnalyzer -CI`): **Pass** (no findings in 340 files).
  - Pester, the affected files: `Vm.SignInResidue`, `DbHubDemo.SignInData`, `DbHubDemo.Arc`, `Vm.SqlVmPlan`, `Vm.HostPlan`, `Vm.EvalArcVm`, `Vm.WorkloadSteps`, `Vm.GuidedSetup`, `Vm.Loader`, `DbHubDemo.Golden`, `DbHubDemo.Tracks`, `DbHubDemo.Bundle`, and `DbHubDemo.T4`: 631 tests, 12 of them new, **Pass**. The repository's and the docs' conventions (`Repo.*.Tests.ps1`, `Docs.Walkthrough.Tests.ps1`): 252 passed and 3 skipped, **Pass**.
  - markdownlint (`-Check Markdown -CI`): **Pass** (0 issues in 134 files).
  - Not run here: the full Pester suite (about 40 minutes; CI runs it), and Bicep, PSRule, DotNet, and FabricApp, because nothing under `infra/`, `workload-generator/`, or `fabric/app` changed.
- **Live:** none, by the coordinator's direction: the live environment is in use (R708).

**Risks.** R706 to R708 (new; R702 to R704 on its branch, renumbered after #37's R702 to R705).

### The history report's snapshot at the end of deploy.ps1

2026-11-06. The owner asked: "Can we add a step that calls the acitivty notebook one time after deployment so it starts to populate data? WOuld there be any concern for executing that?", and chose to add it in its own pull request. The history report was blank after a deployment: `configure-fabric.ps1`'s step 5 runs `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity` once when step 2 first imports it, so a new lakehouse gets the history tables, but `deploy.ps1`'s Fabric track runs in parallel with T1 and T2, before any target sends telemetry, so that run saves almost nothing. The live run that day showed it: the report's check was a **Warning**, "'Analysis Findings'[Blocking Incidents] returned blank over the whole history...", and `FABRIC.SEMANTICMODEL.MEASURES` said "The history report is empty until NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity saves its first snapshots". The design was agreed with the owner before the work; the concerns it answers are below.

**Work summary.**

1. **The step** (`Invoke-DemoHistorySnapshotStep`, new in `HistorySnapshotHelpers.ps1`), last in every run that reaches its validation. In the parallel path, it comes after the validation, **DONE**'s checks (`Invoke-DemoDoneGate`), `DEPLOY.TIMING`, and the switch to the run's activity profile (`Send-DemoPendingActivitySwitch`), and before `DEPLOY.SUMMARY`. `-Sequential` calls it at the same point, after its **DONE** checks and its switch. The switch goes first because the VMs' incidents wait for it and the notebook doesn't touch the VMs; **DONE**'s checks go first because the step reads their telemetry results, and so the clock never includes it.
2. **What runs, as whom.** As whoever ran `deploy.ps1` (the Azure CLI's Fabric token, after the subscription guard): the workspace and lakehouse by name, read-only (`Resolve-DemoFabricConfig` without `-Create`), the capacity's state (`Test-DemoFabricWorkspace`, `FABRIC.P5.CAPACITY`), and the notebook by name. Then `Invoke-DemoFabricNotebookRun` runs it with `Get-DemoHistorySnapshotParameter`: `CONFIRM` false (with its defaults, **Run all** also sends a 240-minute activity request through GitHub, P9, which would duplicate the deployment's), `STOP_ACTIVITY` false, `SAVE_RESULTS` true, `WINDOW_MINUTES` 1440, `KEY_VAULT_URL` empty, and the demo's `RESOURCE_GROUP`, which scopes the notebook's queries (as `stop-demo.ps1 -SaveHistory` sends it). Then one refresh of the history model (`Find-DemoSemanticModel`, `Invoke-DemoSemanticModelRefresh`), which frames its Direct Lake tables, so the report shows the rows at once.
3. **Bounded:** at most 10 minutes for the job, polled every 15 seconds, and 5 for the refresh, with no table retry. A job still running then is a **Warning** (`StillRunning`), and it goes on in Fabric; a deduplicated one is a **Warning** too (`Deduped`). Spark's start and the analysis took about 2 to 5 minutes when they were run by hand (an estimate, not measured in `deploy.ps1`).
4. **The capacity:** a state other than **Active**, or `CapacityNotActive` from Fabric when the step lists the notebooks or submits the job, is a **Warning** (`CapacityPaused`) that names the capacity and its state, with the resume steps. Nothing resumes, pauses, or scales it.
5. **Never blocking.** The one record, `DEPLOY.FABRIC.HISTORY`, is **Pass**, a **Warning**, or **Not selected**, never a **Fail** or **Manual action required**. So **DONE**'s gate, `Get-DemoDeploymentOutcome`, `DEPLOY.SUMMARY`'s status, and the exit codes never count it, and `Get-DemoDeploymentOutcome` is unchanged. Its evidence carries `nonBlocking` (`historySnapshot`), which `Test-DemoNonBlockingResult` recognizes as a second guard. An error (a notebook that failed, a refresh that failed, an exception) is a **Warning** with evidence `reportedStatus` `Fail`. Only Ctrl+C goes through.
6. **Shown like the Insights app.** The progress model's catalog has its own row, `Fabric.History`, **History report's snapshot (NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity, once, analyze-only)**, under **Validation**, marked non-blocking, and absent with `-SkipFabric`. It's **Done**; **Action needed** for a **Warning**; **Failed** for an error; or **Skipped**, with the record's message and fix. One that didn't work is on the console at once, then in the **Needs attention** box, **DONE** and **NOT DONE**, and `DEPLOY.SUMMARY`'s evidence (`Get-DemoHistorySnapshotItem` in `Get-DemoNonBlockingItem`).
7. **Not selected** (`Get-DemoHistorySnapshotSkip`, in this order): `-WhatIf` (a record at the template's what-if), `-SkipFabric`, GitHub Actions, **DONE**'s checks that didn't run, and **DONE**'s telemetry checks without a **Pass**. Then, in Fabric, a workspace, lakehouse, or notebook that the Fabric track didn't create or import. GitHub Actions: [Run On Demand Item Job](https://learn.microsoft.com/rest/api/fabric/core/job-scheduler/run-on-demand-item-job) supports service principals, but the demo has no supported Fabric setup for the workflow's identity (`deploy.yml` skips Fabric by default, `setup-github-oidc.ps1` grants no workspace role, and the notebook's `kusto` token is proven for a user only, R29), so its remediation is the command to run from a terminal.
8. **A resumed run with nothing left to do runs it again** (`Set-DemoProgressPlan` keeps the row **Pending** whenever the validation runs). That's harmless: each run saves its own rows, the notebook replaces a run's rows only when that run saves again, and another snapshot only adds what happened since, such as incidents.
9. **Partial data, said plainly.** The VMs switch to the incident profile only after **DONE**'s checks, so the **Pass** message says that incident measures such as **Blocking Incidents** stay blank until incidents have run and the notebook runs again (`./scripts/stop-demo.ps1 -SaveHistory`, `./scripts/configure-fabric.ps1 -SkipHubChecks -SkipNotebookImport -RunLiveActivity`, or the next `./scripts/deploy.ps1`). With `baseline`, no incident runs until one is started. It also says that the run's `FABRIC.SEMANTICMODEL.*` checks ran before the snapshot.
10. Docs: the README, the architecture, deployment, troubleshooting, cost management, the automation support matrix (row 181), the Fabric and history report guides, ADR 0012's status page section, ADR 0014's amendment, ADR 0018's amendment, the CHANGELOG, and this report.
11. **`configure-fabric.ps1`'s step 5 sends `RESOURCE_GROUP`** (R710, the coordinator's live finding on 2026-11-06). Its `-RunLiveActivity` run, and its first run after an import, sent none, so the notebook's queries kept its default, `rg-dbhub-demo`. A clean deploy in `rg-dbhublt-demo` then saved no telemetry snapshots. Step 5 now sends `rg-<prefix>-<environment>`, as `stop-demo.ps1 -SaveHistory` and the snapshot do; no other script runs the notebook. `Invoke-DemoFabricNotebookRun`'s help example is analyze-only and scoped too.

**Changed files.**

- `scripts/modules/DbHubDemo/Private/HistorySnapshotHelpers.ps1` (new), `ParallelDeployment.ps1`, `DeployExperience.ps1` (`Get-DemoNonBlockingItem` and `Test-DemoNonBlockingResult` only), and `ProgressHelpers.ps1`
- `scripts/modules/DbHubDemo/Public/Invoke-DemoDeployment.ps1`, and `scripts/deploy.ps1` (its help only)
- `scripts/modules/DbHubDemo/Public/Invoke-DemoFabricNotebookRun.ps1` (its help example only), and `scripts/configure-fabric.ps1` (step 5 sends `RESOURCE_GROUP`)
- `tests/unit/DbHubDemo.HistorySnapshot.Tests.ps1` (new), `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Progress.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.Fabric.Tests.ps1`
- `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/deployment.md`, `docs/troubleshooting.md`, `docs/cost-management.md`, `docs/automation-support-matrix.md`, `docs/adr/0012-validation-model.md`, `docs/adr/0014-notebook-role-and-capacity.md`, `docs/adr/0018-fast-redeploy-parallel-tracks-golden-snapshot.md`, `fabric/README.md`, `fabric/powerbi/README.md`, and this report

**Validation.**

- **The new tests** (`DbHubDemo.HistorySnapshot`, 24 tests, and new cases in `DbHubDemo.Tracks`, `.Lifecycle`, `.Progress`, and `.Fabric`) cover:
  - the parameters sent, checked against the notebook's parameters cell, and the **Pass** path with its partial-data text;
  - a paused capacity before the run and at its submit, with no resume, pause, or scale call;
  - a job that outlives its wait, a deduplicated one, a failed notebook, a failed refresh, an exception, and Ctrl+C;
  - every **Not selected** case, and **DONE** with exit code 0 after a **Warning** or an error;
  - the status page's row and its **Needs attention** box, and the order in both paths;
  - step 5's `RESOURCE_GROUP` for a configuration whose prefix isn't the default.
- **Mutations** checked that the new tests fail when the code regresses:
  - without the record in `Test-DemoNonBlockingResult`, with `CONFIRM` true, or without the capacity check;
  - with the snapshot before the switch, without `Fabric.History` in `Set-DemoProgressPlan`'s map, or without the telemetry gate;
  - without step 5's `RESOURCE_GROUP`.

  Each made tests fail, and the files were restored.
- **Static, full** (2026-11-06, on `00cf7b9` after the merge of `main`; Windows, PowerShell 7.6.9, Pester 5.9.1, PSScriptAnalyzer 1.25.0, markdownlint-cli2 0.23.3 on Node.js 26.13.0): `./tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1 -Check PSScriptAnalyzer, Pester, Markdown -CI` passed:
  - PSScriptAnalyzer **Pass**: no findings in 342 files.
  - Pester **Pass**: 3,644 passed, 17 skipped, 0 failed, in 754 seconds.
  - markdownlint **Pass**: no issues in 134 files.

  The first full run had found 54 failures, all in the track and lifecycle tests' mock of the snapshot. Under `Set-StrictMode -Version Latest`, which the script sets, a mock can't read a switch that the call didn't bind; the mocks now read `$PesterBoundParameters`, as the repository's other mocks do (`00cf7b9`). Not run here: Bicep, PSRule, DotNet, and FabricApp, because nothing under `infra/`, `workload-generator/`, or `fabric/app` changed; nor gitleaks, which isn't on this PC. CI runs them.
- **Step 5's fix, after the full run:** `DbHubDemo.Fabric`, `.FabricItem`, `.DashboardData`, `.HubReadiness`, and `.FabricTrack` passed in strict mode (117 passed, 0 failed). PSScriptAnalyzer with the repository's settings found nothing in its files. After the docs' last edit, `Repo.Conventions`, `Repo.FunctionNames`, `Docs.Walkthrough`, and `Repo.NeutralWording` passed in strict mode, and markdownlint found no issues.
- **After the merge of `main` (#36):** in strict mode, the suites that both changes touch passed: `DbHubDemo.Tracks`, `.Lifecycle`, `.HistorySnapshot`, `.Progress`, `.FabricApp`, `.FabricAppSignIn`, `.OneCommand`, `.Fabric`, and `.FabricTrack`. So did `Repo.Conventions`, `Repo.FunctionNames`, `Docs.Walkthrough`, `Repo.NeutralWording`, and `Repo.LifecycleScripts`: 909 passed, 0 failed.
- **Live, by the coordinator** (2026-11-06, a clean deploy in `rg-dbhublt-demo` that reached **DONE** at 13:27 ET; nothing ran from here): `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity` analyze-only, with `SAVE_RESULTS` true over 1,440 minutes.
  - At 13:33 ET, through `-RunLiveActivity` without `RESOURCE_GROUP` (R710): the job took 66 seconds, then `telemetry_snapshots` held 0, `incident_windows` 0, and `analysis_findings` 12, and 43 of the model's 103 measures had a value.
  - At 14:04 ET, through `Save-DemoFabricHistory` with `RESOURCE_GROUP` `rg-dbhublt-demo`, the parameters this step sends, then framing: 90 seconds, `telemetry_snapshots` 1,249, `incident_windows` 12 (the first incidents had started at 13:35 ET), `analysis_findings` 32, and 84 measures with a value, 19 blank, with no error.
  - So the resource group kept the report empty, not the timing. The step itself hasn't run live yet (R709).

**Risks.** R709 to R711 (new; R702 to R704 on its branch, renumbered after #37's R702 to R705 and the sign-in sweep's R706 to R708); R710 is closed (item 11).
